\displaystyle\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}(|r^{p}r^{-1}L(r{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)|+|L{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|+|\underline{L}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|+|r^{-1}\not{\nabla}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|+|r^{-1}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|)|{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(N^{\alpha\beta}(\psi,x)\partial_{\alpha}\psi\,\partial_{\beta}\psi)|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+|\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}(N^{\alpha\beta}(\psi,x)\partial_{\alpha}\psi\,\partial_{\beta}\psi)|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9},v}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9},v}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}+\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9},v}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9},v}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9},v}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})},

(4.4)

while, corresponding to
p=0
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1},v)\cap\{r\geq R\}}

\displaystyle\sum_{|{\bf k}|\leq k}(|r^{-1}L(r{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi)|+|L{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|+|\underline{L}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|+|r^{-1}\not{\nabla}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|+|r^{-1}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\psi|)|{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(N^{\alpha\beta}(\psi,x)\partial_{\alpha}\psi\,\partial_{\beta}\psi)|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+|\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}(N^{\alpha\beta}(\psi,x)\partial_{\alpha}\psi\,\partial_{\beta}\psi)|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0+)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9},v}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0+)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9},v}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}.

(4.5)

We have formulated our null structural assumption directly in terms
of the necessary estimates for maximum generality. We note that, as
shown in Appendix C.2 of
[
DHRT22
]
, a sufficient condition
for the above in the general subextremal Kerr case is that
N(\xi,x)
satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{|\xi|\leq 1,r\geq R}\sum_{|\textbf{k}|+s\leq k}\sum_{A,B=1,2}

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{D}^{\mathbf{k}}\partial_{\xi}^{s}(rN^{uu})|+|\mathfrak{D}^{\mathbf{k}}\partial_{\xi}^{s}N^{uv}|+|\mathfrak{D}^{\mathbf{k}}\partial_{\xi}^{s}N^{vv}|

\displaystyle+|\mathfrak{D}^{\mathbf{k}}\partial_{\xi}^{s}(rN^{Au})|+|\mathfrak{D}^{\mathbf{k}}\partial_{\xi}^{s}(rN^{Av})|+|\mathfrak{D}^{\mathbf{k}}\partial_{\xi}^{s}(r^{2}N^{AB})|\lesssim D_{k},

(4.6)

where the
A
,
B
indices refer to coordinates
\theta,\phi
, and
D_{k}
are
arbitrary constants. This is thus a strict generalisation of the classical null
condition of
[
Kla86
]
and the class of semilinearities on Kerr considered
in
[
Luk13
]
.

Remark 4.1.2
.

The work
[
DHRT22
]
always considered real-valued
\psi
.
Since for convenience,
the formalism of the present paper allows complex valued functions to accommodate
Fourier projections, one may without additional complication consider at the outset also
complex-valued solutions
of (
4.1
),
i.e. replace
\mathbb{R}
by
\mathbb{C}
in (
4.2
).
In that case, it is natural to allow a more general expression

N_{1}^{\mu\nu}(\psi,x)\partial_{\mu}\psi\,\partial_{\nu}\psi+N_{2}^{\mu\nu}(\psi,x)\overline{\partial_{\mu}\psi}\,\partial_{\nu}\psi+N_{3}^{\mu\nu}(\psi,x)\partial_{\mu}\psi\,\overline{\partial_{\nu}\psi}+N_{4}^{\mu\nu}(\psi,x)\overline{\partial_{\mu}\psi}\,\overline{\partial_{\nu}\psi}

on the right hand side of (
4.1
), where

N_{i}:\mathbb{C}\times\mathcal{M}\to T\mathcal{M}\otimes T\mathcal{M}\otimes\mathbb{C}
.
We have that the results of this paper will apply as long as each of the expressions

N_{1}^{\mu\nu}(\psi,x)\partial_{\mu}\psi\,\partial_{\nu}\psi,\,\,\,N_{2}^{\mu\nu}(\psi,x)\overline{\partial_{\mu}\psi}\,\partial_{\nu}\psi,\ldots

individually
satisfy (
4.4
) and (
4.5
).
Note that a sufficient condition for this would be that
each of the
N_{i}
satisfy (
4.6
).

4.2
Local well posedness and Cauchy-type stability

We will review here “local” well posedness and Cauchy-type stability results.
(We emphasise that as these statements are local with respect to the parameter
\tau
and the hypersurfaces

\Sigma(\tau)
asymptote to null infinity
\mathcal{I}^{+}
, they are actually what
are sometimes referred to as
semi-global
statements, and their proofs use already the
generalised null
structure Assumption
4.1.1
.)

We define a smooth initial data set on
\Sigma(\tau_{0})

to be a pair
(\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime})
where
\uppsi
is a function on

\Sigma(\tau_{0})
smooth on

\Sigma(\tau_{0})\cap\{r\leq R\}
and smooth on
\Sigma(\tau_{0})\cap\{r\geq R\}
, and

\uppsi^{\prime}
is a smooth function on
\Sigma(\tau_{0})\cap\{r\leq R\}
,
such that moreover there exists a smooth a function
\Psi
on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

for some
\tau_{1}
such that
\Psi|_{\Sigma(\tau_{0})}=\uppsi
, and
n\Psi|_{\Sigma(\tau_{0})\cap\{r\leq R\}}=\uppsi^{\prime}
,
where
n
denotes the normal to
\Sigma(\tau_{0})\cap\{r\leq R\}
.

We recall the energy norm

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}[\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime}]

(4.7)

from
[
DHRT22
]
defined by
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}[\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime}]=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})[\psi]
,
where the spacetime derivatives of
\psi
contained in the latter
are interpreted in terms of the obvious computation
that arises from setting

\psi|_{\Sigma(\tau_{0})}=\uppsi,\qquad n\psi|_{\Sigma(\tau_{0})\cap\{r\leq R\}}=\uppsi^{\prime}

(4.8)

and imposing the equation (
4.1
) formally.
Note of course that the expression (
4.7
) may well be infinite.

Proposition 4.2.1

(Local well posedness (cf.
[
DHRT22
]
) and Cauchy stability)
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g_{a,M})
be the sub-extremal Kerr manifold of
Section
2.1
for
|a|<M
, and
consider equation (
4.1
) satisfying the assumptions
of Section
4.1
.
Fix either
p=0
or

\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
.

There exists a positive integer
k_{\rm loc}\geq 4
such that the following holds. Let
k\geq k_{\rm loc}
.
Then there exists a positive real constant
C>0
sufficiently large,
a positive real parameter
\varepsilon_{\rm loc}>0
sufficiently small, and a decreasing positive function
\tau_{\rm exist}:(0,\varepsilon_{\rm loc})\to\mathbb{R}

such that for all smooth initial data
(\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime})
on
\Sigma(\tau_{0})
such that

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}[\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime}]\leq\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm loc},

there exists a smooth solution
\psi
of (
4.1
)
in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
for
\tau_{1}:=\tau_{0}+\tau_{\rm exist}
attaining
the data (
4.8
) and satisfying

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]\leq C\varepsilon_{0}.

(4.9)

Moreover,
\Sigma(\tau_{0})
is a past Cauchy hypersurface for
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

and
for all
\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}
,
any other smooth
\widetilde{\psi}
defined on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)
satisfying (
4.1
) in

\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)
and attaining the initial data, i.e. satisfying (
4.8
) (with
\widetilde{\psi}

replacing
\psi
), coincides with the restriction of
\psi
, i.e.
\widetilde{\psi}=\psi|_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)}
.

We have moreover that for
\psi
above, the finiteness (
3.80
) holds
with
\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi
replacing

\psi
for all
|{\bf k}|\leq k
,
and thus the
limiting quantity at
\mathcal{I}^{+}
on the left hand side of (
3.81
)
is well defined
(again with
\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi
replacing

\psi
) and the nonnegativity (
3.81
) holds.
The energy identity corresponding to such
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}_{g_{a,M}}^{\rm total}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi]

may thus be integrated globally in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
with this limiting quantity as additional boundary term.

We have in addition the following propagation of higher order regularity and/or higher weighted estimates.
Given
p
,
k\geq k_{\rm loc}
,
\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm loc}
,
\tau_{0}
,
\tau_{1}
as above,

2-\delta\geq p^{\prime}\geq p
if
p\geq\delta
and either
2-\delta\geq p^{\prime}\geq\delta
or
p^{\prime}=0
if
p=0
, and
k^{\prime}\geq k
, then there exists a constant
C(k^{\prime},\tau_{1}-\tau_{0})
such that
for all
\psi
as above,

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p^{\prime})$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny{$k^{\prime}$}}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]\leq C(k^{\prime},\tau_{1}-\tau_{0})\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p^{\prime})$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny{$k^{\prime}$}}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})[\psi].

We have the following weak global uniqueness statement for
the case
\tau>\tau_{1}
. Suppose
\tau>\tau_{1}
and we are given a smooth solution

\widetilde{\psi}
on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)
with the same data such that
we assume moreover
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau)[\widetilde{\psi}]\leq C\varepsilon_{0}
. Then

\widetilde{\psi}
is a smooth extension of
\psi
and any other solution

\widetilde{\widetilde{\psi}}
on

\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)
with the same initial data
moreover satisfies
\widetilde{\widetilde{\psi}}=\widetilde{\psi}
.

Finally, we have the following Cauchy stability statement:
Suppose

\tau_{0}<\tau<\infty
and
\widetilde{\psi}
is a solution of (
4.1
)
on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)
with initial data

(\widetilde{\uppsi},\widetilde{\uppsi}^{\prime})
satisfying

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}[\widetilde{\uppsi},\widetilde{\uppsi}^{\prime}]\leq\varepsilon_{0}/2,\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau)[\widetilde{\psi}]\leq C\varepsilon_{0}/2,

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu+\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}[\widetilde{\uppsi},\widetilde{\uppsi}^{\prime}]\leq D<\infty,\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu+\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}[\widetilde{\widetilde{\uppsi}},\widetilde{\widetilde{\uppsi}}^{\prime}]\leq D<\infty,

then there exists an
\varepsilon_{\rm Cauchy}(D)>0

such that if

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}[\widetilde{\uppsi}-\widetilde{\widetilde{\uppsi}},\widetilde{\uppsi}^{\prime}-\widetilde{\widetilde{\uppsi}}^{\prime}]\leq\varepsilon_{\rm Cauchy}
,
then
\widetilde{\widetilde{\psi}}
exists on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau)
and satisfies

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}[\widetilde{\widetilde{\uppsi}},\widetilde{\widetilde{\uppsi}}^{\prime}]\leq\varepsilon_{0},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau)[\widetilde{\widetilde{\psi}}]\leq C\varepsilon_{0}.

The above Cauchy stability statement will be useful for the continuity argument applied
in fixed spacetime
slabs, which for technical convenience will be formulated as continuity
with respect to
data
and not with respect to time.

4.3
Basic assumptions on
\psi
, the auxiliary
\widehat{\psi}
and comparison for top order
energies in a fixed
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

In the rest of this section, we fix
\tau_{0}+2\leq\tau_{1}
and consider
a solution
\psi
of (
4.1
) on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

satisfying the assumptions of Section
4.1

with
a\neq 0
. (We will discuss the
a=0
case and the issue
of uniform dependence of our estimates on
a
in Remark
4.7.2
.)

We first introduce our basic smallness assumption in Section
4.3.1
(necessary already for the stability of energy current coercivity to be shown
in Section
4.4
).
We then define in Section
4.3.2
an auxiliary
\widehat{\psi}
extending
\psi
in the future by solving the linear homogeneous wave equation. This extension will be useful technically.
Finally, we will define our top order energies in Section
4.3.3

and give some elementary
comparison properties.

4.3.1
Basic smallness assumption

We will need to assume an additional smallness assumption on
\psi
.
For many of the results of this section it would be sufficient to assume that

|\psi|_{C^{1}(\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r\leq R/2\})}\leq\varepsilon,

which, in view of our assumptions on
g(\psi,x)
from Section
4.1
, would imply

|g-g_{a,M}|_{C^{1}(\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\cap\{r\leq R/2\})}\lesssim\varepsilon.

(4.10)

Importantly, for
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small, this already implies that
the spacelike character of
\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\leq R/2\}
and
\mathcal{S}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
is retained with respect
to
g
,
and that all induced volume forms with respect to both metrics are comparable.
(Recall that
g(\psi,x)=g_{a,M}
for
r\geq R/2
.)

For convenience, let us make already the stronger assumption

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]\leq\varepsilon

(4.11)

for suitably small
\varepsilon
,
and for a large
k
, large enough such that
\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{\small$\ll$}}}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}
is itself sufficiently large
such that (
4.10
) is a consequence
of (
4.11
) and the Sobolev estimate of Proposition
2.3.1
.

4.3.2
The auxiliary
\widehat{\psi}
and the definition of
\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}

Given
\psi
as above,
we define
\widehat{\psi}
as follows.
First, let us define

\hat{g}:=g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x),\qquad\hat{F}:=N(\partial\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)

where
\chi_{\tau_{1}}:=1
for
\tau\leq\tau_{1}-1
and
\chi_{\tau_{1}}:=0
for
\tau\geq\tau_{1}
,
and so that
\chi_{\tau_{1}}(\tau-\tau_{1})=\chi_{\tau_{1}^{\prime}}(\tau-\tau_{1}^{\prime})
.
Now let
\widehat{\psi}
be the unique solution in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\infty)

of the initial value problem for
the linear equation

\Box_{\hat{g}}\widehat{\psi}=\hat{F},

with initial data on
\Sigma(\tau_{0})
coincinding with the data of
\psi
.
Note that in the region where
\chi_{\tau_{1}}=1
, we have
\widehat{\psi}=\psi
.
while in the region
\chi_{\tau_{1}}=0
then
\widehat{\psi}
solves the linear homogeneous
equation
\Box_{g_{a,M}}\widehat{\psi}=0
.

Finally, we fix

\widehat{\tau}_{1}:=\tau_{1}+L_{\rm long}[\psi],

for an
L_{\rm long}
(depending on
\psi
!) to be determined later (immediately before Remark
4.5.4
), and
we define

\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}
by (
3.49
), but replacing
\psi
with
\widehat{\psi}

and replacing
\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}
with
\chi_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}
, i.e.

\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}:=P_{n}(\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\widehat{\psi}).

(4.12)

Let us note that

Proposition 4.3.1
.

With
\psi
and
\widehat{\psi}
as above, we have

{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})[\widehat{\psi}]\lesssim{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi],\qquad\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)[\widehat{\psi}]\lesssim\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)[\psi],

{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})[\widehat{\psi}]+{}^{\natural}\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})[\widehat{\psi}]\lesssim{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]+{}^{\natural}\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi],\qquad{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})[\widehat{\psi}]\lesssim{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi],

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})[\widehat{\psi}]\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi],\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}{}^{*}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})[\widehat{\psi}]\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}{}^{*}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi],

where the implicit constants are in particular
independent
of the choice of
L_{\rm long}
.

Proof.

This follows easily from Theorem
3.2
.
∎

In view of the above, we shall primarily use the quantities relating to
\psi
on the right
hand side of estimates.
In particular,
{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
, etc., will always denote

{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]
, unless indicated otherwise.

Remark 4.3.2
.

As discussed in Section
1.2.4
,
the reason for introducing the future extension
\widehat{\psi}
is that our top order estimates will require going to a late time in the future in order to estimate an earlier time.
See already Lemma
4.5.3
. Note that by
[
DRSR16
]
, it follows
that
\widehat{\psi}

decays to zero at fixed
r
as
\tau\to\infty
.
In principle, one could work directly with
\widehat{\psi}
cut off only near
\tau_{0}
,
but note that this decay
would generically only be at some fixed inverse power law rate.
The additional cutoff at
\widehat{\tau}_{1}
is simply to ensure that
\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\widehat{\psi}
lies in the Schwartz class.

Remark 4.3.3
.

We emphasise that
L_{\rm long}=L_{\rm long}[\psi]
depends on the solution
\psi
and is thus

not
to be considered a parameter in the
sense of Section
2.1.5
.
Thus, in particular, the implicit constants in the inequalities in the above
proposition and all inequalities to follow will
always
be independent of
L_{\rm long}
(and
\widehat{\tau}_{1}=\tau_{1}+L_{\rm long}
), even though the precise values
of (
4.12
) of course depend on
L_{\rm long}
.

4.3.3
Top order energies and a comparison theorem

In addition to the energies of
[
DHRT22
]
reviewed in Section
2.3
,
we will need
an additional energy to replace the
\mathfrak{E}
energies of
[
DHRT22
]
.
These energies have been discussed already informally in Section
1.2.4
.

Recall from Section
3.4

that the boundary terms of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}^{{\rm total},n}
on
\Sigma(\tau)
enjoy coercivity properties
in the region
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n}\cup\{r\geq R_{\rm freq}\}

whereas the bulk term
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}^{{\rm total},n}
enjoy coercivity properties in

\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}\setminus\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n}
.

(Recall always that for generalised superradiant ranges, we have

\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n}=\emptyset
.)

In anticipation of the coercive terms in our energy identities,
we define

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{{\rm bdry}}(\tau)[\widehat{\psi}]

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n}\sum_{|{\bf k}|=k}\int_{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n}\cap\Sigma(\tau)}|L\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}+|\underline{L}\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}

(4.13)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\int_{\{r\geq R_{\rm freq}\}\cap\Sigma(\tau)}r^{p}|r^{-1}L(r{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi})|^{2}+r^{\frac{p}{2}}|L\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|\not{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\nabla\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+r^{\frac{p}{2}-2}|{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+\iota_{r\leq R}|\underline{L}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2},

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{{\rm bulk}}(\tau)[\widehat{\psi}]

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n}\sum_{|{\bf k}|=k}\int_{(\{r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}\setminus\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n})\cap\Sigma(\tau)}|L\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}+|\underline{L}\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}

(4.14)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\{r\geq R_{\rm freq}\}\cap\Sigma(\tau)}r^{p-1}(|r^{-1}Lr{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|L{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}|+|\not{\nabla}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2})+r^{-1-\delta}|\underline{L}{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+r^{p-3}|{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\epsilon_{\rm redder}\int_{\{r\leq r_{1}\}\cap\Sigma(\tau)}|L\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|\underline{L}\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2},

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)[\widehat{\psi}]

\displaystyle:=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{\rm bdry}(\tau)[\widehat{\psi}]+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{\rm bulk}(\tau)[\widehat{\psi}],

(4.15)

where
\epsilon_{\rm redder}>0
is a sufficiently small parameter to be chosen later.
We note finally that we are borrowing here the
\mathfrak{E}
notation from
[
DHRT22
]
,
despite not tracking
exact constants (i.e. these energies are not defined by the fluxes themselves), to emphasise that (as in
[
DHRT22
]
) the estimates for these energies will be derived
by applying an energy identity with
respect to the operator
\Box_{g(\psi,x)}
.
Note in particular, however, that as these do not represent exact fluxes, the volume forms
above are here for convenience taken with respect to
g_{a,M}
, and not
g
,
unlike in
[
DHRT22
]
.

Let us note finally that the above definitions (
4.13
) and (
4.14
) are
global: They depend in particular also on the choice of fixed
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

one is considering, and moreover, they also depend of course
on
L_{\rm long}
determining
\widehat{\tau}_{1}
and thus
\widehat{\psi}
.

Because the above energies are defined only with the commutation operators
\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}
and
\mathfrak{D}
, they are not pointwise
coercive as they do not span derivatives in all directions.
Nonetheless, we have the following:

Proposition 4.3.4
.

Let
k\geq k_{\rm prelim}
for
k_{\rm prelim}
sufficiently large, and let

\psi
be a smooth solution of (
4.1
) on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

satisfying (
4.11
) for sufficiently small
\varepsilon
.
Then, defining
\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}

by (
4.12
) for arbitrary
L_{\rm long}
, and defining
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)
by (
4.15
),
we
have for all
\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\widehat{\tau}_{1}-1
, the inequality

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)[\widehat{\psi}]\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})[\psi]+{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)[\widehat{\psi}]

(4.16)

and for all
\tau_{0}+1\leq\tau\leq\widehat{\tau}_{1}-1
, the inequality

{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}}{}_{r\leq r_{1}}[\widehat{\psi}]+{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)[\widehat{\psi}]\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{{\rm bulk}}(\tau)[\widehat{\psi}]+{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau)[\widehat{\psi}].

(4.17)

Proof.

Inequality (
4.16
) follows from the triangle inequality, the Sobolev inequality of
Proposition
2.3.1
and the elliptic estimates of
Proposition
2.3.3
(cf. Corollaries 4.3.3 and 4.5.3 of
[
DHRT22
]
)
and Proposition
4.3.1
.

Inequality (
4.17
) on the other hand follows simply from the triangle inequality.
∎

We also have a reverse inequality

Proposition 4.3.5
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
4.3.4
, for all

\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\widehat{\tau}_{1}-1
, we have

\int_{\tau+\frac{1}{2}}^{\tau+1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]d\tau^{\prime}\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)[\widehat{\psi}]+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi].

(4.18)

Proof.

We have

\int_{\tau+\frac{1}{2}}^{\tau+1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]\lesssim\int_{\tau}^{\tau+\frac{3}{2}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)[\widehat{\psi}]+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})[\widehat{\psi}]

from the properties of the pseudodifferential calculus (Proposition
3.2.2
and
3.2.3
),
whence (
4.18
) then follows from Proposition
4.3.1

and Proposition
4.2.1
, provided

k_{\rm loc}\leq\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{\small$\ll$}}}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}
.
∎

We note that the
\lesssim
in the Proposition
4.3.4
depends (unfavourably!) on
the yet-to-be-chosen parameter
\epsilon_{\rm redder}
introduced in (
4.14
).
The following proposition will provide an
\epsilon_{\rm redder}
-independent
statement, which will be necessary for absorbing some top order
\epsilon_{\rm redder}
-error terms.

Proposition 4.3.6
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
4.3.4
, we have
the additional statement

\int_{\tau_{1}+2}^{\widehat{\tau}_{1}-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}_{r_{1}\leq r\leq r_{2}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]d\tau^{\prime}\lesssim\inf_{\tau_{1}+1\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau_{1}+2}\mathring{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]

(4.19)

where we recall
\mathring{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}}(\tau)[\psi]

is defined in (
2.20
).

Proof.

Note that
\widehat{\psi}
satisfies the homogeneous wave equation
\Box_{g_{a,M}}\hat{\psi}=0

in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{1}+1,\widehat{\tau}_{1}-1)
.
The statement then follows from the
k=0
case of Theorem
3.1
after applying
commutation by
\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}
and
the elliptic estimate (
2.3.3
).
∎

As the above proposition is proven without reference to the energies (
4.14
),
the constant above is in particular independent of
\epsilon_{\rm redder}
.
As a corollary of the above and the previous, we obtain an
\epsilon_{\rm redder}
-independent
estimate (involving
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{{\rm bulk}}
on the right hand side) of the following form:

Proposition 4.3.7
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
4.3.4
,
we have

\int_{\tau_{0}+1}^{\widehat{\tau}_{1}-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}_{r_{1}\leq r\leq r_{2}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]d\tau^{\prime}\lesssim\int_{\tau_{0}+2}^{\tau_{1}+2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{{\rm bulk}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]d\tau^{\prime}+{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})[\psi]+\inf_{\tau_{1}+1\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau_{1}+2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]

where the implicit constant in
\lesssim
is independent of the choice of

\epsilon_{\rm redder}
in (
4.14
).

Proof.

We note first that for
\tau_{0}+1\leq\tau\leq\widehat{\tau}_{1}-1
,
we have the following variant of (
4.16
)

\mathring{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]+{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]

(4.20)

and

\mathring{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}}_{r_{1}\leq r\leq r_{2}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}_{\rm bulk}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]+{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]

(4.21)

where the constant implicit in
\lesssim
is independent of
\epsilon_{\rm redder}
.
Integrating in
\tau^{\prime}
we obtain

\int_{\tau_{0}+2}^{\tau_{1}+2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}_{r_{1}\leq r\leq r_{2}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]d\tau^{\prime}\lesssim\int_{\tau_{0}+2}^{\tau_{1}+2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{{\rm bulk}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]d\tau^{\prime}+{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]

where the constant implicit in
\lesssim
is again
independent of
\epsilon_{\rm redder}
.
Thus
the desired statement follows combining the above and (
4.19
)
applied to (
4.20
).
∎

4.4
The covariant currents
J^{{\rm total},n}_{g}
,
K^{{\rm total},n}_{g}

and stability of generalised coercivity properties

Let us now promote our currents of Section
3.4
to covariant currents
with respect to a general Lorentzian metric
g
on
\mathcal{M}
, i.e. let
us define

J^{{\rm main},n}_{g}[\Psi],\qquad K^{{\rm main},n}_{g}[\Psi],\qquad H^{{\rm main},n}_{g}[\Psi]

(4.22)

by making our currents covariant in
g
, either directly in twisted form or equivalently by
reexpressing the currents

J^{{\rm main},n}_{g_{a,M}}[\Psi]
,

K^{{\rm main},n}_{g_{a,M}}[\Psi]
,
H^{{\rm main},n}_{g_{a,M}}[\Psi]

with respect to the

V_{n}
,
w_{n}
,
q_{n}
,
\varpi_{n}
parametrisation
and replacing
g_{a,M}
with
g
in
the formulas (
2.9
)–(
2.11
).

We similarly now define

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}^{\rm total}_{g}[\Psi],\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}^{\rm total}_{g}[\Psi],\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}^{{\rm total}}_{g}[\Psi]

and

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}^{\rm redder}_{g}[\Psi],\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}^{\rm redder}_{g}[\Psi],\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}^{{\rm redder}}_{g}[\Psi]

by promoting to covariant the currents defined in Section
3.5
.

We have the following:

Proposition 4.4.1

(Stability of coercivity properties with allowed errors)
.

Let
g
be a metric on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

such that
|g-g_{a,M}|_{C^{1}}\lesssim\varepsilon
and
g=g_{a,M}
for
r\geq R/2
, for
a\neq 0
,
and let (
4.22
) be the currents defined above.

Then for
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small,
we have that
\mathcal{S}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
and
\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\leq R\}
for all
\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}

are spacelike with respect to
g
, and
for a general function
\Psi
, the following
boundary and bulk coercivity statements hold in the region
r\geq R_{\rm freq}
:

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}{}_{g}^{{\rm total}}[\Psi]\cdot{\rm n}_{\Sigma(\tau)}\gtrsim

\displaystyle r^{p}|r^{-1}L(r\Psi)|^{2}+r^{\frac{p}{2}}|L\Psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\Psi|^{2}+r^{\frac{p}{2}-2}|\Psi|^{2}+\iota_{r\leq R}|\underline{L}\Psi|^{2},

\displaystyle\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta,

(4.23)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{J}{}_{g}^{{\rm total}}[\Psi]\cdot{\rm n}_{\Sigma(\tau)}\gtrsim

\displaystyle|L\Psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\Psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|\Psi|^{2}+\iota_{r\leq R}|\underline{L}\Psi|^{2},

\displaystyle p=0,

(4.24)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}{}^{{\rm total}}_{g}[\Psi]\gtrsim

\displaystyle r^{p-1}((r^{-1}|Lr\Psi|)^{2}+|L\Psi|+|\not{\nabla}\Psi|^{2})+r^{-1-\delta}|\underline{L}\Psi|^{2}+r^{p-3}|\Psi|^{2},

\displaystyle\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta,

(4.25)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{K}{}^{{\rm total}}_{g}[\Psi]\gtrsim

\displaystyle r^{-1-\delta}(|L\Psi|+|\not{\nabla}\Psi|^{2}+|\underline{L}\Psi|^{2})+r^{-3-\delta}|\Psi|^{2},

\displaystyle p=0.

(4.26)

On
\Sigma(\tau)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{n}
, we have, for a general function
\Psi
,
the boundary coercivity statement

J{}_{g}^{{\rm main},n}[\Psi]\cdot{\rm n}_{\Sigma(\tau)}\gtrsim|L\Psi|^{2}+|\underline{L}\Psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\Psi|^{2}+|\Psi|^{2}.

(4.27)

On
\mathcal{S}
we have, for a general function
\Psi
,
the boundary coercivity statement

J^{\rm redder}_{g}[\Psi]\cdot{\rm n}_{\mathcal{S}}\geq c(r_{0})(|L\Psi|^{2}+|\underline{L}\Psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\Psi|^{2})

(4.28)

and on
\Sigma(\tau)
the positivity statement

J_{g}^{\rm redder}[\psi]\cdot{\rm n}_{\Sigma(\tau)}\geq 0.

(4.29)

For a general function
\Psi
, we moreover
have
in the region
r_{0}\leq r\leq r_{1}+2\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{3}
the bulk coercivity statement

K{}^{{\rm redder}}_{g}[\Psi]\gtrsim|L\Psi|^{2}+|\underline{L}\Psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\Psi|^{2},

(4.30)

and for a solution
\psi
of (
4.1
) in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

with
g=g(\psi,x)

under the assumption (
4.11
), the bulk coercivity:

\displaystyle\sum_{|{\bf k}|=k}\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq r_{1}+2\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{3}\}}K{}^{{\rm redder}}_{g}[\alpha({\bf k})\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi]+{\rm Re}(H{}^{\rm redder}_{g}[\psi]\cdot\overline{\Box_{g_{a,M}}\alpha({\bf k})\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi})

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq r_{1}+2\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{3}\}}\frac{1}{4}K{}^{\rm redder}_{g}[\alpha({\bf k})\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi]-C\,{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})[\psi],

(4.31)

where
\alpha({\bf k})
are as in Proposition
3.5.2
,
and a similar estimate holds for
\widehat{\psi}
for
\tau_{0}+1\leq\tilde{\tau}_{0}\leq\tilde{\tau}_{1}-2\leq\widehat{\tau}_{1}-3
and
g=g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)
.

For a solution

\psi
of (
4.1
) in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

under the assumption (
4.11
), we have the bulk coercivity:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\geq R/4\}}\sum_{|{\bf k}|=k}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}{}^{{\rm total}}_{g}[\alpha({\bf k})\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi]+{\rm Re}(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}{}^{{\rm total}}_{g}[\psi]\cdot\overline{\Box_{g_{a,M}}\alpha({\bf k})\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi})

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\geq R/4\}}\frac{1}{4}\sum_{|{\bf k}|=k}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}{}^{{\rm total}}_{g}[\alpha({\bf k})\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\psi]-C\,{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}),

(4.32)

and a similar estimate holds for
\widehat{\psi}
for
\tau_{0}+1\leq\tilde{\tau}_{0}\leq\tilde{\tau}_{1}-2\leq\widehat{\tau}_{1}-3
and
g=g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)
and
g=g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)
.

Finally, applied to the projections
\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}
,
we have
for all
\tau_{0}+1\leq\tilde{\tau}_{0}\leq\tilde{\tau}_{1}-2\leq\widehat{\tau}_{1}-3
the coercivity statements

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{S}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})}{J_{g}^{{\rm main},n}}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\cdot{\rm n}_{\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{S}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})}c(r_{0})(|L\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}+|\underline{L}\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2})

(4.33)

\displaystyle\qquad-C\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tilde{\tau}_{0}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff},\tilde{\tau}_{0}+\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})-C\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tilde{\tau}_{1}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff},\tilde{\tau}_{1}+\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})

\displaystyle\qquad-C\,{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi],

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{\rm fixed}\}}K^{{\rm main},n}_{g}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{\rm fixed}\}}\chi_{n}\left(|L\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}+|\underline{L}\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}\right)\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,

(4.34)

\displaystyle\qquad-C\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}\,\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)[\psi]-C\,{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]

(4.35)

\displaystyle\qquad-\varepsilon C\int_{\tilde{\tau}_{0}}^{\tilde{\tau}_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)[\widehat{\psi}]d\tau

(4.36)

where
k:=|{\bf k}|
, and where the convention of Section
2.1.5

regarding
dependence of constants on
\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}
has been adhered to.

Here the normals and volume forms are with respect to
g
.
Under the assumption (
4.11
),
if
g=g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)
as in Section
4.1
,
then
\varepsilon
on the right hand side of
line (
4.36
) may be replaced by
\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}
.

Proof.

The purely pointwise statements all follow
immediately from the covariant nature of the currents and the
C^{1}
closeness
of
g
and
g_{a,M}
.
To obtain (
4.31
), we have also used the
more general inhomogeneous version of (
3.78
) and the
elliptic estimates
of Proposition
2.3.3
while
to obtain (
4.32
), we have used the more general inhomogeneous version
of (
3.79
), the elliptic estimates
of Proposition
2.3.3

and Assumption
4.1.1
(see
[
DHRT22
]
for details). For the inequalities
for
\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}
, note that
we have also used Proposition
4.3.1
.
∎

4.5
The top order energy identity and estimates

In this section we will apply our currents at top order to solutions of (
4.1
)
with
g=g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)
to obtain the following statement:

Proposition 4.5.1

(Top order energy estimate)
.

Let
k
be sufficiently large, the parameters
\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}
of Section
3.2.1
and
\epsilon_{\rm redder}
of (
4.14
) be sufficiently small,
and
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small,
let
\psi
be a solution of the quasilinear
equation (
4.1
)
(satisfying the assumptions of Section
4.1
with
a\neq 0
)
in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

and assume the bound (
4.11
).

Then there exists an
L_{\rm long}[\psi]\geq 1
(depending in fact
only on
{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]
) such that
defining
\widehat{\tau}_{1}:=\tau_{1}+L_{\rm long}
, and the associated

\widehat{\psi}
, etc.,
the following is true.
For
p=0
or
0<\delta\leq p\leq 2-\delta
, it follows that

{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})[\psi]+\mathcal{Y}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})[\widehat{\psi}],

(4.37)

where
\mathcal{Y}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})
is defined in (
4.65
).

Remark 4.5.2
.

Again we emphasise that the implicit constants are in particular independent of
L_{\rm long}[\psi]
.

Proof.

Let
\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}
be defined as in Section
4.3.2
by (
4.12
)
for
L_{\rm long}
to be determined.
The expression
g
below will denote
g=g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)
.
It follows that

\Box_{g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)}\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}=F_{n,{\bf k}}:=F^{\rm cutoff}_{n,{\bf k}}+F^{\rm commu}_{n,{\bf k}}+F^{\rm pseudo}_{n,{\bf k}}+F^{\rm semi}_{n,{\bf k}}

(4.38)

where
F^{\rm cutoff}_{n,{\bf k}}
represent “linear” terms from the time cutoffs

F^{\rm cutoff}_{n,{\bf k}}:=P_{n}(\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\Box_{g}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}})\widehat{\psi}+2\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\nabla_{g}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\cdot\nabla_{g}\widehat{\psi})),

(4.39)

F^{\rm commu}_{n,{\bf k}}
represents the
nonlinear commutation terms

F^{\rm commu}_{n,{\bf k}}:=P_{n}[\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k},\Box_{g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi_{,}x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\chi_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}^{2}\psi,

F^{\rm pseudo}_{n,{\bf k}}
represent nonlinear pseudodifferential commutation terms

F^{\rm pseudo}_{n,{\bf k}}:=[P_{n},\Box_{g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)}]\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}=[P_{n},\Box_{g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\widehat{\psi}

and
F^{\rm semi}_{n,{\bf k}}
represents nonlinear terms arising from the semilinear expression

F^{\rm semi}_{n,{\bf k}}:=P_{n}(\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}(N(\partial\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x))).

On the other hand, we have

\Box_{g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)}\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}=F_{{\bf k}}:=F^{\rm lincom}_{{\bf k}}+F^{\rm nonlincom}_{{\bf k}}+F^{\rm semi}_{{\bf k}}

(4.40)

where

F^{\rm lincom}_{{\bf k}}
represents the “linear” commutation terms

F^{\rm lincom}_{\bf k}:=[{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k},\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\widehat{\psi},

(4.41)

F^{\rm nonlincom}_{\bf k}
represents the nonlinear commutation terms

F^{\rm nonlincom}_{\bf k}:=[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi_{,}x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\psi,

and
F^{\rm semi}_{{\bf k}}
represents the semilinear terms

F^{\rm semi}_{\bf k}:=\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}N(\partial\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x).

We apply now the energy identity associated to the current

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}^{{\rm main},n}_{g}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]
of (
4.22
)
where
g=g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)
:

\nabla^{\mu}_{g}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}_{g}^{{\rm main},n}{}_{\mu}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}_{g}^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]+{\rm Re}\left(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}_{g}^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\overline{F_{n,{\bf k}}}\right).

(4.42)

Dropping now the
g
subscript,
integrating (
4.42
) in an arbitrary

\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}

for
\tau_{0}+3\leq\tilde{\tau}_{0}+2\leq\tilde{\tau}_{1}\leq\widehat{\tau}_{1}-1
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma(\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\cdot{\rm n}+\int_{\mathcal{S}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\cdot{\rm n}+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma(\tilde{\tau}_{0})\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\cdot{\rm n}-\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}{\rm Re}\left(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\overline{F_{n,{\bf k}}}\right)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r=R_{\rm freq}\}}J^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\cdot{\rm n}_{\{r=R_{\rm freq}\}},

where the currents and volume forms are all with respect to
g=g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)
.
We now sum the above identity over
n
and
|{\bf k}|=k
with coefficients
\alpha({\bf k})

from Propositions
3.5.2
and
3.5.4

(here we may interpret these coefficients as applying to the
\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}
operators
as
\alpha(k_{1},k_{2}):=\alpha(k_{1},k_{2},0,\ldots)
)
and add it to the following identity
summed over
{\bf k}
(now corresponding to the
\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}
operators) with
|{\bf k}|=k
, with coefficients
\alpha({\bf k})
:

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma(\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\geq R_{\rm freq}\}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}^{{\rm total}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]\cdot{\rm n}+\int_{\mathcal{I}^{+}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}^{{\rm total}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]\cdot{\rm n}+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\geq R_{\rm freq}\}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{K}^{{\rm total}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}{\rm Re}\left(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}^{{\rm total}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]\overline{F^{\rm lincom}_{\bf k}}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma(\tilde{\tau}_{0})\cap\{r\geq R_{\rm freq}\}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}^{{\rm total}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]\cdot{\rm n}-\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}{\rm Re}\left(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}^{{\rm total}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}](\overline{F^{\rm nonlincom}_{\bf k}}+\overline{F^{\rm semi}_{\bf k}})\right)

(4.43)

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r=R_{\rm freq}\}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{J}^{{\rm total}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]\cdot{\rm n}_{\{r=R_{\rm freq}\}}.

Recalling the parameter
\epsilon_{\rm redder}
from (
4.14
), we finally
add
\epsilon_{\rm redder}>0
times the identity, summed with
\alpha({\bf k})
coefficients:

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma(\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\leq r_{2}\}}J^{{\rm redder}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]\cdot{\rm n}+\int_{\mathcal{S}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})}J^{{\rm redder}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]\cdot{\rm n}+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\leq r_{2}\}}K^{{\rm redder}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\leq r_{2}\}}{\rm Re}\left(H^{{\rm redder}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]\overline{F^{\rm lincom}_{\bf k}}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma(\tilde{\tau}_{0})\cap\{r\leq r_{2}\}}J^{{\rm redder}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]\cdot{\rm n}-\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\leq r_{2}\}}{\rm Re}\left(H^{{\rm redder}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}](\overline{F^{\rm nonlincom}_{\bf k}}+\overline{F^{\rm semi}_{\bf k}})\right).

(4.44)

(Note that (
4.44
) does not contain any other boundary terms in view
of the support of the current
J^{\rm redder}
.)

The resulting summed identity yields the following inequality:

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{{\rm bdry}}(\tilde{\tau}_{1})

\displaystyle+\int_{\tilde{\tau}_{0}}^{\tilde{\tau}_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{\rm bulk}(\tau^{\prime})\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{\rm bdry}(\tilde{\tau}_{0})+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{\rm bulk}(\tilde{\tau}_{0})+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{\rm bulk}(\tilde{\tau}_{1})

(4.45)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon_{\rm redder}\int_{\tilde{\tau}_{0}}^{\tilde{\tau}_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}_{r_{1}\leq r\leq r_{2}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]d\tau^{\prime}

(4.46)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}\,\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)

(4.47)

\displaystyle\quad+\,{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})+\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\int_{\tilde{\tau}_{0}}^{\tilde{\tau}_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau)[\widehat{\psi}]d\tau

(4.48)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{|{\bf k}|=k}\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\geq R_{\rm freq}\}}|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}^{{\rm total}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]F^{\rm nonlincom}_{{\bf k}}|+|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}^{{\rm total}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]F^{\rm semi}_{{\bf k}}|

(4.49)

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon_{\rm redder}\sum_{|{\bf k}|=k}\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq r_{1}\}}|H^{{\rm redder}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]F^{\rm nonlincom}_{{\bf k}}|+|H^{{\rm redder}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]F^{\rm semi}_{{\bf k}}|

(4.50)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{n,|{\bf k}|=k}\left|\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}{\rm Re}\left(H^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\overline{F^{\rm cutoff}_{n,{\bf k}}}\right)\right|

(4.51)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{n,|{\bf k}|=k}\left|\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}{\rm Re}\left(H^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\overline{F^{\rm commu}_{n,{\bf k}}}\right)\right|

(4.52)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{n,|{\bf k}|=k}\left|\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}{\rm Re}\left(H^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\overline{F^{\rm pseudo}_{n,{\bf k}}}\right)\right|

(4.53)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{n,|{\bf k}|=k}\left|\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}{\rm Re}\left(H^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\overline{F^{\rm semi}_{n,{\bf k}}}\right)\right|

(4.54)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathring{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tilde{\tau}_{0}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff},\tilde{\tau}_{0}+\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})[\widehat{\psi}]+\mathring{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tilde{\tau}_{1}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff},\tilde{\tau}_{1}+\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})[\widehat{\psi}]

(4.55)

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r=R_{\rm fixed}\}}\left(\ J^{{\rm total}}\left[\sum_{n,|{\bf k}|=k}\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}-\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}\right]\right)\cdot{\rm n}_{\{r=R_{\rm freq}\}}\right|.

(4.56)

The terms on the left hand side arise from the coercivity properties
of Proposition
4.4.1
, the definitions of the energies of Section
4.3
, and the closeness (
4.10
) which follows from (
4.11
).
In particular, we have used the improved coercivity (
4.31
)
and (
4.32
)
to
already absorb the linear commutation terms (
4.41
) in the bulk.
(Note that
applying these statements generates error terms on the right hand side discussed in connection to
lines (
4.46
)–(
4.48
) and (
4.55
) below.)

The terms on the right hand side arise as follows:

The terms on line (
4.45
) arise from bounding the boundary terms in the region
not coercively controlled by energy.

The term on line (
4.46
) arises from the failure of coercivity of
K^{\rm redder}
in
the support of the cutoff
\nabla\hat{\hat{\zeta}}
. The convention of Section
2.1.5

applies here to the implicit constant multiplying
\epsilon_{\rm redder}
.

The terms on lines (
4.47
)–(
4.48
)
arise from the error terms (
4.35
)–(
4.36
)
in the coercivity estimate of
K^{{\rm main},n}
. Note that we have used here also Proposition
4.3.1
.
Again, the convention of Section
2.1.5

applies here to the implicit constant multiplying
\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}
.

The terms on lines (
4.49
) and (
4.50
)
arise from the inhomogeneous term in (
4.43
) and (
4.44
)
while
the terms on lines (
4.51
)–(
4.54
)
arise from the four inhomogeneous terms
in (
4.38
).

Now we may replace the terms on
lines (
4.51
)–(
4.54
)
with the sum

\displaystyle\quad\sum_{n,|{\bf k}|=k}\left|\int_{\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}{\rm Re}\left(H^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\overline{F^{\rm cutoff}_{n,{\bf k}}}\right)\right|

(4.57)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{n,|{\bf k}|=k}\left|\int_{\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}{\rm Re}\left(H^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\overline{F^{\rm commu}_{n,{\bf k}}}\right)\right|

(4.58)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{n,|{\bf k}|=k}\left|\int_{\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}{\rm Re}\left(H^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\overline{F^{\rm pseudo}_{n,{\bf k}}}\right)\right|

(4.59)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{n,|{\bf k}|=k}\left|\int_{\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}{\rm Re}\left(H^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\overline{F^{\rm semi}_{n,{\bf k}}}\right)\right|

(4.60)

and an additional term on line (
4.47
) and an additional

\,{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

term on line (
4.48
),
where again the implicit constants are independent of
\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}

except for the constant multiplying the
\,{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
term.

To see this, say for (
4.57
),
we note that (
4.57
) differs from (
4.51
)
by a term which may be estimated by

\sum_{n,|{\bf k}|=k}\int_{\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}\left|\tilde{\tilde{\chi}}^{4}{\rm Re}\left(H^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\overline{F^{\rm cutoff}_{n,{\bf k}}}\right)\right|

where
\tilde{\tilde{\chi}}
is a smooth cutoff supported in an
\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}
-neighbourhood
of
\tau=\tilde{\tau}_{0}
and
\tau=\tilde{\tau}_{1}
.
We may rewrite this schematically as a sum of terms

\int_{\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}\left|\tilde{\tilde{\chi}}^{2}\partial P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}^{2}\widehat{\psi})\cdot\tilde{\tilde{\chi}}^{2}P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\left(\nabla_{g}\chi_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\cdot\nabla_{g}\chi_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\widehat{\psi}\right)\right|

(4.61)

for
|{\bf k}|=k
plus lower order terms. We apply Cauchy–Schwarz. Consider say the term arising from the first factor of (
4.61
).
Applying Proposition
3.2.4
,
we may rewrite

\tilde{\tilde{\chi}}^{2}\partial_{x^{i}}P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}=\tilde{\tilde{\chi}}^{2}P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\partial_{x^{i}}=\tilde{\tilde{\chi}}P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\tilde{\tilde{\chi}}\partial_{x^{i}}+\tilde{\tilde{\chi}}Q_{1}\partial_{x^{i}}=\tilde{\tilde{\chi}}P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\tilde{\tilde{\chi}}\partial_{x^{i}}+Q_{1}\tilde{\tilde{\chi}}\partial_{x^{i}}+Q_{2}\partial_{x^{i}}

where
Q_{1}
is of order
k-1
and
Q_{2}
is of order
k-2
.
We see by Proposition
3.2.2

that the resulting terms can be bounded by

C\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}\,\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)[\psi]+C\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)[\psi]+C\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi],

(4.62)

where we have used also Proposition
4.3.1
,
and where the first
C
is independent of

\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}
whereas the other
C
’s depend unfavourably on
\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}
.
Thus this term can indeed be estimated as claimed by the term on line (
4.47
) and
the first term on line (
4.48
), which includes in its definition the latter two terms of (
4.62
).
The term resulting from the second factor in (
4.61
) can be estimated similarly (it is in fact smoothing though we need not make use of this here), as can all other lower order terms.
The terms (
4.58
)–(
4.60
)
can be shown to replace (
4.52
)–(
4.54
)
up to the
error terms described above by a similar computation. (The latter resulting error terms have in fact additional
smallness as they are nonlinear, but it is not necessary to use this.)

The terms on line (
4.55
) appear with a constant independent
of
\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}
and
arise to control the highest order error terms in the boundary coercivity (
4.33
)
(given that the first term of (
4.48
) has already been included on the right hand side).
Regarding the terms on line (
4.55
),
note that
for
\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}
sufficiently small,
we may bound

\displaystyle\mathring{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}}_{\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle(\tilde{\tau}_{0}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff},\tilde{\tau}_{0}+\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})[\widehat{\psi}]+\mathring{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}}_{\mathcal{S}}(\tilde{\tau}_{1}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff},\tilde{\tau}_{1}+\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})[\widehat{\psi}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}}{}_{r\leq r_{1}}(\tilde{\tau}_{0}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})[\widehat{\psi}]+\mathring{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}}{}_{r\leq r_{1}}(\tilde{\tau}_{1}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})[\widehat{\psi}]+\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}\sqrt{\varepsilon}\,\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)

(4.63)

\displaystyle\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{\rm bulk}(\tilde{\tau}_{0}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})[\widehat{\psi}]+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{\rm bulk}(\tilde{\tau}_{1}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})[\widehat{\psi}]+\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}\sqrt{\varepsilon}\,\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)+{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}).

(4.64)

Here, inequality (
4.63
)
holds from the local existence Proposition
4.2.1
,
localised to the future domain of dependence of
\Sigma(\tau)\cap\{r\leq r_{1}\}
with respect to
the metric
g
, where we note that
for
\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}
and
\varepsilon
sufficiently small, this domain of dependence
includes
\mathcal{S}(\tau,\tau+2\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})
.
(More specifically, the estimate follows by applying the divergence identity of

J^{{\rm redder}}_{\Sigma(\tau)}(\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi})

and estimating the error bulk
term by
\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}\sqrt{\varepsilon}\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)
, where
the
\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}
comes from the dimensions of the bulk and
the
\sqrt{\varepsilon}
comes from the estimate of the commutator terms.)
The inequality (
4.64
)
on the other
hand follows from inequality (
4.21
) (appearing in the proof
of Proposition
4.3.7
).
We note that the implicit constants in
\lesssim
of (
4.63
) and (
4.64
) are all independent of both

\epsilon_{\rm redder}
and

\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}
.

The final term (
4.56
) arises by combining the boundary terms on

\{r=R_{\rm fixed}\}
recalling that the currents are independent of
n
there.
This term in fact
vanishes
however in view of the restriction on
\tilde{\tau}_{0}
and
\tilde{\tau}_{1}
so as for
\chi_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}=1
in the domain of integration.

Thus the estimate holds with only (
4.45
)–(
4.50
)
and (
4.51
)–(
4.54
)
on the right hand side provided
that we add the first two terms on the right hand side of (
4.64
) to the right
hand side of line (
4.45
).

We now define

\mathcal{Y}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})[\widehat{\psi}]:=\sum\sup_{\tau_{0}+1\leq\tilde{\tau}_{0}\leq\tilde{\tau}_{0}+2\leq\tilde{\tau}_{1}\leq\widehat{\tau}_{1}-1}\text{\rm terms\ on\ lines\ \eqref{fromcoerc}--\eqref{oldtermsredder}}\text{\rm\ and\ on\ lines\ \eqref{oneinhomogtermheresmooth}--\eqref{andonemoreinhomogtermheresmooth}},

(4.65)

where in the above definition, so as to be able to consistently apply our convention related
to multiplication by
\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}
and
\epsilon_{\rm redder}
,
we keep on the right hand side of (
4.65
) all constants implicit in the
\lesssim
of (
4.45
)
with
unfavourable dependence on
\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}
and
\epsilon_{\rm redder}
together with their correesponding “terms”.
We finally define

\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})[\widehat{\psi}]:=\mathcal{Y}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})[\widehat{\psi}]+\epsilon_{\rm redder}\int_{\tau_{0}+1}^{\widehat{\tau}_{1}-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}_{r_{1}\leq r\leq r_{2}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]d\tau^{\prime}+\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}\,\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)[\psi].

(4.66)

We may thus write the estimate we have obtained as the statement that
for all
\tau_{0}+1\leq\tilde{\tau}_{0}\leq\tilde{\tau}_{1}-2\leq\widehat{\tau}_{1}-3
,
the following holds:

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{{\rm bdry}}(\tilde{\tau}_{1})

\displaystyle+\int_{\tilde{\tau}_{0}}^{\tilde{\tau}_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{\rm bulk}(\tau^{\prime})\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{\rm bdry}(\tilde{\tau}_{0})+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{\rm bulk}(\tilde{\tau}_{0})+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{\rm bulk}(\tilde{\tau}_{1})

\displaystyle+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{\rm bulk}(\tilde{\tau}_{0}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{\rm bulk}(\tilde{\tau}_{1}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})+\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}).

(4.67)

In view of our comment after (
4.65
), we
emphasise that the implicit constant in
\lesssim
is itself independent of
\epsilon_{\rm redder}

and
\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}
, while all unfavourable dependence on
\epsilon_{\rm redder}
and

\epsilon_{\rm redder}
in the estimate is restricted to some of the terms in (
4.65
) appearing in
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})
,
with the bad constant incorporated into the definition of (
4.65
).

To make use of the above estimate, let us first state a simple general calculus lemma.

Lemma 4.5.3
.

Let
B>1
and
H\geq 0
be constants, let
\tau_{0}^{*}+8\leq\widehat{\tau}_{1}
be arbitrary
and let
F(\tau)
,
G(\tau)
be two nonnegative
functions on
[\tau_{0}^{*},\widehat{\tau}_{1}]

satisfying

F(\tilde{\tau}_{1})+\int_{\tilde{\tau}_{0}}^{\tilde{\tau}_{1}}G(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}\leq B(F(\tilde{\tau}_{0})+G(\tilde{\tau}_{0})+G(\tilde{\tau}_{1})+G(\tilde{\tau}_{0}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})+G(\tilde{\tau}_{1}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})+H)

(4.68)

for all
\tau_{0}^{*}+1\leq\tilde{\tau}_{0}\leq\tilde{\tau}_{0}+2\leq\tilde{\tau}_{1}\leq\widehat{\tau}_{1}-1
.

Then, given
\gamma>0
and
\epsilon>0
,
there exists an
L_{\rm lem}=L_{\rm lem}(B,\gamma,\epsilon)\geq 4

depending only on
B
,
\gamma
and
\epsilon
,
such that if

\int_{\tau}^{\tau+\frac{1}{2}}G(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}\leq\gamma

(4.69)

for all
\tau\in[\tau_{0}^{*}+1,\widehat{\tau}_{1}-2]
,
then in fact

\int_{\tau^{*}_{0}+1}^{\hat{\tau}_{1}-L_{\rm lem}+3}G(\tau^{\prime})\leq 2B(F(\tau_{0}^{*}+1)+G(\tau_{0}^{*}+1)+G(\tau_{0}^{*}+1-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})+H)+\epsilon

(4.70)

and

\inf_{\tau\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau+1}F(\tau^{\prime})+G(\tau^{\prime})\leq 14B(F(\tau_{0}^{*}+1)+G(\tau_{0}^{*}+1)+G(\tau_{0}^{*}+1-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})+H)+\epsilon

(4.71)

for all
\tau\in[\tau_{0}^{*}+1,\widehat{\tau}_{1}-L_{\rm lem}]
, provided

\widehat{\tau}_{1}-\tau_{0}^{*}\geq L_{\rm lem}+4
.

Proof of lemma.

By the pigeonhole principle applied to the left hand side of (
4.69
),
there exists a
\widehat{\tau}_{\rm final}\in[\widehat{\tau}_{1}-3/2,\widehat{\tau}_{1}-5/4]

such that

G(\widehat{\tau}_{\rm final})+G(\widehat{\tau}_{\rm final}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})\leq 8\gamma.

By the pigeonhole principle applied to the integral on the left hand side of (
4.68
) with

\tilde{\tau}_{0}=\tau_{0}^{*}+1
and
\tilde{\tau}_{1}=\widehat{\tau}_{\rm final}
,
given
L_{\rm lem}\geq 4
arbitrary such that
\widehat{\tau}_{1}-\tau_{0}^{*}\geq L_{\rm lem}+4
,
there
exists a
\tilde{\tau}_{\rm final}\in[\widehat{\tau}_{1}-L_{\rm lem}+4,\widehat{\tau}_{1}]

such that

\displaystyle G(\tilde{\tau}_{\rm final}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})+G(\tilde{\tau}_{\rm final})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2B}{L_{\rm lem}}(F(\tau_{0}^{*}+1)+G(\widehat{\tau}_{\rm final})+G(\widehat{\tau}_{\rm final}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+G(\tau_{0}^{*}+1)+G(\tau_{0}^{*}+1-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})+H)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2B}{L_{\rm lem}}(F(\tau_{0}^{*}+1)+G(\tau_{0}^{*}+1)+G(\tau_{0}^{*}+1-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})+H)+\frac{16B\gamma}{L_{\rm Lem}},

where for the second inequality we have used (
4.69
).

If we require
L_{\rm lem}
sufficiently large such that

|\frac{2B}{L_{\rm lem}}|<1
and
\left|\frac{32B^{3}\gamma}{L_{\rm lem}}\right|<\epsilon
, we that

G(\tilde{\tau}_{\rm final}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})+G(\tilde{\tau}_{\rm final})\leq F(\tau_{0}^{*}+1)+G(\tau_{0}^{*}+1)+G(\tau_{0}^{*}+1-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})+H+\frac{\epsilon}{2B^{2}}.

(4.72)

Now
we may reapply (
4.68
) in
[\tau_{0}^{*}+1,\tilde{\tau}_{\rm final}]
. We immediately
obtain (
4.70
). Moreover,
given arbitrary
\tau_{0}^{*}+2\leq\tau\leq\widehat{\tau}_{1}-L_{\rm lem}
,
and using now (
4.72
)
and the pigeonhole principle it follows
that there exists a
\tau-1\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau
such that

G(\tau^{\prime})+G(\tau^{\prime}-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})\leq 6B(F(\tau_{0}^{*}+1)+G(\tau_{0}^{*}+1)+G(\tau_{0}^{*}+1-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})+H)+\frac{\epsilon}{2B}.

(4.73)

Hence, again applying (
4.68
) now in
[\tau_{0}+1,\tau^{\prime}]
, it follows
that

F(\tau^{\prime})\leq 7B(F(\tau_{0}^{*}+1)+G(\tau_{0}^{*}+1)+G(\tau_{0}^{*}+1-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})+H)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

(4.74)

Estimates (
4.73
) and (
4.74
) yield (
4.71
).
∎

We now apply the lemma to

F(\tau):=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{{\rm bdry}}(\tau)[\widehat{\psi}],\qquad G(\tau):=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{{\rm bulk}}(\tau)[\widehat{\psi}],,\qquad H:=\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})[\widehat{\psi}],

(4.75)

where
L_{\rm long}
and thus
\widehat{\tau}_{1}
and
\widehat{\psi}
will be determined immediately below
and where
\tau_{0}^{*}\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{0}+1]
is arbitrary.
Note that the estimate (
4.67
) yields (
4.68
),
where we take
B
to be the constant implicit in the
\lesssim
of (
4.67
).
Recall that the constant implicit in the
\lesssim
of (
4.67
) is a
general constant in the sense of Section
2.1.5
and thus
in particular,

B
is independent of
L_{\rm long}
defining
\widehat{\tau}_{1}

and
\widehat{\psi}
(cf. Remark
4.3.3
).
Moreover, by our remarks above,

B
is independent of
\epsilon_{\rm redder}

and
\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}
.
Let us note that by our assumptions on existence of
\psi\in\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

postulated in
Proposition
4.5.1
,
we have the (non-quantitative) finiteness statement

{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]<\infty.

(4.76)

Let us note that by Proposition
4.3.1
and
4.3.5
,
there exists a
\gamma=\gamma[\psi]<\infty
, depending in fact only on the value of

{}^{\chi}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]
, such that (
4.69
) holds, independently
of the choice of
L_{\rm long}
defining
\widehat{\tau}_{1}
and
\widehat{\psi}
. Finally, let us set

\epsilon:=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})[\psi].

(4.77)

This determines a sufficiently large
L_{\rm lem}=L_{\rm lem}[\psi]
by the lemma, where the
\psi
-dependence
arises from the
\psi
-dependence of our chosen
\gamma
and
\epsilon
.

We may finally
choose
L_{\rm long}
in the definition of
\widehat{\tau}_{1}
and
\widehat{\psi}

to be precisely
L_{\rm long}[\psi]=L_{\rm lem}
given by the lemma with
B
,
\gamma=\gamma[\psi]
and
\epsilon=\epsilon[\psi]
determined above.

The statement (
4.71
)
of the lemma thus holds for all
\tau_{0}^{*}\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{0}+1]
, and given
moreover the relation
\widehat{\tau}_{1}=\tau_{1}+L_{\rm long}
,. we obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{0}+2}^{\tau_{1}+2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{{\rm bulk}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau^{*}_{0}+1)+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau^{*}_{0}+1-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})+\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})+\epsilon

\displaystyle\inf_{\tau\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau+1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})

\displaystyle\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau^{*}_{0}+1)+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau^{*}_{0}+1-\epsilon_{\rm cutoff})+\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})+\epsilon

for all
\tau\in[\tau_{0}^{*}+1,\tau_{1}]
, with
\epsilon
given by (
4.77
).

Remark 4.5.4
.

We emphasise that (
4.69
) applied with
\gamma
as above
is essentially (
1.55
) of the introduction (averaged over a unit time length).
The assumption is indeed nonquantitative because
\gamma
depends on
the nonquantitative (
4.76
).

It follows by a little bit of averaging (integrating (
4.71
) in
\tau_{0}^{*}\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{0}+1]
) and applying Proposition
4.3.5

and the local existence Proposition
4.2.1
(for
\varepsilon
sufficiently small in the bound (
4.11
), provided that
k_{\rm loc}\leq\overset{\scalebox{.5}{\mbox{\small$\ll$}}}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}
),
that for all
\tau_{0}+2\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}

\displaystyle\inf_{\tau\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau+1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})+\int_{\tau_{0}+2}^{\tau_{1}+2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{{\rm bulk}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{0}+1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau^{*}_{0}+1)d\tau_{0}^{*}+\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})+\epsilon

\displaystyle\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})+\epsilon

\displaystyle\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})

where we have plugged in (
4.77
) in the last inequality.

From the definition (
4.66
) we have thus for all
\tau_{0}+2\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}
,

\inf_{\tau\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau+1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})+\int_{\tau_{0}+2}^{\tau_{1}+2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{{\rm bulk}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]d\tau^{\prime}\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\mathcal{Y}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})+\epsilon_{\rm redder}\int_{\tau_{0}+1}^{\widehat{\tau}_{1}-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}_{r_{1}\leq r\leq r_{2}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]d\tau^{\prime}+\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}\,\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau).

Tracking the dependence of various constants implicit in
\lesssim

on
\epsilon_{\rm redder}
in the propositions we have applied, the
reader can check that
the convention of Section
2.1.5
still applies to both the constant multiplying

\epsilon_{\rm redder}
and the constant multiplying
\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}
above.

It follows that we may apply Proposition
4.3.7
, noting that
the implicit constant in the
\lesssim
in that proposition also is independent of

\epsilon_{\rm redder}
, to absorb the term multiplying
\epsilon_{\rm redder}
, provided we choose

\epsilon_{\rm redder}>0
sufficiently small.
We obtain thus

\inf_{\tau\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau+1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})+\int_{\tau_{0}+2}^{\tau_{1}+2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{{\rm bulk}}(\tau^{\prime})[\widehat{\psi}]d\tau^{\prime}\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\mathcal{Y}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})+\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}\,\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau).

(4.78)

Whence, it follows from (
4.16
) of Proposition
4.3.4

that

\inf_{\tau\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau+1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\mathcal{Y}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})+\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}\,\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau),

for all
\tau_{0}+2\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}
.
But now an additional application of Proposition
4.2.1
yields
that
