\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\mathcal{Y}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})+\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}\,\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau),

whence, since in writing the above we have adhered to the convention of Section
2.1.5
regarding
constants multiplying
\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}
,
we may absorb the last term on the right
hand side for sufficiently small
\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}

to obtain

\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\mathcal{Y}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}).

(4.79)

Revisiting now (
4.78
), we obtain that we may estimate the bulk

\int_{\tau_{0}+2}^{\tau_{1}+2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathfrak{E}}}{}_{\rm bulk}(\tau)d\tau\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\mathcal{Y}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}).

Using now inequality (
4.17
) of Proposition
4.3.4
,
it follows that

\int_{\tau_{0}+2}^{\tau_{1}+2}{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}[\widehat{\psi}]\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\mathcal{Y}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}).

(4.80)

Again, using Proposition
4.2.1
and in view of (
4.79
),
we promote (
4.80
) to the statement

\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}[\psi]\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\mathcal{Y}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})

(4.81)

and similarly for the boundary terms.

From (
4.79
) and (
4.81
) it is now elementary to obtain similar bounds for the additional flux terms in
the definition of
{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
.
We obtain thus

{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\mathcal{Y}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})

which is precisely the desired (
4.37
).
∎

In what follows, we will consider both
\epsilon_{\rm redder}>0
and

\epsilon_{\rm cutoff}>0
to have been fixed to satisfy the smallness necessary for the above proof.

4.6
Estimates for the inhomogeneous terms

In this section we estimate the inhomogeneous terms
contained in
\mathcal{Y}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})

from our basic energies.

4.6.1
Estimates for the linear terms arising from the cutoff

For the linear terms arising from the cutoff, we have:

Proposition 4.6.1
.

The term in line (
4.57
) may be estimated:

\left|\int_{\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}{\rm Re}\left(H^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\overline{F^{\rm cutoff}_{n,{\bf k}}}\right)\right|\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}).

(4.82)

Proof.

From (
4.39
), one sees that the
highest order terms here schematically take the form

\partial P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}^{2}\widehat{\psi})\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\left(\nabla_{g}\chi_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\cdot\nabla_{g}\chi_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\widehat{\psi}\right).

(4.83)

We note that by the disjoint
support properties of
\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}
and

\nabla_{g}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}
and Proposition
3.2.3

it follows that, defining
Q
by

Q\Psi:=\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}((\nabla_{g}\chi_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}})\cdot\Psi),

Q
is
(t^{*},\phi^{*})
-smoothing, which we may view as applied to
\Psi:=\nabla_{g}(\chi_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\widehat{\psi})
.
We may integrate (
4.83
)
in the region
r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{\rm freq}
globally in
t^{*}
and
\phi^{*}
and integrate
by parts two say
\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}

off from the first factor (
4.83
). In view of the factor

\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}
,
there are no boundary terms.
The resulting operator on the second factor

\tilde{Q}\Psi:=\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}((\nabla_{g}\chi_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}})\cdot\Psi)

is again
(t^{*},\phi^{*})
-smoothing by Proposition
3.2.3
and satisfies for instance

\|\tilde{Q}\Psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}\lesssim\left(1+\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\right)\|\Psi\|_{H^{k-3,0}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}.

(4.84)

Upon application of Cauchy–Schwarz,
both arising terms can thus be bounded by

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu3$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})[\widehat{\psi}]\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

(where for the above inequality we have appealed to Proposition
4.3.1
).

The lower order terms in (
4.83
) can of course be estimated similarly.
∎

4.6.2
Nonlinear terms arising from commuting with
\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}

Recall that we have absorbed all linear commutation terms in the bulk,
so all remaining commutation terms are nonlinear.

Proposition 4.6.2
.

The term in line (
4.58
) may be estimated:

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}{\rm Re}\left(H^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\overline{F^{\rm commu}_{n,{\bf k}}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sqrt{\tau_{1}-\tau_{0}}\,\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}.

(4.85)

Proof.

The highest order terms are of the form

\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\partial P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\widehat{\psi})\cdot P_{n}[\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k},\Box_{g(\chi_{\tau_{1}}^{2}\psi_{,}x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\widehat{\psi}.

In view of the presence of
\chi_{\tau_{1}}
we may rewrite this as

\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\partial P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\widehat{\psi})\cdot P_{n}[\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k},\Box_{g(\chi_{\tau_{1}}^{2}\psi_{,}x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}

and thus as

\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\partial P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+3}\widehat{\psi})\cdot P_{n}[\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k},\Box_{g(\chi_{\tau_{1}}^{2}\psi_{,}x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}

(4.86)

\displaystyle+\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+2}\partial P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}+3,\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\widehat{\psi})\cdot P_{n}[\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k},\Box_{g(\chi_{\tau_{1}}^{2}\psi_{,}x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}

(4.87)

\displaystyle+\partial P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}+3,\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\widehat{\psi})\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}+2,\widehat{\tau}_{1}}P_{n}[\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k},\Box_{g(\chi_{\tau_{1}}^{2}\psi_{,}x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}.

(4.88)

For the term (
4.86
),
we apply Cauchy–Schwarz, to estimate by:

\epsilon\|\partial P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+3}^{2}\widehat{\psi})\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}^{2}+\epsilon^{-1}\|P_{n}[\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k},\Box_{g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}^{2}\widehat{\psi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}^{2},

and thus, applying Proposition
3.2.2
, by

\epsilon\|\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+3}^{2}\widehat{\psi})\|_{H^{1}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}^{2}+\epsilon^{-1}\|[\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k},\Box_{g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}^{2}\widehat{\psi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}^{2}.

Now by the Sobolev estimate (
2.33
) of Proposition
2.3.1
, we may estimate

\displaystyle\|[\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k},\Box_{g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}^{2}\widehat{\psi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}+1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(-1\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})

(4.89)

(where we are also using Proposition
4.3.1
),
whereas we may similarly estimate

\|\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+3}^{2}\widehat{\psi})\|_{H^{1}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}^{2}\lesssim(\tau_{1}-\tau_{0})\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime}).

If
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})=0
, then
\psi
vanishes identically and the statement
of our Proposition is trivially true. Otherwise, we choose

\epsilon:=(\tau_{1}-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}
in the above, to obtain
that the term (
4.86
) is
indeed estimated by the right hand side of (
4.85
).

For the term (
4.87
), we notice that the first factor may be rewritten
as

\partial Q\chi_{\tau_{1}+3,\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\widehat{\psi}

where
Q
is a smoothing operator satisfying in particular say

\|Q\Psi\|_{H^{0,1}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}\lesssim\|\Psi\|_{H^{k-2,1}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}
.
Applying Cauchy–Schwarz as before, the above term is
bounded by say

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu3$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}{}^{*}(\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1})[\widehat{\psi}]\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}{}^{*}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0}),

while the second term is bounded as before by (
4.89
).
Here we have used Proposition
4.3.1
.
Choosing say

\epsilon:=\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}
, these two terms
are bounded by the right hand side of (
4.85
).

For the term (
4.88
),
upon integration with respect to
t^{*}
and
\phi^{*}
,
we may integrate by parts two
\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}
from the first to the second factor.
There are no boundary terms in view of the cutoffs.
The second factor becomes

\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}+2,\widehat{\tau}_{1}}P_{n}[\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k},\Box_{g(\chi_{\tau_{1}}^{2}\psi_{,}x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}.

(4.90)

We expand the commutator as

\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}+2,\widehat{\tau}_{1}}P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\Box_{g(\chi_{\tau_{1}}^{2}\psi_{,}x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}})\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}-\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}+2,\widehat{\tau}_{1}}P_{n}(\Box_{g(\chi_{\tau_{1}}^{2}\psi_{,}x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}})\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}

which we may express as

\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}+2,\widehat{\tau}_{1}}P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}S\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}+\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}+2,\widehat{\tau}_{1}}P_{n}\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\tilde{S}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}

where
S
and
\tilde{S}
are differential operators of order two.

Setting

Q:=\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}+2,\widehat{\tau}_{1}}P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1},\qquad\tilde{Q}:=\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}+2,\widehat{\tau}_{1}}P_{n}\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1},

these are both smoothing operators
and we may rewrite the
term as

QS\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}+\tilde{Q}\tilde{S}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}.

Now we note that

\|S\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}\lesssim\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\|\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}\|_{H^{0,2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}

while say

\|\tilde{S}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}\|_{H^{-2,0}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}\lesssim\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\|\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}\|_{H^{k-2,2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}.

For
Q
it is enough to apply

\|Q\Psi\|_{L^{2}((\mathcal{M}\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{\rm fixed})}\lesssim\,\|\Psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{\rm fixed}\})},

while for
\tilde{Q}
we apply

\|\tilde{Q}\Psi\|_{H^{0,0}((\mathcal{M}\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{\rm fixed})}\lesssim\|\Psi\|_{H^{-2,0}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{\rm fixed}\})}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\|QS\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}\|^{2}+\|\tilde{Q}\tilde{S}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\,\|\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}\|^{2}_{H^{k-2,2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}^{*}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}).

We may thus estimate as before (
4.88
) with Cauchy–Schwarz (factored
as described above with (
4.90
) as the second factor) and

\epsilon:=\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}
, estimating now both
terms by the right hand side of (
4.85
).
∎

4.6.3
Terms arising from
P_{n}
: pseudodifferential commutation estimates

Proposition 4.6.3
.

The term in line (
4.59
) may be estimated:

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}{\rm Re}\left(H^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\overline{F^{\rm pseudo}_{n,{\bf k}}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sqrt{\tau_{1}-\tau_{0}}\,\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}.

(4.91)

Proof.

The highest order terms take the form

\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\partial P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}^{2}\widehat{\psi})\cdot[P_{n},\Box_{g}(\psi,x)-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}^{2}\widehat{\psi}

which we may again partition as

\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\partial P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+3}\widehat{\psi})\cdot[P_{n},\Box_{g(\chi_{\tau_{1}}^{2}\psi_{,}x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}

(4.92)

\displaystyle+\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+2}\partial P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}+3,\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\widehat{\psi})\cdot[P_{n},\Box_{g(\chi_{\tau_{1}}^{2}\psi_{,}x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}

(4.93)

\displaystyle+\partial P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}+3,\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\widehat{\psi})\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}+2,\widehat{\tau}_{1}}[P_{n},\Box_{g(\chi_{\tau_{1}}^{2}\psi_{,}x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}.

(4.94)

Let us consider first the term (
4.92
).
By Cauchy–Schwarz, we may bound this by

\epsilon\|\partial P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+3}^{2}\widehat{\psi})\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}^{2}+\epsilon^{-1}\|[P_{n},\Box_{g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}^{2}\widehat{\psi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}^{2}

whence

\epsilon\|\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+3}^{2}\widehat{\psi})\|_{H^{1}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}^{2}+\epsilon^{-1}\|[P_{n},\Box_{g(\psi,x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}^{2}\widehat{\psi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}^{2}.

(4.95)

Now applying Proposition
3.2.5
to the operators
P_{n}
and

(\Box_{g(\psi,x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}})\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\big\|[P_{n},

\displaystyle\Box_{g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\Psi\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\,\left(\sup_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}}\|\chi_{\tau,\tau+2}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\Psi\|_{H^{-1,2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}+\|\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\Psi\|_{H^{-3,2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\,\left(\sup_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}}\|\chi_{\tau,\tau+2}\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\Psi\|_{H^{k-1,2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}+\|\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\Psi\|_{H^{k-3,2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}\right),

(4.96)

where
\Psi=\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}
.

Plugging (
4.96
) into (
4.95
) we obtain

\epsilon(\tau_{1}-\tau_{0})\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})[\psi]+\epsilon^{-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]\cdot{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi],

(4.97)

where we have used also Proposition
4.3.1
.
Now, as before, we remark that
if
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})=0
then
\psi=0
identically and the proposition trivially follows. Otherwise, choose
\epsilon=(\tau_{1}-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}
in (
4.97
)
and we see that both terms are estimated by (
4.91
).

The remaining terms (
4.93
) and (
4.94
)
can be similarly estimated as in Section
4.6.2

by the right hand side of (
4.91
)
and lower order terms may obviously also be estimated similarly.
∎

4.6.4
Terms arising from the semilinearity in
r\leq R_{\rm freq}

Proposition 4.6.4
.

The terms on line (
4.60
) may be estimated:

\left|\int_{\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\}}\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}{\rm Re}\left(H^{{\rm main},n}[\widehat{\psi}_{n,{\bf k}}]\overline{F^{\rm semi}_{n,{\bf k}}}\right)\right|\lesssim\sqrt{\tau_{1}-\tau_{0}}\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}.

Proof.

The highest order terms in the integrand on the left hand side take the form

\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\partial P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}^{2}\widehat{\psi})\cdot P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}N(\partial\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x).

We once again partition as

\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\partial P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+3}\widehat{\psi})\cdot P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}N(\partial\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)

(4.98)

\displaystyle+\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+2}\partial P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}+3,\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\widehat{\psi})\cdot P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}N(\partial\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)

(4.99)

\displaystyle+\partial P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}+3,\widehat{\tau}_{1}}\widehat{\psi})\cdot\tilde{\chi}_{\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}^{4}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}+2,\widehat{\tau}_{1}}P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}N(\partial\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x).

(4.100)

We consider the first term (
4.98
).
We may argue as before
via Cauchy–Schwarz to estimate by:

\epsilon\|P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+3}^{2}\widehat{\psi})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}+\epsilon^{-1}\|P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\widehat{\tau}_{1}}N(\partial\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}

and thus simply by the
L^{2}
mapping properties of
P_{n}
(Proposition
3.2.2
with
s=0=k=j
)

\epsilon\|\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}(\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+3}^{2}\psi)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}+\epsilon^{-1}\|\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}+3}^{2}N(\partial\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathcal{M}\cap\{r\leq R_{\rm freq}\})}

and hence

\epsilon(\tau_{0}-\tau_{1})\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})+\epsilon^{-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\,\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime}),

(4.101)

where we have used the Sobolev inequality of Proposition
2.3.1
.
Again, noting that if
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})=0
then
\psi=0
and the desired
statement is trivially true, the term (
4.98
) is estimated
applying (
4.101
) with
\epsilon=(\tau_{1}-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}
.

The terms (
4.99
) and (
4.100
) are
again estimated as in Section
4.6.2
.

Lower order terms are of course estimated similarly.
∎

.

We first estimate the terms in the subregion

:

.

R_{\rm freq}\leq r\leq R
, the terms on line (

) may be estimated:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{R_{\rm freq}\leq r\leq R\}}|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}^{{\rm total}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]\cdot[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi_{,}x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\psi|+|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}^{{\rm total}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]\cdot\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}N(\partial\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\tau_{1}-\tau_{0}}\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau_{1}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})\sqrt{\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}.

]
. Note that we could also of course estimate in this region by

but there is no gain as we already must include the right hand

)

We now estimate the terms in the subregion

.

It is here of course that the null structure
Assumption
4.1.1
enters:

Proposition 4.6.6
.

Restricted to
r\geq R
, the terms on line (

) may be estimated:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r\geq R\}}|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}^{{\rm total}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]\cdot\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}N(\partial\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}+\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\,.

Proof.

As in

]
, this
follows immediately from the properties of the current
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{H}^{\rm total}
and
from Assumption

.
∎

Clearly now, the terms on line (

)
arising from

,
i.e. we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})\cap\{r_{0}\leq r\leq r_{1}\}}|H^{{\rm redder}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]\cdot[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k},\Box_{g(\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi_{,}x)}-\Box_{g_{a,M}}]\widehat{\psi}|+|H^{{\rm redder}}[\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}\widehat{\psi}]\cdot\mathfrak{D}^{\bf k}N(\partial\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1}}\psi,x)|

4.6.6
An alternative estimate for the nonlinear terms

Finally, we note the following alternative estimate for the non-linear terms:

Proposition 4.6.8
.

Let
\tau_{0}=\tilde{\tau}_{0}\leq\tilde{\tau}_{1}\leq\cdots\leq\tilde{\tau}_{q}=\tau_{1}
be an arbitrary partition
of the interval
[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
such that
\tilde{\tau}_{i}-\tilde{\tau}_{i-1}\geq 2
.
We may replace the right hand side of the estimates
in Propositions
4.6.2
–
4.6.4

with the expression

{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi]}+\sum_{i=0}^{q-1}\sqrt{\tilde{\tau}_{i+1}-\tilde{\tau}_{i}}\,\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tilde{\tau}_{i},\tilde{\tau}_{i+1})[\psi]\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tilde{\tau}_{i},\tilde{\tau}_{i+1})[\psi]}

(4.104)

while we may replace the right hand side of Proposition
4.6.5

and
4.6.7

with the
\sum_{i=0}^{q-1}

of the analogous expression with
(\tilde{\tau}_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{1})
replaced by

(\tilde{\tau}_{i},\tilde{\tau}_{i+1})
.

Proof.

The second statement is clear from the fact that no pseudodifferential operators are applied
and thus the statement arises from a local estimate in physical space. The first statement,
on the other hand follows as before from pseudodifferential commutator estimates,
using now also the dependence of the constant
C
in Proposition
3.2.3

on the distance between the supports of the cutoffs.
∎

We shall only use Proposition
4.6.8

to obtain the statement in the last sentence of Proposition
4.7.1
,
which in turn will only be used to
replace top order slow polynomial growth with uniform boundedness in the statement
of our main theorem.

4.7
Putting it all together: the final estimates

We may now state a closed system of estimates involving our master
energies:

Proposition 4.7.1
.

Fix
0\neq|a|<M
, and let
(\mathcal{M},g_{a,M})
be the sub-extremal Kerr manifold of

and consider equation (

Then for all

sufficiently large, there exist

\displaystyle\qquad+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}+\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sqrt{\tau_{1}-\tau_{0}}\,\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\sqrt{\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})}\,,

\displaystyle{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

Remark 4.7.2
.

By the results of
[
DHRT22
]
, in the case
a=0
, the above Proposition holds as stated where

{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
is
replaced by
{}^{\chi}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
for all

as above.
In fact, by the results of
[
DHRT22
]
,
the Proposition holds as stated
with this substitution for
|a|\leq a_{\rm small}
for some

,
with constants independent of
