in this range.

5
The main theorem: global existence and stability for quasilinear waves on Kerr in the full subextremal range

|a|<M

We may now state the precise version of the main result of this paper.

Theorem 5.1

(Global existence and stability)
.

Fix
|a|<M
, and let
(\mathcal{M},g_{a,M})
be the sub-extremal Kerr manifold of
Section
2.1
,
and consider equation (
4.1
) with
g(\psi,x)
,
N(\partial\psi,\psi,x)

satisfying the assumptions
of Section
4.1
.

There exists a positive integer
k_{\rm global}\geq k_{\rm local}
sufficiently large,
such that, given
k\geq k_{\rm global}
, there exists a positive
0<\varepsilon_{\rm global}<\varepsilon_{\rm loc}
sufficiently small,
and a positive constant
C>0
sufficiently large, so that the following holds.

Fix
\tau_{0}=1

and consider as in Proposition
4.2.1

initial data
(\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime})
on
\Sigma(\tau_{0})
for (
4.1
) satisfying

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}[\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime}]+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}\,[\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime}]\leq\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm global}.

Then the
\psi
given by Proposition
4.2.1

extends to a unique globally defined solution of (
4.1
) in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\infty)
,
satisfying the estimates

{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau)[\psi]+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau)[\psi]\leq C\varepsilon_{0},

(5.1)

for all
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
, in particular

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)[\psi]+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)[\psi]\leq C\varepsilon_{0}.

(5.2)

The solution will satisfy moreover additional power law decay estimates, for instance (
6.33
).

Remark 5.0.1
.

For fixed
M>0
,
choosing the ambient structure of Section
2.1.1

to depend smoothly on
a\in(-M,M)
,
it follows that the constants
\varepsilon_{\rm global}
and
C
above can be chosen uniformly for all
a\in[-a_{0},a_{0}]
for any
0<a_{0}<M
,
i.e. we may write
C=C(a_{0},M)
,
\varepsilon_{\rm global}=\varepsilon_{\rm gobal}(a_{0},M)
.
This will follow because
C
and
\varepsilon_{\rm global}
will ultimately depend on the constants
of Proposition
4.7.1
, and these constants may
be chosen to depend smoothly on
a
for
0<|a|<M
,
while for
|a|\leq a_{\rm small}
we may apply Remark
4.7.2
.
Note, on the other hand, that we have no control over
C(a_{0},M)
as
a_{0}\to M
, even
for the linear wave equation.

6
Proof of global existence and decay via iteration on consecutive spacetime slabs

We will prove in this section Theorem
5.1
,
following a version of the scheme of Section 6
of
[
DHRT22
]
discussed already in Section
1.1.4
of the introduction.

We begin in Section
6.1
by reinterpreting our main estimates of Section
4.7
as a suitable

p
-hierarchy. We then infer a global existence statement on fixed
{\rm L}
-slabs in
Section
6.2
.
In Section
6.3
, we show that by strengthening the initial data assumptions on such
a slab, we may obtain better
estimates at the top of the slab via a pigeonhole argument, leading to an iterating hierarchy.
This allows us in Section
6.4

to deduce global existence, boundedness and decay using an elementary iteration.

In what follows, we recall that Proposition
4.7.1
holds also for the
a=0
case
with the notational substitution of Remark
4.7.2
, and that this substitution may
also be applied in the case
|a|\leq a_{\rm small}(M)
.

6.1
A revised estimate hierarchy

We first reinterpret Proposition
4.7.1
in the form of a
p
-hierarchy of
estimates.

Proposition 6.1.1
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g_{a,M})
be the sub-extremal Kerr manifold of
Section
2.1
for
|a|<M
,
and consider equation (
4.1
) satisfying the assumptions
of Section
4.1
.

Let
k
be sufficiently large.
There exist constants
C>0
implicit in the
\lesssim
notation
and an
\varepsilon_{\rm boot}>0
sufficiently small
such that the following is true.

Consider a region
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

and a
\psi
solving (
4.1
) on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
, satisfying
moreover (
4.11
) and
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{\rm boot}
.
Let us assume moreover that

\tau_{1}=\tau_{0}+{\rm L}

for some arbitrary
{\rm L}\geq 2
.
We have the following hierarchy of inequalities:

\displaystyle{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}+\sqrt{{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{\rm L}\,,

(6.1)

\displaystyle{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{{\rm L}}\,,

(6.2)

\displaystyle{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\left(\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+(\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{1-\delta}{1+\delta}}(\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{2\delta}{1+\delta}}\right)\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+(\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{1-\delta}{1+\delta}}(\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{2\delta}{1+\delta}}}+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{{\rm L}}\,.

(6.3)

Proof.

From Proposition
4.7.1
we immediately deduce the following inequalities:

\displaystyle{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}+\sqrt{{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{{\rm L}}\,,

(6.4)

\displaystyle{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}+\sqrt{{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{{\rm L}},

(6.5)

\displaystyle{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{{\rm L}}\,,

(6.6)

\displaystyle{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{{\rm L}},

(6.7)

\displaystyle{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+\left(\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+(\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{1-\delta}{1+\delta}}(\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{2\delta}{1+\delta}}\right)\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+(\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{1-\delta}{1+\delta}}(\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{2\delta}{1+\delta}}}+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{{\rm L}}\,,

(6.8)

\displaystyle{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})+\left(\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+(\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{1-\delta}{1+\delta}}(\,{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{2\delta}{1+\delta}}\right)\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+(\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{1-\delta}{1+\delta}}(\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}})^{\frac{2\delta}{1+\delta}}}+{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\sqrt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}}\sqrt{{\rm L}},

(6.9)

where we have also applied the interpolation estimate (
2.35
)
to the
p=0
estimates, and we have estimated

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}_{\frac{8R}{9}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\lesssim{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}),{\rm\ etc.}

We now sum pairwise, where we weight the second summand with a sufficiently large multiple to absorb the second term on the right hand side of the first summand.
∎

6.2
Global existence in an
{\rm L}
-slab

It is now elementary to infer a global existence result on
{\rm L}
-slabs.

Proposition 6.2.1
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g_{a,M})
be the sub-extremal Kerr manifold of
Section
2.1
for
|a|<M
,
and consider equation (
4.1
) satisfying the assumptions
of Section
4.1
.

Let
k\geq k_{\rm loc}
be sufficiently large.
Then there exists a positive constant
\varepsilon_{\rm slab}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm loc}
and a constant
C>0
implicit in the sign
\lesssim
below such that the following is true.

Given arbitrary
{\rm L}\geq 2
,

\tau_{0}\geq 0
,
0<\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm slab}
and initial data
(\uppsi,\uppsi^{\prime})

on
\Sigma(\tau_{0})
as in Proposition
4.2.1
, satisfying moreover

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})[\psi]\leq\varepsilon_{0},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})[\psi]\leq\varepsilon_{0}{\rm L}^{-1},

(6.10)

then the unique solution of Proposition
4.2.1

obtaining the data can be extended to
a
\psi

defined on the entire spacetime slab
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{0}+{\rm L})

satisfying the equation (
4.1
)
and
the estimates

{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}[\psi]+{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu5$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}[\psi]\lesssim\varepsilon_{0},\qquad{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}[\psi]+{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu7$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}[\psi]\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\rm L}^{-1}.

(6.11)

Remark 6.2.2
.

We have labelled the highest order energy in the above by
k-4
instead of
k

to be consistent with the way this proposition will be used in the section to follow.

Proof.

It will be convenient to do a continuity argument
in the data
rather than
in time
, i.e. let us replace
the data with
s\uppsi
,
s\uppsi^{\prime}
for
s\in[0,1]
. Let us denote the resulting solution

\psi_{s}
. Note that the statement of the proposition trivially holds for
\psi_{0}
since

\psi_{0}=0
.
Given
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small, let us consider the set
S
consisting of all
s\in[0,1]
such that the
solution
\psi_{s}
exists in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
and satisfies

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)[\psi]\leq\varepsilon,\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)[\psi]\leq\varepsilon{\rm L}^{-1}

(6.12)

for all
\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{1}
.
Note that (
6.12
) in particular implies
assumption (
4.11
).
We have just shown that
0\in S
and thus
S
is nonempty,
and we note that by the Cauchy stability statement of Proposition
4.2.1
,
the set
S
is manifestly closed.
We will show below that the statement of the proposition holds for
\psi_{s}
if
s\in S
,
and that moreover, for
s\in S
, (
6.12
) holds with the improved constant

\varepsilon/2
in place of
\varepsilon
.
It then follows again by the Cauchy stability statement of
Proposition
4.2.1
that the set
S
is open, and thus coincides
with
[0,1]
.

Since (
6.12
) (and thus also (
4.11
)) holds, if
k
is sufficiently large we may
indeed apply (
6.2
) of Proposition
6.1.1

for
k-4
, absorb (using the smallness of
\varepsilon
) the last two terms on the right hand side of (
6.2
) into the first term on the left hand side,
and obtain the first inequality of (
6.11
) for
\psi_{s}
. Then,
we may apply (
6.3
)
for
k-6
and obtain the second inequality of (
6.11
) for
\psi_{s}
,
again using the smallness of
\varepsilon
and the “bootstrap” assumption (
6.12
) to absorb the nonlinear terms.
This yields the statement of the Proposition for
\psi_{s}
, including (
6.11
).
Finally, for sufficiently small
\varepsilon_{\rm slab}\ll\varepsilon
,
then the inequalities (
6.11
)
improve the constant of (
6.12
)
to say
\varepsilon/2
.
We thus indeed obtain openness,
and the Proposition follows as stated.
∎

6.3
The pigeonhole argument

The key to our iterative argument for global existence and decay is the following
pigeonhole argument:

Proposition 6.3.1
.

Fix
\beta>0
such that
\beta+\delta<1
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
6.2.1
,
there exists a constant
C>0
, implicit in the notation
\lesssim
below,
a parameter
\alpha_{0}\geq 3

and, for all
\alpha\geq\alpha_{0}
, a parameter
\hat{\varepsilon}_{\rm slab}(\alpha)

such that for all
0<\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{\rm slab}(\alpha)
the following holds.

Let us assume in addition to (
6.10
) that we have

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}{\rm L}^{\beta},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}{\rm L}^{\beta},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{2}{\rm L}^{-1+\beta},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{4}{\rm L}^{-1+\delta+\beta},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{8}{\rm L}^{-2+\delta+\beta}.

(6.13)

Then the solution
\psi
of (
4.1
)
on
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{0}+{\rm L})
given by Proposition
6.2.1

satisfies the additional estimates

\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{0}+{\rm L}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{\beta}{\rm L}^{\beta},\qquad\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{0}+{\rm L}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{\beta}{\rm L}^{\beta},

(6.14)

{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}{\rm L}^{\beta},\qquad{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu3$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}{\rm L}^{\beta},

(6.15)

{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu3$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{2}{\rm L}^{-1+\beta},\qquad{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu5$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{4}{\rm L}^{-1+\delta+\beta},\qquad{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}+{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu7$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{8}{\rm L}^{-2+\delta+\beta}.

(6.16)

Moreover,
for all times
\tau^{\prime}
with
{\rm L}\geq\tau^{\prime}-\tau_{0}\geq{\rm L}/2
, we have that

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha{\rm L}^{-1+\beta},

(6.17)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{2}{\rm L}^{-1+\delta+\beta},

(6.18)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{4}{\rm L}^{-2+\delta+\beta}.

(6.19)

Proof.

We note first that for
\varepsilon_{\rm slab}(\alpha)
sufficiently small,
then (
6.13
) implies assumption (
6.10
) of
Proposition
6.2.1
.

We thus have that the solution indeed exists in
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{0}+{\rm L})
and satisfies the bounds (
6.11
), whence it follows
that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\varepsilon{\rm L}^{-1}
, while
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\varepsilon
.

Turning to the hierarchy of Proposition
6.1.1
,
it follows from (
6.2
)
that the first inequality of (
6.15
) holds.
It now follows from (
6.3
) that
the first inequality of (
6.16
) holds.
It now follow from (
6.1
) that the
second inequality of (
6.15
) holds.

Applying (
6.2
) now with
k-4
, it now follows
that the second inequality of (
6.16
) holds, while from (
6.3
) applied now with
k-6
,
we obtain the final inequality of (
6.16
).

Note that it now follows in particular that

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$\ll\mkern-6.0muk$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\varepsilon{\rm L}^{-2+\delta+\beta}.

To obtain (
6.17
)–(
6.19
),
we apply the pigeonhole principle to

\int_{\tau_{0}+{\rm L}/4}^{\tau_{0}+{\rm L}/2}\left({\rm L}^{-\beta}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})+{\rm L}^{-\beta}\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})+\alpha^{-4}{\rm L}^{1-\delta-\beta}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})\right)d\tau^{\prime}\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}

which, upon addition, follows from the inequalities of the
estimates (
6.15
)–(
6.16
)
already shown.
Recalling from (
2.25
) that we have

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}\gtrsim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}\gtrsim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}^{\prime}\gtrsim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}},

we obtain
that there exists
\tau^{\prime\prime}\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{0}+{\rm L}/2]
, whose precise value depends on the solution, such that

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}{\rm L}^{-1+\beta},

(6.20)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}{\rm L}^{-1+\beta},

(6.21)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{4}{\rm L}^{-2+\delta+\beta}.

(6.22)

Now in view of the interpolation estimate (
2.34
) of Proposition
2.3.2
,
we have

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\lesssim\left(\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)^{1-\delta}\left(\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)^{\delta}\leq\left(\,\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\right)^{1-\delta}\left(\,\,\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{0}+{\rm L}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\right)^{\delta}

(6.23)

and thus

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}{\rm L}^{-1+\delta+\beta},

(6.24)

where we have used (
6.21
) and
the estimate for the second factor on the right hand side of (
6.23
) contained in the second inequality of (
6.15
).

Now
we apply (
6.2
)
and (
6.3
) again, with
\tau^{\prime\prime}
in place of
\tau_{0}
,
using (
6.20
), (
6.24
) and (
6.22
) to bound the initial data,
to obtain that
for all
\tau_{0}+{\rm L}\geq\tau^{\prime}\geq\tau_{0}+{\rm L}/2
, we have

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}{\rm L}^{-1+\beta},

(6.25)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}{\rm L}^{-1+\delta+\beta},

(6.26)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha{\rm L}^{-2+\delta+\beta}.

(6.27)

Thus, in view of the requirement
\alpha\geq\alpha_{0}\geq 3
, for sufficiently large
\alpha_{0}
we may
absorb the constants implicit in
\lesssim
by explicit constants of our choice by adding extra positive
\alpha

powers to the right hand side of (
6.26
) and (
6.27
).
In this way, we obtain the specific
estimates (
6.17
), (
6.18
) and (
6.19
).
In the same way, we also obtain the specific constant of the estimate
of (
6.14
)
which will be convenient in our scheme.

∎

6.4
The proof of Theorem
5.1
: iteration in consecutive spacetime slabs

It is now elementary to deduce Theorem
5.1
:

We fix
\alpha\geq\alpha_{0}
so that the statement of Proposition
6.3.1
holds,
for instance
\alpha:=\alpha_{0}
.
(Note that we shall no longer track the dependence of constants and parameters on
\alpha
since it is now considered fixed.
Thus implicit constants depending on the choice of
\alpha
will from now on be incorporated in the notations
\sim
and
\lesssim
. The
\alpha
factors in exact inequalities
\leq
will of course still
necessarily be denoted!)

For
i=0,1,\ldots
, we define

\tau_{i}:=\alpha^{i},\qquad{\rm L}_{i}:=\alpha^{i+1}-\alpha^{i}=\alpha^{i}(\alpha-1)\geq 2.

(6.28)

Note that

{\rm L}_{i+1}=\alpha{\rm L}_{i}.

(6.29)

For
0<\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm global}
and
\varepsilon_{\rm global}
sufficiently small,
we have by assumption

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})\leq\varepsilon_{0},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})\leq\varepsilon_{0}.

(6.30)

In particular, for
\varepsilon_{\rm global}
sufficiently small we have

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{i})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}{\rm L}_{i}^{\beta},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{i})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}{\rm L}_{i}^{\beta},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{i})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{2}{\rm L}_{i}^{-1+\beta},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{i})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{4}{\rm L}_{i}^{-1+\delta+\beta},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{i})\leq\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\alpha^{8}{\rm L}_{i}^{-2+\delta+\beta}

(6.31)

for
i=0
(for which
\tau_{0}=1
,
L_{0}=\Xi-1
), and where by the smallness of
\varepsilon_{\rm global}
, these imply also (
6.10
).

Now suppose (
6.31
) hold for some
i\geq 0
where
\tau_{i}
,

{\rm L}_{i}
are defined as in (
6.28
). Note that (
6.10
) then also holds.
Proposition
6.3.1
applies to yield that the solution extends to

\mathcal{R}(\tau_{i},\tau_{i}+{\rm L}_{i})=\mathcal{R}(\tau_{i},\tau_{i+1})
and satisfies

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\rm L}_{i}^{\beta},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}{\rm L}_{i}^{\beta},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}{\rm L}_{i}^{-1+\beta},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}{\rm L}_{i}^{-1+\delta+\beta},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}{\rm L}_{i}^{-2+\delta+\beta}

(6.32)

for all
\tau\in[\tau_{i},\tau_{i+1}]
,
as well as the estimates at
\tau=\tau_{i+1}
with precise constant (
6.14
), (
6.17
)–(
6.19
). Examining the powers of
\alpha
in these latter estimates,
it follows that (
6.31
) holds
for
i+1
, in view of (
6.29
).

It follows by induction that the solution extends to
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\infty)

and satisfies (
6.32
) with
\tau
replacing
{\rm L}_{i}
, for all
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
,
so in particular
we have the higher order inverse power law decay statements

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\tau^{-1+\beta},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu4$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\tau^{-1+\delta+\beta},\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu6$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau)\lesssim\hat{\varepsilon}_{0}\tau^{-2+\delta+\beta}.

(6.33)

For each
\tau_{i}
, we may revisit the estimates (
4.105
)
and (
4.106
) of Proposition
4.7.1

but now in the (non-dyadic!) region
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},\tau_{i})
, both
for
k
and
p=1

and for
k-2
and
p=2-\delta
,
using the alternative right hand side (
4.104
)
referred to in the last sentence of the statement of the proposition, where we
apply (
4.104
) to the partition of
(\tau_{0},\tau_{i})

by
\tau_{j}
for
j=0,\ldots,i
.
Plugging in the bounds already shown, we may now bound the arising terms (
4.104
)
by

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(p)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})
.
Summing (
4.105
)
and (
4.106
)
as before, we deduce

{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{i})[\psi]+{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{i})[\psi]\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(1)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})[\psi],

{}^{\rho}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu2$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{i})[\psi]+{}^{\chi\natural}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(0)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu3$}}}{\mathcal{X}}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{i})[\psi]\lesssim\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny$(2-\delta)$}}}{\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{E}}}(\tau_{0})[\psi]

(and thus the boundedness statements (
5.2
)).

This completes the proof of Theorem
5.1
.

Appendix A
Estimates in Carter frequency space

In this section, we collect all results which are proven using fixed frequency
estimates. Although deferred to the last section of the paper, the constructions here should really be viewed
as the main content of our argument.

We first derive in Section
A.1

some basic properties of the potential
V
arising in Carter’s separation,
in particular deducing those fundamental properties in the superradiant and non-superradiant
regimes that we shall use for our later constructions.
We shall then review in Section
A.2
our separated
current formalism
from
[
DRSR16
]
and provide a translation to the physical space twisted
currents of
[
DHRT22
]
.
Section
A.3
then proves our elliptic refinement (Proposition
3.3.4
).
Section
A.4
proves our global (degenerate) bulk coercivity (Theorem
3.3
).
Finally, Section
A.5
proves our global boundary coervicity (Proposition
3.4.8
).

A.1
Properties of the potential
V
and determination of
the frequency covering
\mathcal{F}_{n}

We review in this section the properties of the potential
V
arising from Carter’s separation
and use these to fix
our covering
\mathcal{F}_{n}
of frequency space.

In Section
A.1.1
, we will decompose the potential
V
into its high frequency
V_{0}
and remainder part
V_{1}
and introduce also the
\gamma
-dependent part
V_{\gamma}
.
In Section
A.1.2

we shall review some basic facts about
V_{0}
from
[
DRSR16
]
valid for
all frequencies.
In Section
A.1.3
we shall deduce some properties special to the (generalised) superradiant regime, while
in Section
A.1.4
we shall deduce properties special to the non-superradiant regime.
(These properties can be viewed as refinements of properties shown in
[
DRSR16
]
.)
Finally, in Section
A.1.5
, on the basis of the above properties,
we shall fix our covering
\mathcal{F}_{n}
of frequency space announced in
Section
3.4.1
.

A.1.1
The high-frequency potential
V_{0}
and the
\gamma
-dependent piece
V_{\gamma}

Recall the potential
V(\omega,m,\ell,r)
defined in (
3.26
) appearing in the radial
ode (
3.25
), where
\Lambda
is given by

\Lambda_{\ell m}^{(a\omega)}:=\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+a^{2}\omega^{2}

and
\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}
represent the eigenvalues of (
3.21
)
parametrised by
\ell\geq|m|
.

In practice, we shall typically think of
\Lambda
as an independent parameter, subject to the inequalities (
3.23
),
(
3.24
), and consider (
3.26
) to define
an expression
V(\omega,m,\Lambda,r)
. Recall that a triple

(\omega,m,\Lambda)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{R}

satisfying (
3.23
) and (
3.24
) is known as an
admissible frequency triple
.

We decompose

V(\omega,m,\Lambda,r)=V_{0}(\omega,m,\Lambda,r)+V_{1}(r)

(A.1)

into the
high frequency potential
given by

V_{0}(\omega,m,\ell,r)=\frac{4Mram\omega-a^{2}m^{2}+\Delta\Lambda}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}

(A.2)

and the
remainder potential
given by

V_{1}(r)=\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{4}}\left(a^{2}\Delta+2Mr(r^{2}-a^{2})\right).

(A.3)

Note that
V_{1}\geq 0
.

Let us decompose
V_{0}
further as

V_{0}=V_{\gamma}+\Delta\Lambda(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-2}

where we define
\gamma=\frac{\omega}{am}
, if
m\neq 0
and
a\neq 0
,

V_{\gamma}=\frac{a^{2}m^{2}(4Mr\gamma-1)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}.

If
m=0
or
a=0
we set
V_{\gamma}=0
.
If
\omega\neq 0
,
m\neq 0
, may rewrite
V_{\gamma}
as

V_{\gamma}=\omega^{2}\gamma^{-1}\frac{(4Mr-\gamma^{-1})}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}.

Note that for
\gamma=(2Mr_{+})^{-1}
,

V_{\gamma}(r_{+})=\omega^{2}(2Mr_{+})^{2}(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2})^{-2}=\omega^{2},

and more generally

V_{\gamma}(r_{+})\leq\omega^{2}

(A.4)

with equality iff
\gamma=(2Mr_{+})^{-1}
.

Remark A.1.1
.

In what follows we recall from Section
3.2.5
the definition of the rescaled coordinate
r^{*}
given by

\frac{dr^{*}}{dr}=\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta},\qquad r^{*}(3M)=0,

and our notation

^{\prime}:=\frac{d}{dr^{*}}.

(A.5)

We note that functions of
r\in(r_{+},\infty)
can be thought of as functions of
r^{*}\in(-\infty,\infty)

and we will implicitly go back and forth between these two parametrisations of the domain
without further comment. Thus we emphasise that in writing
V^{\prime}(r)
, etc.,
we really do mean the derivative defined by (
A.5
). When referring to the
r
-derivative
\frac{d}{dr}
, we shall
always use Leibniz notation. Note that these operators are of course related by

\frac{d}{dr^{*}}=\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\frac{d}{dr}.

A.1.2
The parameters
r_{\rm pot}
and
R_{\rm pot}
and general properties of
V_{0}

In this section we will review some basic properties of
V_{0}
, valid for all admissible frequencies

(\omega,m,\Lambda)
, which can be read off from
the analysis in
[
DRSR16
]
. (We shall infer more refined properties localised to
fixed
\gamma
in the two sections that follow, according to whether
\gamma

is (generalised) superradiant or non-superradiant.)

We have the following proposition which collects various facts about
V_{0}
shown in
[
DRSR16
]
:

Proposition A.1.2

(General properties of
V_{0}

[
DRSR16
]
)
.

Let
|a|<M
.
Let
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
be an admissible frequency triple.
The potential
V_{0}(\omega,m,\Lambda,r)
is either strictly decreasing on
(r_{+},\infty)

or has a unique interior local maximum
r_{\rm max}^{0}(\omega,m,\Lambda)

at which

-\frac{d^{2}}{dr^{2}}V_{0}\gtrsim\Lambda

(A.6)

Moreover, when
r_{\rm max}^{0}
exists we have

r_{\rm max}^{0}(\omega,m,\Lambda)\leq 5M
.

The potential may also have a single interior local minimum at some
r_{+}<r_{\rm min}^{0}<r_{\rm max}^{0}
.
In this case,
V_{0}^{\prime}<0
for
r_{+}<r<r^{0}_{\rm min}
,
V^{\prime}_{0}>0
for
r^{0}_{\rm min}<r<r^{0}_{\rm max}

and
V^{\prime}_{0}<0
for
r>r^{0}_{\rm max}
.

If
r^{0}_{\rm min}(\omega,m,\Lambda)
exists, then
\frac{d}{dr}V_{0}(r_{+})<0
, and

\gamma>(2M\sqrt{M^{2}-a^{2}})^{-1}
and thus
(\omega,m)
is in particular non-superradiant.
In this case, we have

V_{0}(r)-\omega^{2}\gtrsim\omega^{2}

(A.7)

for all
r\in[r_{+},r_{\rm min}]
.

Remark A.1.3
.

We remind the reader that in accordance with our conventions, under the assumptions given above
for which (
A.6
) and (
A.7
) hold,
the constants implicit in
\gtrsim
are
in particular independent of the frequency triple
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
.
This will apply in our subsequent use of
\lesssim
,
\gtrsim
in what follows.

Let us first introduce two parameters
r_{\rm pot}
and
R_{\rm pot}
which will satisfy

r_{2}<r_{\rm pot}<2.01M<5M<R_{\rm pot}<2R_{\rm pot}<R_{\rm freq}

(A.8)

where
R_{\rm freq}
will be determined in Section
A.4.2
.

The parameters
r_{\rm pot}
and
R_{\rm pot}
are directly related to properties
of
V_{0}
and will be further constrained by the propositions in Sections
A.1.3

and
A.1.4

as well as the following statement which may be easily inferred:

Proposition A.1.4
.

Let
|a|<M
.
If
r_{\rm pot}>r_{+}
is sufficiently close to
r_{+}
then
r_{\rm pot}<r^{0}_{\rm max}

for all admissible triples
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
for which
r^{0}_{\rm max}
exists and the one-sided
bound

-V^{\prime}_{0}(r)\lesssim\Delta r^{-2}|m\omega|

(A.9)

holds in
[r_{+},r_{\rm pot}]

for all admissible frequencies
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
, as well as the bound

\left(\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{(r-r_{+})}(V-V(r_{+}))\right)^{\prime}\gtrsim\Delta(\Lambda+1).

(A.10)

If
R_{\rm pot}
is sufficiently large then

-V_{0}^{\prime}\gtrsim\Lambda r^{-3}
,
-(rV_{0})^{\prime}\gtrsim\Lambda r^{-2}

and in fact

-V^{\prime}\gtrsim\Lambda r^{-3},\qquad-(rV)^{\prime}\gtrsim\Lambda r^{-2}+r^{-3},\qquad V\lesssim r^{-1}V^{\prime}

(A.11)

in
r\geq R_{\rm pot}
.

Let us note that the constraint provided by the above proposition
requires us to take
r_{\rm pot}\to r_{+}
as
|a|\to M
. For more discussion of the properties of
V
in the extremal case
|a|=M
see
[
TdC20
,
Gaj23
]
.

A.1.3
Properties of
V_{0}
in the superradiant regime: supperadiant frequencies
are not trapped

In
[
DRSR16
]
, a fundamental role was played by the remark that superradiant frequencies
are never trapped, more precisely, the maximum of
V_{0}
is quantitatively
greater than
\omega^{2}
for all admissible superradiant
frequency triples
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
.

Examination of the proof of
[
DRSR16
]
reveals that something slightly stronger
is true, namely that given
\gamma
,
there is a fixed domain in
r
for which
V_{0}
is quantitatively
greater than
\omega^{2}
for
all
superradiant frequency triples
(\omega,m,\Lambda)

such that
\omega/am=\gamma
. We show this fact here.

We have the following:

Proposition A.1.5

(Common elliptic region for fixed-
\gamma
generalised superradiant frequencies)
.

Let
0\neq|a|<M
.
There exists an open interval

I_{\rm gensuper}:=(-\epsilon,(2Mr_{+})^{-1}+\epsilon),

(A.12)

defined for some sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
, such that for all
r_{\rm pot}>r_{+}

sufficiently close to
r_{+}
and all
R_{\rm pot}
sufficiently large the following holds.

Let
\gamma\in I_{\rm gensuper}
. Then there exists a nonempty interval

[r_{\gamma,1},r_{\gamma,2}]\subset(r_{\rm pot},R_{\rm pot})

and sufficiently small parameters
b_{\gamma}>0
,
b^{\prime}_{\gamma}>0
,
depending continuously on
\gamma
, such that
for all admissible
(\omega,m\neq 0,\Lambda)
with
\omega/am=\gamma
, then

\displaystyle\{r>r_{+}:V_{0}-\omega^{2}<b_{\gamma}\omega^{2},\quad V^{\prime}_{0}>0\}

\displaystyle\subset(r_{+},r_{\gamma,1}),

(A.13)

\displaystyle\{r>r_{+}:V_{0}-\omega^{2}<b_{\gamma}\omega^{2},\quad V^{\prime}_{0}<0\}

\displaystyle\subset(r_{\gamma,2},\infty),

(A.14)

\displaystyle\{r>r_{+}:V_{0}-\omega^{2}\geq b_{\gamma}\omega^{2}\}

\displaystyle\supset[r_{\gamma,1},r_{\gamma,2}],

(A.15)

\displaystyle\{r_{+}<r<2R_{\rm pot}:|V_{0}-\omega^{2}|\leq b_{\gamma}\omega^{2},\quad|V^{\prime}_{0}|\leq b^{\prime}_{\gamma}\Delta r^{-2}\omega^{2}\}

\displaystyle=\emptyset,

(A.16)

as well as the bound

V^{\prime}_{0}\gtrsim\Delta r^{-2}(\Lambda+\omega^{2}){\rm\ in\ }(r_{+},r_{\rm pot}].

(A.17)

For

|\gamma-(2Mr_{+})^{-1}|<\epsilon
, we may choose
r_{\gamma,1}
,
r_{\gamma,2}

independently of
\gamma
.

Moreover, for
|\gamma|<\epsilon
, we
we may choose
r_{\gamma,1}
,
r_{\gamma,2}
independently of
\gamma

satisfying

[r_{\gamma,1},r_{\gamma,2}]\subset(4M,6M)

and the following additional bounds hold:

\displaystyle V^{\prime}_{0}\gtrsim\Delta r^{-2}(\Lambda+\omega^{2})

\displaystyle{\rm\ in\ }(r_{+},2.1M),

(A.18)

\displaystyle V_{0}\gtrsim r^{-2}(\Lambda+\omega^{2})

\displaystyle{\rm\ in\ }[2.01M,\infty),

(A.19)

\displaystyle V_{0}\gtrsim\frac{1}{2}\omega^{2}

\displaystyle{\rm\ in\ }[2.01M,1.2R_{\rm pot}].

(A.20)

Proof.

We first show (
A.15
) and the properties of
[r_{\gamma,1},r_{\gamma,2}]
.

Consider first the case

0<\gamma<\frac{1}{2Mr_{+}}.

Let us note that from (
3.23
) we immediately have

V_{0}\geq\frac{4Mr\gamma^{-1}-\gamma^{-2}+\Delta a^{-2}\gamma^{-2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\omega^{2}.

We estimate
V_{0}-\omega^{2}
at
r_{\gamma}:=\gamma^{-1}/2M
, noting the relation

(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}=\Delta(r^{2}+2Mr+a^{2})+4M^{2}r^{2},

as follows:

\displaystyle V_{0}(r_{\gamma})-\omega^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{-2}+\Delta a^{-2}\gamma^{-2}-(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\omega^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4M^{2}r^{2}+4M^{2}r^{2}\Delta a^{-2}-(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\omega^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4M^{2}r^{2}+4M^{2}r^{2}\Delta a^{-2}-\Delta(r^{2}+2Mr+a^{2})-4M^{2}r^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\omega^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4M^{2}r^{2}a^{-2}-(r^{2}+2Mr+a^{2})}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Delta\omega^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{(M-a)(M+a)}{a^{2}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Delta\omega^{2}.

Now since by (
A.8
) and Proposition
A.1.2
it follows that

V^{\prime}_{0}<0
for
r\geq 5M
, let us define

\hat{r}_{\gamma}:=\min\{5M,r_{\gamma}\}.

We may now choose a suitably small interval
[r_{\gamma,1},r_{\gamma,2}]
containing

\hat{r}_{\gamma}
, depending continuously on
\gamma
, so that (
A.15
) holds
for sufficiently small
b_{\gamma}>0
.

Note that if
0<\gamma<\epsilon
, then
\hat{r}_{\gamma}=5M
. It is clear that
one may in fact choose
[r_{\gamma,1},r_{\gamma,2}]
to be a fixed interval around

5M
for all
|\gamma|<\epsilon
, say contained in
(4M,6M)
,
and (
A.15
) holds for sufficiently small
b_{\gamma}>0
.

It remains only to consider the case
|\gamma-(2Mr_{+})^{-1}|<\epsilon
.
For this, notice that
r_{\gamma}=r_{+}
for
\gamma=\frac{1}{2Mr_{+}}
.
But, for such
\gamma

\frac{d}{dr}V_{0}(r_{+})=(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}(4M\gamma^{-1})>0.

It follows immediately that for sufficiently small
\epsilon
,
and all
|\gamma-(2Mr_{+})^{-1}|<\epsilon
,
there exists an interval
r_{1,\gamma},r_{2,\gamma}

satisfying (
A.15
) for sufficiently small
b_{\gamma}>0
, which may in fact be taken independent of
\gamma
,
with
r_{1,\gamma}>r_{\rm pot}
,
for
r_{\rm pot}>r_{+}
sufficiently close to
r_{+}
.

Let us recall that if
\epsilon>0
is sufficiently small, then by
Proposition
A.1.2
, there is no
r^{0}_{\rm min}
for
V_{0}

for
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
with
\gamma\in I_{\rm gensuper}
.

Finally, note that (
A.13
) and (
A.14
) follow
from (
A.15
) and the uniqueness
of an interior local maximum
V_{0}
.

Note that if (
A.13
)–(
A.15
)
hold for some
b_{\gamma}>0
, they hold for any smaller
b_{\gamma}>0
.
Now, given
b^{\prime}_{\gamma}>0
sufficiently small, then since there is no interior local maximum
such that
V_{0}-\omega^{2}=0
, by our computation at
r_{+}
, and by the behaviour
of
V_{0}
as
\Lambda\to\infty
, it follows by compactness that there exists a
b_{\gamma}

satisfying in addition (
A.16
).

The property (
A.17
) follows easily from direct computation for
\epsilon>0

sufficiently small.

To show (
A.18
), we note that for
\gamma=0
,

V_{\gamma}^{\prime}\gtrsim m^{2}r^{-4}

while

(\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-2})^{\prime}\gtrsim\Delta

in say
(r_{+},2.1M)
. The result follows by continuity for

|\gamma|<\epsilon
for sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
.

Similarly, to show (
A.19
) and (
A.20
), let us first note that for
\gamma=0
, by (
3.23
)
we have

V_{0}\geq\frac{(\Delta-a^{2})m^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}.

The statements (
A.19
) and (
A.20
) for sufficiently small
\epsilon

then follows by continuity in
\gamma
since
\Delta-a^{2}\gtrsim 1
in
r\geq 2.01M
.

∎

A.1.4
Properties of
V_{0}
in the non-superradiant regime

In this section, we turn to the non-superradiant regime.

Assume
0\neq|a|<M
.
Let us fix an open

I_{\rm nonsuper}\subset\mathbb{R}\setminus[0,(2Mr_{+})^{-1}]

(A.21)

with two connected components such that
0
and
(2Mr_{+})^{-1}
are not limit points
of
I_{\rm nonsuper}
and

I_{\rm nonsuper}\cup I_{\rm gensuper}=\mathbb{R},

(A.22)

where
I_{\rm gensuper}
is defined by (
A.12
).

For
\gamma\in I_{\rm nonsuper}
, we define
r^{0}_{\gamma}
as follows:

If

\max\gamma^{-1}\frac{4Mr-\gamma^{-1}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}>1,

(A.23)

then
V_{\gamma}
obtains a maximum at a unique
r
-value.
This is then
r^{0}_{\gamma}
.
Note that

V_{0}(\omega,m,\Lambda)(r^{0}_{\gamma})-\omega^{2}\geq V_{\gamma}(r^{0}_{\gamma})-\omega^{2}\geq b(\gamma)\omega^{2}.

Otherwise,
there is a unique
\Xi^{2}_{\gamma}\in(0,\infty)
such that, for any

\omega\neq 0
,
m\neq 0
with
\omega/am=\gamma
, setting
\Lambda(\omega,\gamma):=\Xi_{\gamma}^{2}\omega^{2}
, then
r^{0}_{\rm max}(\omega,m,\Lambda(\omega,\gamma))
exists and

V_{\rm max}:=V_{0}(r^{0}_{\rm max}(\omega,m,\Lambda))=\omega^{2}.

In this case then, let us denote
r^{0}_{\gamma}:=r^{0}_{\rm max}(\omega,m,\Lambda(\omega,\gamma))
.
Note this is indeed independent of the choice of
\omega,m
such that
\omega/am=\gamma
.
We have that there exists
\epsilon>0

such that for all
\gamma\in I_{\rm nonsuper}
,

R_{\rm pot}-\epsilon>r^{0}_{\gamma}>r_{\rm pot}+\epsilon

provided that
R_{\rm pot}
is sufficiently large and
r_{\rm pot}>r_{+}
is sufficiently
close to
r_{+}
.

Let us note finally that under this definition
r^{0}_{\gamma}
depends continuously
on
\gamma
in
I_{\rm nonsuper}
, and in the case (
A.23
), then setting

\Xi^{2}_{\gamma}:=0
,
\Lambda:=0
, then

r^{0}_{\gamma}=r^{0}_{\rm max}(\omega,m,\Lambda=0)
for any
\omega\neq 0
,
m\neq 0
with
\omega/am=\gamma
. (Note that
(\omega\neq 0,m\neq 0,\Lambda=0)
does not constitute an admissible triple, but we may of course nonetheless plug such a
\Lambda
into the expression (
A.2
).)

Proposition A.1.6

(
\gamma
-dependent localisation of trapping for non-superradiant frequencies)
.

Let
0\neq|a|<M
.
For all

r_{\rm pot}>r_{+}
sufficiently close to
r_{+}
and
R_{\rm pot}
sufficiently large,
the following holds:

Let
\gamma\in I_{\rm nonsuper}
. Given any
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, and defining

r_{\gamma,1}:=r^{0}_{\gamma}-\epsilon,\qquad r_{\gamma,2}:=r^{0}_{\gamma}+\epsilon,

(A.24)

we have

[r_{\gamma,1},r_{\gamma,2}]\subset(r_{\rm pot},R_{\rm pot})

and
there exist sufficiently small parameters
b_{\gamma}>0
,
b^{\prime}_{\gamma}>0
, depending
on
\epsilon

and continuously
on
\gamma
,
such that for all admissible
(\omega\neq 0,m\neq 0,\Lambda)
with
\omega/am=\gamma
, the following
inclusions hold:

\displaystyle\{r>r_{+}:|V_{0}-\omega^{2}|<b_{\gamma}\omega^{2},\quad V^{\prime}_{0}>0\}

\displaystyle\subset(r_{+},r_{\gamma,2}),

(A.25)

\displaystyle\{r>r_{+}:|V_{0}-\omega^{2}|<b_{\gamma}\omega^{2},\quad V^{\prime}_{0}<0\}

\displaystyle\subset(r_{\gamma,1},\infty),

(A.26)

\displaystyle\{r_{+}<r<2R_{\rm pot}:|V_{0}-\omega^{2}|<b_{\gamma}\omega^{2},\quad|V^{\prime}_{0}|<b^{\prime}_{\gamma}\Delta r^{-2}\omega^{2}\}

\displaystyle\subset(r_{\gamma,1},r_{\gamma,2}),

(A.27)

as well as

\omega^{2}+V_{0}\gtrsim\Delta\Lambda\text{\ in\ }\{r_{+}\leq r\leq 1.2R_{\rm pot}\}\cap\{V_{0}\geq\omega^{2}\}.

(A.28)

Moreover, there exist
b_{\infty}>0
,
b^{\prime}_{\infty}>0
sufficiently small
and
\gamma_{\infty}
sufficiently large, and an interval

[r_{\infty,1},r_{\infty,2}]\subset(2.1M,R_{\rm pot})

such that
if
|\gamma|\geq\gamma_{\infty}
or
m=0
, then (
A.25
)–(
A.27
) hold
with
r_{\infty,1}
replacing
r_{\gamma,1}
and
r_{\infty,2}
replacing
r_{\gamma,2}

and with
b_{\infty}
,
b^{\prime}_{\infty}
in place of
b_{\gamma}
,
b^{\prime}_{\gamma}
.

Proof.

Given
\gamma\in I_{\rm nonsuper}
and given

\epsilon>0
and
r_{\gamma,1}
,
r_{\gamma,2}
defined by (
A.24
),
the relations (
A.25
)–(
A.27
)
hold for all admissible

(\omega\neq 0,m\neq 0,\Lambda=\Xi^{2}_{\gamma}\omega^{2})
with
\omega/am=\gamma

as long as
b_{\gamma}
is sufficiently small,
regardless of the size of
b^{\prime}_{\gamma}
, if
\Xi^{2}_{\gamma}\neq 0
. (In the case (
A.23
)
when
\Xi^{2}_{\gamma}=0
,
note that for
b_{\gamma}
and
b^{\prime}_{\gamma}
sufficiently small, then the set on the left hand side of (
A.27
)
is empty, and the inclusions (
A.25
)–(
A.26
) hold.)
On the other hand, for fixed
\omega\neq 0,m\neq 0
with
\omega/am=\gamma
, the set of
\Lambda
for which the above relations all hold for
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
is open,
so they hold for

\alpha\Xi^{2}_{\gamma}\omega^{2}<\Lambda<\alpha^{-1}\Xi^{2}_{\gamma}\omega^{2}

(A.29)

for some
\alpha<1
. Moreover, by homogeneity in
\omega^{2}
, it follows that (
A.25
)–(
A.27
) in fact hold for all admissible
(\omega\neq 0,m\neq 0,\Lambda)

with
\omega/am=\gamma
with
\Lambda
satisfying (
A.29
).

Note that if the inclusions (
A.25
)–(
A.27
)
hold for some
b_{\gamma}
,
b^{\prime}_{\gamma}>0
,
then they trivially continue to hold if we take
b_{\gamma}>0
,
b^{\prime}_{\gamma}>0
smaller.
But clearly if
\Lambda\leq\alpha\Xi^{2}_{\gamma}\omega^{2}
, then, after restriction of
b_{\gamma}
,
the inclusions (
A.25
)–(
A.27
) hold trivially, because
\{r:|V_{0}-\omega^{2}|<b_{\gamma}\omega^{2}\}=\emptyset
.

So it suffices to show that, possibly by restricting
b_{\gamma}
and
b^{\prime}_{\gamma}
further, (
A.25
)–(
A.27
) hold also for

\Lambda\geq\alpha^{-1}\Xi^{2}_{\gamma}\omega^{2}.

(A.30)

Now, recall from (
A.7
),
that for all admissible
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
, for
b_{\gamma}>0

sufficiently small,
V_{\rm min}\leq(1-2b_{\gamma})\omega^{2}
.
We claim that for
b_{\gamma}>0
sufficiently small, we have that for all
admissible
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
satisfying also (
A.30
),
the following holds:

V_{\rm max}(\omega,m,\Lambda)\geq(1+3b_{\gamma})\omega^{2}.

(A.31)

This follows because

V_{\rm max}(\omega,m,\Lambda)-V_{\rm max}(\omega,m,\Xi^{2}_{\gamma}\omega^{2})\geq(\Lambda-\Xi^{2}_{\gamma}\omega^{2})h

(A.32)

where
h=\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-2}(r_{\rm max}^{0}(\omega,m,\Lambda=\Xi^{2}_{\gamma}\omega^{2}))\geq b
, since
r_{\rm max}^{0}(\omega,m,\Lambda=\Xi^{2}_{\gamma}\omega^{2})

lies in
(r_{\rm pot},R_{\rm pot})
.

On the other hand, given
b_{\gamma}>0
, we
may then choose
b^{\prime}_{\gamma}
sufficiently small (depending on
b_{\gamma}
), such that
for all admissible
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
with
\omega/am=\gamma
, we have

\{r_{+}<r<2R_{\rm pot}:|V^{\prime}_{0}|<b^{\prime}_{\gamma}\Delta r^{-2}\omega^{2}\}\subset\{|V_{0}-V^{0}_{\rm max}|\leq b_{\gamma}\omega^{2}\}\cup\{|V_{0}-V^{0}_{\rm min}|\leq b_{\gamma}\omega^{2}\}.

(A.33)

We see (
A.33
) as follows:
Let us write
\Lambda=\beta\omega^{2}
and write, for
\omega/am=\gamma
,

V_{0}(\omega,m,\Lambda=\beta\omega^{2},r)=\mathcal{V}_{\beta,\gamma}(r)\omega^{2}

\frac{d}{dr}V_{0}=\frac{d}{dr}\mathcal{V}_{\beta,\gamma}(r)\omega^{2}.

Note that
\mathcal{V}_{\beta,\gamma}(r)
satisfies
\frac{d}{dr}\mathcal{V}_{\beta,\gamma}(r_{+})\neq 0
and its critical points and values
coincide with those of
\omega^{2}V_{0}(\omega,m,\Lambda=\beta\omega^{2},r)
.
For fixed
\beta\geq\gamma^{-1}
(admissibility), and more generally for
\beta\in X
for any compact subset
X\subset[\gamma^{-1},\infty)
, it follows by compactness that given
\hat{b}>0
,
then if
\hat{b}^{\prime}>0
is sufficiently small, depending on
\hat{b}
,
\gamma
and
X
, then

\left\{r_{+}<r<2R_{\rm pot}:\left|\frac{d}{dr}\mathcal{V}_{\beta,\gamma}\right|<\hat{b}^{\prime}\right\}\subset\{|\mathcal{V}_{\beta,\gamma}-\mathcal{V}^{\rm max}_{\beta,\gamma}|\leq\hat{c}\}\cup\{|\mathcal{V}_{\beta,\gamma}-\mathcal{V}^{\rm min}_{\beta,\gamma}|\leq\hat{c}\}.

(A.34)

But we notice that given
\hat{b}>0
, for
\hat{b}^{\prime}>0
sufficiently small, (
A.34
) also holds for
all
\beta\gg\alpha^{-1}\Xi^{2}_{\gamma}
. To see this note that there is a unique
r_{\rm tr}\in[r_{+},2R_{\rm pot}]

such that

\frac{d}{dr}\left[\Delta(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-2}\right](r_{\rm tr})=0,

and the left hand side of (
A.34
)
is contained in the set
\{r:|r-r_{tr}|\leq C(\gamma)\beta^{-1}\}
for some
C(\gamma)
. On the other hand, we claim
that on
\{r:|r-r_{tr}|\leq C(\gamma)\beta^{-1}\}
we have

|\mathcal{V}_{\beta,\gamma}-\mathcal{V}^{\rm max}_{\beta,\gamma}|\leq C(\gamma)\beta^{-1}\cdot\beta^{2}=C(\gamma)\beta^{-1}
. Thus, given any
\hat{b}
we indeed have (
A.34
) also for

\beta\gg\alpha^{-1}\Xi^{2}_{\gamma}
for all
\hat{b}^{\prime}>0
sufficiently small (depending on
\hat{b}

and
\gamma
).
Having established that (
A.34
) for all
\beta\geq\gamma^{-1}
, we note
that (
A.33
) follows for all admissible
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
with
\omega/am=\gamma
by re-expressing in terms of
V_{0}
and expressing
\frac{d}{dr}

in terms of
{}^{\prime}=\frac{d}{dr^{*}}
.

It follows that given
b_{\gamma}>0

sufficiently small, we may choose
b^{\prime}_{\gamma}>0
sufficiently small so that
the left hand side of (
A.27
) is empty for all admissible
(\omega,m,\Lambda)

with
\omega/am=\gamma
and
\Lambda
satisfying (
A.30
).

Thus, in view of our comments above (
A.27
) indeed
holds for all admissible
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
with
\omega/am=\gamma
, provided
that
b_{\gamma}>0
and
b^{\prime}_{\gamma}>0
are chosen sufficiently small.

To show the remaining inclusions (
A.25
)–(
A.26
) in the case (
A.30
),
let us first note that given
b_{\gamma}>0
,
there exists a
0<\epsilon_{0}<\epsilon
, depending on
b_{\gamma}
, such that
for all admissible
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
with
\omega/am=\gamma
and

\Lambda\geq\alpha^{-1}\Xi^{2}_{\gamma}\omega^{2}
, we have:

V_{0}>(1+b_{\gamma})\omega^{2}\text{\ on\ }(r^{0}_{\gamma}-\epsilon_{0},r^{0}_{\gamma}+\epsilon_{0}).

(A.35)

(This follows from similar considerations to those leading to (
A.32
).)

Recall now that
V_{0}(r_{+})<\omega^{2}
for all admissible frequencies with
\gamma\in I_{\rm nonsuper}
.
Appealing to the bound of
\gamma
away from
\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}
in the definition of
I_{\rm nonsuper}
,
let us require
b_{\gamma}>0

to be sufficiently small such that say
V_{0}(r_{+})<(1-3b_{\gamma})\omega^{2}

for all admissible frequencies with

\gamma\in I_{\rm nonsuper}
.
Since
V_{0}(r)\to\infty
as
r\to\infty
, it follows from
Proposition
A.1.2
and (
A.31
),
that for all admissible
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
with
\omega/am=\gamma
and

\Lambda
satisfying (
A.30
), given
x\in(c,c)
, there exist unique
r
-values
r_{\uparrow}(\omega,m,\Lambda,x)<r_{\downarrow}(\omega,m,\Lambda,x)
such that

\{r>r_{+}:V_{0}(\omega,m,\Lambda)=(1+x)\omega^{2}\}=\{r_{\uparrow},r_{\downarrow}\}.

Moreover, it follows from (
A.35
) that
r_{\uparrow}\leq r^{0}_{\gamma}+\epsilon_{0}
,
r_{\downarrow}\geq r^{0}_{\gamma}-\epsilon_{0}
and

V_{0}^{\prime}(\omega,m,\Lambda,r_{\uparrow})>0,\qquad V_{0}^{\prime}(\omega,m,\Lambda,r_{\downarrow})<0.

The relations (
A.25
)–(
A.26
) now follow immediately upon varying
x
.

The bound (
A.28
) follows immediately from the form of the expression (
A.2
) defining
V_{0}

(where we use that
m^{2}\lesssim\omega^{2}
, noting that this depends on the fact that
0

is not a limit point of
I_{\rm nonsuper}
).

The final statement of the proposition concerning
\gamma_{\infty}
or the case
m=0
follows easily.
∎

Remark A.1.7
.

Note that the frequencies for which
\gamma
satisfies (
A.23
)
are in fact never trapped,
and could also have been merged with the generalised superradiant frequencies
in our treatment later.

A.1.5
Determining the frequency space covering
\mathcal{F}_{n}

In what follows, let us fix
2.01M>r_{\rm pot}>r_{+}
,
R_{\rm pot}>5M
satisfying all the constraints of the previous
section.

Recall the intervals
I_{\rm gensuper}
,
I_{\rm nonsuper}
defined by (
A.12
), (
A.21
) and satisfying (
A.22
).
The following proposition now determines covering intervals
I_{n}
that in
turn determine the
(\omega,m)
frequency-space covering
\mathcal{F}_{n}

of Section
3.4.1
.

Proposition A.1.8
.

Let
0\neq|a|<M
.
There exists a finite covering of
\mathbb{R}

\mathbb{R}=\cup_{n=1}^{N}I_{n},

by connected open intervals
I_{n}\subset\mathbb{R}
,
n=1,\ldots,N
,
a collection

\alpha_{n}\in\mathbb{R},

a collection of nonempty intervals

[r_{n,1},r_{n,2}]\subset(r_{\rm pot},R_{\rm pot}),

(A.36)

and parameters
b_{\rm pot}>0
,
b^{\prime}_{\rm pot}>0

such that
the following properties hold:

•

0\in I_{1}
,
(2Mr_{+})^{-1}\in I_{2}
, the collection

I_{3},\ldots,I_{N_{s}}
are contained in
I_{\rm strictsuper}:=(\epsilon/2,(2Mr_{+})^{-1}-\epsilon/2)
,
and

\cup_{n=1}^{N_{s}}I_{n}=I_{\rm gensuper}.

•

For all
n=1,\ldots,N_{s}
, all
\gamma\in I_{n}
and
all admissible
(\omega,m\neq 0,\Lambda)
with
\omega/am=\gamma
, we have

\displaystyle\{r>r_{+}:V_{0}-\omega^{2}<b_{\rm pot}\omega^{2},\quad V^{\prime}_{0}>0\}

\displaystyle\subset(r_{+},r_{n,1}),

(A.37)

\displaystyle\{r>r_{+}:V_{0}-\omega^{2}<b_{\rm pot}\omega^{2},\quad V^{\prime}_{0}<0\}

\displaystyle\subset(r_{n,2},\infty),

(A.38)

\displaystyle\{r>r_{+}:V_{0}-\omega^{2}\geq b_{\rm pot}\omega^{2}\}

\displaystyle\supset[r_{n,1},r_{n,2}],

(A.39)

\displaystyle\{r_{+}<r<2R_{\rm pot}:|V_{0}-\omega^{2}|\leq b_{\rm pot}\omega^{2},\quad|V^{\prime}_{0}|\leq b^{\prime}_{\rm pot}\Delta r^{-2}\omega^{2}\}

\displaystyle=\emptyset.

(A.40)

We set
\alpha_{n}=\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}
. We have moreover

\displaystyle V^{\prime}_{0}\gtrsim\Delta r^{-2}(\Lambda+\omega^{2})

\displaystyle{\rm\ in\ }(r_{+},r_{\rm pot}].

(A.41)

If
n\neq 1
, we have

(\omega^{2}+V_{0})\gtrsim\Delta\Lambda\text{\ in\ }\{r_{+}\leq r\leq 1.2R_{\rm pot}\}\cap\{V_{0}\geq\omega^{2}\}.

(A.42)

•

In the case of
n=1
, we have the additional statements

[r_{1,1},r_{2,1}]\subset(4M,6M)
,

\displaystyle V^{\prime}_{0}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\Delta r^{-2}(\Lambda+\omega^{2})\}

\displaystyle{\rm\ in\ }(r_{+},2.1M],

(A.43)

\displaystyle V_{0}

\displaystyle\gtrsim r^{-2}(\Lambda+\omega^{2})

\displaystyle{\rm\ in\ }[2.01M,\infty),

(A.44)

\displaystyle V_{0}

\displaystyle>\frac{1}{2}\omega^{2}

\displaystyle{\rm\ in\ }[2.01M,1.2R_{\rm pot}].

(A.45)

•

For all
n=1,\ldots,N_{s}
, all

\gamma\in I_{n}
and all admissible
(\omega,m\neq 0,\Lambda)
with,

\omega/am=\gamma
, we have

\chi_{\natural}(r,\omega,m,\Lambda)=1,

(A.46)

provided that the parameter

in (
3.28
) is
sufficiently small.

•

The collection

satisfies

\cup_{n=N_{s}+1}^{N-1}I_{n}=I_{\rm nonsuper}.

•

For all
n\in N_{s}+1,\ldots,N
, all
\gamma\in I_{n}
and all
admissible
(\omega,m\neq 0,\Lambda)
with
\omega/am=\gamma

\displaystyle\{r>r_{+}:|V_{0}-\omega^{2}|<b_{\rm pot}\omega^{2},\quad V^{\prime}_{0}>0\}

\displaystyle\subset(r_{+},r_{n,2}),

(A.47)

\displaystyle\{r>r_{+}:|V_{0}-\omega^{2}|<b_{\rm pot}\omega^{2},\quad V^{\prime}_{0}<0\}

\displaystyle\subset(r_{n,1},\infty),

(A.48)

\displaystyle\{r_{+}<r<2R_{\rm pot}:|V_{0}-\omega^{2}|<b_{\rm pot}\omega^{2},\quad|V^{\prime}_{0}|<b^{\prime}_{\rm pot}\Delta r^{-2}\omega^{2}\}

\displaystyle\subset(r_{n,1},r_{n,2}).

(A.49)

We have moreover (
A.42
).
For
n=N-1
, then
-\infty
is a limit point of
I_{n}

and (

)–(
A.49
) hold also
for

,
a\omega<0
, while for

then
\infty
is a limit point of

and (
A.47
)–(
A.49
) hold also
for
m=0
,
a\omega>0
.
Defining the spacetime region
\mathcal{L}_{n}=\{r_{n,1}<r<r_{n,2}\}
, then
there exists an
\alpha_{n}

satisfying

0\leq\alpha_{n}\leq\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}

such that

g(T+\alpha_{n}\Omega_{1},T+\alpha_{n}\Omega_{1})\leq b<0

(A.50)

in
\mathcal{L}_{n}
. For
n=N-1
and

, we may in fact take

\alpha_{n}=0
.

•

For all
n=N_{s}+1,\ldots,N
, all
\gamma\in I_{n}
and all

with
\omega/am=\gamma
, we have

\chi_{\natural}(r,\omega,m,\Lambda)\gtrsim 1

(A.51)

in
\{r_{+}<r\leq r_{n,1}\}\cup\{r\geq r_{n,2}\}
(where the constant implicit in

is in particular independent of frequency),
and similarly for
n=N-1
if
m=0
,
a\omega<0
and for
n=N
if
m=0
,
a\omega>0
,
provided that the parameter
b_{\rm trap}>0
in (
3.28
) is
sufficiently small.

Proof.

This follows easily from Propositions
A.1.5

and
A.1.6
and compactness considerations.
To ensure properties (
A.46
) and (

)
we must require

sufficiently small in (
