).
∎

Remark A.1.9
.

We note that the we could in fact combine the frequency ranges
I_{N-1}
and
I_{N}
,
(and thus
\mathcal{F}_{N-1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{N}
) as our currents to be defined
in Section
A.2

will in fact be identical in these two ranges). We have kept them apart
simply so that the
I_{n}
are all connected intervals.

A.2
Fixed frequency currents of
[
DRSR16
,
DRSR18
]

and their relation to twisted physical
space currents of
[
DHRT22
]

Let
u
be a solution of the inhomogeneous ode

u^{\prime\prime}+(\omega^{2}-V)u=G

(A.52)

where
V
is the potential defined by (
A.2
).

We recall the fixed Carter frequency currents of
[
DRSR16
,
DRSR18
]
:

\displaystyle{\text{Q}}^{f}[u]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega^{2}-V)|u|^{2})+f^{\prime}{\rm Re}(u^{\prime}\bar{u})-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime}|u|^{2},

\displaystyle{\text{Q}}^{h}[u]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle hRe(u^{\prime}\bar{u})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime}|u|^{2},

\displaystyle{\text{Q}}^{y}[u]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle y(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega^{2}-V)|u|^{2}),

\displaystyle{\text{Q}}^{\hat{z}}[u]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{z}|u^{\prime}-i\omega u|^{2}-\hat{z}V|u|^{2},

\displaystyle{\text{Q}}^{z}_{\rm red}[u]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle z|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m)u|^{2}-z(V-V(r_{+}))|u|^{2},

\displaystyle{\text{Q}}^{T}[u]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega{\rm Im}(u^{\prime}\bar{u}),

\displaystyle{\text{Q}}^{\Omega_{1}}[u]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-m{\rm Im}(u^{\prime}\bar{u}).

Here
f(r)
,
h(r)
,
y(r)
,
\hat{z}(r)
and
z(r)
are functions.

From (
A.52
) we have the identities

\displaystyle({\text{Q}}^{f}[u])^{\prime}=

\displaystyle 2f^{\prime}|u^{\prime}|^{2}-fV^{\prime}|u|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}|u|^{2}

\displaystyle+{\rm Re}(2f\bar{G}u^{\prime}+f^{\prime}\bar{G}u),

(A.53)

\displaystyle({\text{Q}}^{h}[u])^{\prime}=

\displaystyle h(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(V-\omega^{2})|u|^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}|u|^{2}

\displaystyle+h{\rm Re}(u\bar{G}),

(A.54)

\displaystyle({\text{Q}}^{y}[u])^{\prime}=

\displaystyle y^{\prime}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega^{2}-V)|u|^{2})-yV^{\prime}|u|^{2}

\displaystyle+2y{\rm Re}(u^{\prime}\bar{G}),

(A.55)

\displaystyle({\text{Q}}^{\hat{z}}[u])^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\hat{z}^{\prime}|u-i\omega u|^{2}-(\hat{z}V)^{\prime}|u|^{2}

\displaystyle+2\hat{z}{\rm Re}\left(G\overline{u^{\prime}-i\omega u}\right),

(A.56)

\displaystyle({\text{Q}}^{z}_{\rm red}[u])^{\prime}=

\displaystyle z^{\prime}|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m)u|^{2}

(A.57)

\displaystyle-(z(V-V(r_{+})))^{\prime}|u|^{2}

\displaystyle+2z{\rm Re}\left(G\overline{u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m)u}\right),

\displaystyle({\text{Q}}^{T}[u])^{\prime}=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle+\omega{\rm Im}(G\bar{u}),

(A.58)

\displaystyle({\text{Q}}^{\Omega_{1}}[u])^{\prime}=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle-{\rm Im}(G\bar{u}),

(A.59)

\displaystyle({\text{Q}}^{Z}[u])^{\prime}=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle+\omega{\rm Im}(G\bar{u})

(A.60)

\displaystyle-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m{\rm Im}(G\bar{u}),

\displaystyle({\text{Q}}^{T}[u]+\qquad

(A.61)

\displaystyle\chi_{{\rm Killing},n}\alpha_{n}{\text{Q}}^{\Omega_{1}}[u])^{\prime}=

\displaystyle-\chi^{\prime}_{{\rm Killing},n}\alpha_{n}m{\rm Im}(u^{\prime}\bar{u})

\displaystyle+(\omega-m\alpha_{n}\chi_{{\rm Killing},n}){\rm Im}(G\bar{u}),

\displaystyle({\text{Q}}^{T}[u]+\qquad

\displaystyle\frac{\chi_{{\rm Killing},n}(2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}{(1-2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}{\text{Q}}^{Z}[u])^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\frac{\chi^{\prime}_{{\rm Killing},n}(2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}{(1-2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m){\rm Im}(u^{\prime}\bar{u})

\displaystyle+\omega{\rm Im}(G\bar{u})

(A.62)

\displaystyle+\frac{\chi_{{\rm Killing},n}(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m)}{(1-2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}{\rm Im}(G\bar{u}),

where we have replaced some letters from the original
[
DRSR16
]
so as not to conflict with the notation of the present
paper.

Let us define the “bulk” quantities
\mathfrak{P}^{f}[u]
by the first column on the right
hand side of (
A.53
)–(
A.62
), i.e.

\mathfrak{P}^{f}[u]:=2f^{\prime}|u^{\prime}|^{2}-fV^{\prime}|u|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}|u|^{2},

etc.

The above identities translate to physical space identities as follows:
If
f
,
h
,
y
,
\hat{z}
,
z
are independent of frequency, then
the above identities (
A.53
)–(
A.62
)
all correspond to the divergence identity of physical space currents.
We will denote these “corresponding currents” as

\tilde{J}^{f}[\psi],\quad\tilde{J}^{h}[\psi],\quad\tilde{J}^{y}[\psi],\quad\tilde{J}^{\hat{z}}[\psi],\quad\tilde{J}^{z}_{\rm red}[\psi],\quad-\tilde{J}^{T}[\psi],\quad-\tilde{J}^{\Omega_{1}}[\psi],\quad-\tilde{J}^{Z}[\psi],

(A.63)

and these satisfy divergence identities of the form

\nabla^{\mu}\tilde{J}^{f}_{\mu}[\psi]=\tilde{K}^{f}[\psi]+{\rm Re}(\tilde{H}^{f}[\psi]\overline{\Box_{g}\psi}),

(A.64)

etc.
(The unusual choice of sign in
-\tilde{J}^{T}[\psi]
,
-\tilde{J}^{\Omega_{1}}[\psi]

-\tilde{J}^{Z}[\psi]

is to retain consistency with
[
DRSR16
]
.)

The above currents (
A.63
) correspond to twisted currents as described in Appendix A.2 of
[
DHRT22
]
,
with twisting function
\beta:=(r_{BL}^{2}+a^{2})^{-1/2}
.
Recall the twisted derivative operator

\tilde{\nabla}_{\mu}\psi:=\beta\nabla_{\mu}(\beta^{-1}\psi)

(A.65)

and the twisted energy momentum tensor:

\tilde{T}_{\mu\nu}[\psi]:={\rm Re}(\tilde{\nabla}_{\mu}\psi\overline{\tilde{\nabla}_{\nu}\psi})-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}\left({\rm Re}(\tilde{\nabla}^{\alpha}\psi\overline{\tilde{\nabla}_{\alpha}\psi})+(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)^{-1}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}\Delta^{-1}V_{1}|\psi|^{2}\right)

(A.66)

where
V_{1}
is defined in (
A.3
).

The current

\tilde{J}^{f}_{\mu}[\psi]:=\tilde{T}_{\mu\nu}[\psi]X^{\nu}+f^{\prime}{\rm Re}(\psi\overline{\tilde{\nabla}_{\mu}\psi})-\frac{1}{2}|\psi|^{2}\nabla_{\mu}f^{\prime}

where
X=2f(r^{*})\partial_{r^{*}}
. (It thus corresponds to what was referred to as

\tilde{J}^{X}_{\mu}+\tilde{J}_{\mu}^{aux,f^{\prime}}
in
[
DHRT22
]
.)
We then have

\tilde{J}^{y}_{\mu}[\psi]:=\tilde{T}_{\mu\nu}[\psi]X^{\nu},

(A.67)

where
X=2y(r^{*})\partial_{r^{*}}
,

\tilde{J}^{h}_{\mu}[\psi]=h{\rm Re}(\psi\overline{\tilde{\nabla}_{\mu}\psi})-\frac{1}{2}|\psi|^{2}\nabla_{\mu}h

(the above corresponding to what was referred to as
\tilde{J}^{aux,h}
in
[
DHRT22
]
),

\tilde{J}^{\hat{z}}_{\mu}[\psi]:=\tilde{T}_{\mu\nu}[\psi]X^{\nu},\qquad

where
X=2\hat{z}(r^{*})(\partial_{r^{*}}+\partial_{t^{*}})

{\tilde{J}^{z}_{\rm red}}_{\,\mu}[\psi]:=\tilde{T}_{\mu\nu}[\psi]X^{\nu},\qquad

(A.68)

where
X=2z(r^{*})(\partial_{r^{*}}-\partial_{t^{*}}-\frac{a}{Mr_{+}}\partial_{\phi^{*}})
,

\tilde{J}^{T}_{\mu}[\psi]:=\tilde{T}_{\mu\nu}[\psi]T^{\nu},

(A.69)

\tilde{J}^{\Omega_{1}}_{\mu}[\psi]:=\tilde{T}_{\mu\nu}[\psi](\Omega_{1})^{\nu}.

(A.70)

Let us note that if
f(r)
,
y(r)
are smooth functions in
r\in[r_{+},\infty)
, then
X=2f(r^{*})\partial_{r_{*}}

or
X=2y(r^{*})\partial_{r^{*}}

extend to smooth vector fields beyond the horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
to the full region

r\geq r_{0}
,
while if
\Delta z(r)
is smooth in
r\in[r_{+},\infty)
, then a similar statement holds concerning

X=2z(r^{*})(\partial_{r^{*}}-\partial_{t^{*}}-\frac{a}{Mr_{+}}\partial_{\phi^{*}})
.
Given currents defined with such
f
,
y
,
z
in
r\geq r_{+}
, we may define
arbitrary smooth extensions to
r\geq r_{0}
.

Remark A.2.1
.

Since we shall restrict to
r_{0}
close to
r_{+}
,
then Propositions
3.4.6

and
3.4.14
of Section
3.4

will hold
independently of the choice of the smooth extensions to the region
r_{0}\leq r\leq r_{+}
. Consequently, in the sections to follow
we shall only make explicit choices in the region
r\geq r_{+}
.

The bulk currents
\tilde{K}^{f}[\psi]
,
\tilde{K}^{y}[\psi]
, etc., in (
A.64
)
can then be read off from the bulk currents
\mathfrak{P}^{f}[u]
,
\mathfrak{P}^{y}[u]
of the first
column of the right hand side (
A.53
)–(
A.62
)
by making the following replacements:

\displaystyle|u|^{2}

\displaystyle\mapsto|\psi|^{2}

(A.71)

\displaystyle|u^{\prime}|^{2}

\displaystyle\mapsto|\tilde{\nabla}_{r^{*}}\psi|^{2}

(A.72)

\displaystyle\omega m|u|^{2}

\displaystyle\mapsto{\rm Re}(\tilde{\nabla}_{t}\psi\overline{\tilde{\nabla}_{\phi}\psi})

(A.73)

\displaystyle m^{2}|u|^{2}

\displaystyle\mapsto|\tilde{\nabla}_{\phi}\psi|^{2}

(A.74)

\displaystyle\Lambda|u|^{2}

\displaystyle\mapsto|\tilde{\nabla}_{\theta}\psi|^{2}+\sin^{-2}\theta|\tilde{\nabla}_{\phi}\psi|^{2}+a^{2}(\cos^{2}\theta+1)|\tilde{\nabla}_{t}\psi|^{2}

(A.75)

\displaystyle m{\rm Im}(u^{\prime}\bar{u})

\displaystyle\mapsto{\rm Re}(\tilde{\nabla}_{\phi}\psi\overline{\tilde{\nabla}_{r^{*}}\psi})

(A.76)

and multiplying everything by

\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)\Delta}.

For example, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{K}^{f}[\psi]

\displaystyle=\Biggl(2f^{\prime}|\tilde{\nabla}_{r^{*}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-f\left(\left(\frac{4Mra}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)^{\prime}{\rm Re}(\tilde{\nabla}_{t}\psi\overline{\tilde{\nabla}_{\phi}\psi})-\left(\frac{a^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)^{\prime}|\tilde{\nabla}_{\phi}\psi|^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\qquad\left.+\left(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{\prime}(|\tilde{\nabla}_{\theta}\psi|^{2}+\sin^{-2}\theta|\tilde{\nabla}_{\phi}\psi|^{2}+a^{2}(\cos^{2}\theta+1)|\tilde{\nabla}_{t}\psi|^{2})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-fV_{1}^{\prime}|\psi|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}|\psi|^{2}\Biggl)\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)\Delta}.

Finally, we note that by Plancherel relations of Section
3.2.6
,
we have the following:
If
u(r,\omega,m,\ell):=(r^{2}+a^{2})^{1/2}\mathfrak{C}\circ\mathfrak{F}_{RL}[\Psi]
,
then

\int_{-\infty}^{R_{\rm freq}^{*}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{m,\ell}\mathfrak{P}^{f}[u](r,\omega,m,\ell)d\omega dr^{*}=\int_{r_{+}\leq r\leq R_{\rm freq}}\tilde{K}^{f}[\Psi],

(A.77)

etc.

A.3
The elliptic refinement: Proof of Proposition
3.3.4

In this section, we prove Proposition
3.3.4
.
Recall the parameters
b_{\rm elliiptic}
and
\gamma_{\rm elliptic}
from Section
3.3.2
,
and the definition (
3.30
) of
r_{\rm elliptic}
.

The crux of the proof is the following fixed frequency estimate:

Proposition A.3.1
.

Let
|a|<M
,
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
be admissible frequencies and let

u
satisfy (
A.52
). Given
b_{\rm elliptic}>0
and
\gamma_{\rm elliptic}>0
, then we have the estimate

\int_{r^{*}_{\rm elliptic}(\omega,m)}^{(1.2R_{\rm pot})^{*}}\Delta r^{-2}\iota_{V_{0}-\omega^{2}\geq b_{\rm elliptic}\omega^{2}}(V_{0}-\omega^{2})^{2}|u|^{2}\lesssim\int_{(\max\{.99r_{\rm elliptic},r_{+}\})^{*}}^{(1.3R_{\rm pot})^{*}}\Delta r^{-2}\iota_{V_{0}-\omega^{2}\geq b_{\rm elliptic}\omega^{2}/2}\left((\omega^{2}+\Lambda)|u|^{2}+|G|^{2}\right),

(A.78)

where the constant implicit in
\lesssim
depends on the choice of
b_{\rm elliptic}
and

\gamma_{\rm elliptic}
.

Proof.

Since our implicit constants are independent of frequency,
we may assume without loss of generality
\omega\neq 0
.

Let
\xi(s)
be function such that
\xi(s)=s
if
s\geq b_{\rm elliptic}\omega^{2}
,
\xi(s)=0
for
s\leq b_{\rm elliptic}\omega^{2}/2
and

0\leq\frac{d}{ds}\xi\leq 4
,
0\leq\frac{d^{2}}{ds^{2}}\xi\leq 8\omega^{-2}
, and
\xi(s)\leq s
for
s\geq 0
.
If
r_{\rm elliptic}=r_{+}
, let
\chi_{\rm pot}
be a cutoff function which
=1
for
r\leq 1.2R_{\rm pot}

and
=0
for
r\geq 1.3R_{\rm pot}
.
If
r_{\rm elliiptic}=2.03M
then let
\chi_{\rm pot}
be a cutoff function which
=1

in
2.03M\leq r\leq 1.2R_{\rm pot}
and
0
if
r\leq.99r_{\rm elliptic}
or
r\geq 1.3R_{\rm pot}
.

We will consider the current
{\text{Q}}^{h}[u]
of Section
A.2
, choosing:

h:=\xi(V_{0}-\omega^{2})\Delta r^{-2}\chi_{{\rm pot}}(r).

We compute

h^{\prime}=\frac{d}{ds}\xi(V_{0}-\omega^{2})V_{0}^{\prime}\Delta r^{-2}\chi_{\rm pot}(r)+\xi(V_{0}-\omega^{2})\left[\Delta r^{-2}\chi_{{\rm pot}}\right]^{\prime}

and

h^{\prime\prime}=\frac{d^{2}}{ds^{2}}\xi(V_{0}-\omega^{2})(V_{0}^{\prime})^{2}\Delta r^{-2}\chi_{\rm pot}(r)+\frac{d}{ds}\xi(V_{0}-\omega^{2})V_{0}^{\prime\prime}\Delta r^{-2}\chi_{\rm pot}(r)+\xi(V_{0}-\omega^{2})\left[\Delta r^{-2}\chi_{{\rm pot}}\right]^{\prime\prime}.

We note that

|h^{\prime}|\lesssim|V_{0}^{\prime}|\Delta r^{-2}\lesssim(\Lambda+\omega^{2})\Delta^{2}r^{-4}\iota_{\max\{.99r_{\rm elliptic},r_{+}\}\leq r\leq 1.3R_{\rm pot}}

while

\displaystyle|h^{\prime\prime}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\frac{d^{2}}{ds^{2}}\xi(V_{0}-\omega^{2})\right|(\Lambda^{2}+\omega^{4})\Delta^{3}r^{-10}\chi_{{\rm pot}}(r)+(\Lambda+\omega^{2})r^{-8}\Delta^{2}\chi_{\rm pot}+\omega^{2}\Delta r^{-4}\iota_{\max\{.99r_{\rm elliptic},r_{+}\}\leq r\leq 1.3R_{\rm pot}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\omega^{-2}\iota_{\omega^{2}/2\leq(V_{0}-\omega^{2})\leq\omega^{2}}(\Lambda^{2}+\omega^{4})\Delta^{3}r^{-10}\chi_{{\rm pot}}(r)

(A.79)

\displaystyle\qquad+(\Lambda+\omega^{2})r^{-8}\Delta^{2}\chi_{\rm pot}+\omega^{2}\Delta r^{-4}\iota_{\max\{.99r_{\rm elliptic},r_{+}\}\leq r\leq 1.3R_{\rm pot}}.

Now, we claim that for all admissible frequencies
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
for which
\omega\neq 0
,

\iota_{\omega^{2}/2\leq(V_{0}-\omega^{2})\leq\omega^{2}}\omega^{-2}\Lambda^{2}\Delta^{3}r^{-10}\chi_{{\rm pot}}(r)\lesssim\Delta^{2}\Lambda r^{-6}\chi_{\rm pot}.

(A.80)

To see this, note that if
a=0
or
m=0
or
|\omega/am|\geq\gamma_{\rm elliptic}
or
\gamma:=\omega/am\in I_{n}
for
n\neq 1
, then

\Delta\Lambda r^{-4}\lesssim V_{0}+r^{-2}\omega^{2}

and thus

\iota_{\omega^{2}/2\leq(V_{0}-\omega^{2})\leq\omega^{2}}\Delta\Lambda r^{-4}\lesssim\omega^{2},

whence (
A.80
) indeed follows.
On the other hand, if
|\omega/am|<\gamma_{\rm elliptic}
and
n=1
,
then, since
\chi_{\rm pot}
is supported in
r\geq 2.01M
, we have
by (
A.44
)

\chi_{\rm pot}(r)\Delta\Lambda r^{-4}\lesssim\chi_{\rm pot}(r)(V_{0}+r^{-2}m^{2})\lesssim\chi_{\rm pot}(r)V_{0}

and thus

\iota_{\omega^{2}/2\leq(V_{0}-\omega^{2})\leq\omega^{2}}\chi_{\rm pot}(r)\Delta\Lambda r^{-4}\lesssim\iota_{\omega^{2}/2\leq(V_{0}-\omega^{2})\leq\omega^{2}}\chi_{\rm pot}(r)V_{0}\lesssim\chi_{\rm pot}(r)\omega^{2},

whence (
A.80
) again follows.

Integrating (
A.54
), and using the above bounds for
|h^{\prime}|
and
|h^{\prime\prime}|

(where we apply (
A.80
)
to bound the first term on the right hand side of (
A.79
))
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Delta r^{-2}\chi_{\rm pot}\xi(V_{0}-\omega^{2})(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(V_{0}-\omega^{2})|u|^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\int_{\{V\geq b_{\rm elliptic}\omega^{2}/2\}\cap\{r\leq 1.3R_{\rm pot}\}}\Delta r^{-4}(\Lambda+\omega^{2})|u|^{2}+\Delta r^{-2}\chi_{\rm pot}\xi(V_{0}-\omega^{2})|u\bar{G}|,

and thus, since
\xi^{2}(V_{0}-\Omega)\leq\xi(V_{0}-\Omega)\cdot(V_{0}-\Omega)
,
we may absorb the
u
from the
|u\bar{G}|
into the left hand side to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Delta r^{-2}\chi_{\rm pot}\xi(V_{0}-\omega^{2})(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(V_{0}-\omega^{2})|u|^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\int_{\{V_{0}\geq b_{\rm elliptic}\omega^{2}/2\}\cap\{r\leq 1.3R_{\rm pot}\}}\Delta r^{-4}(\Lambda+\omega^{2}+1)|u|^{2}+\Delta r^{-2}\chi_{\rm pot}|\bar{G}|^{2}.

Now the left hand side manifestly controls

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Delta r^{-2}\chi_{\rm pot}\iota(V_{0}-\omega^{2})(V_{0}-\omega^{2})|u|^{2}

whence (
A.78
) follows immediately.
∎

Let
\psi
now be as in the statement of Proposition
3.3.4
,
and apply the above to

u:=\mathfrak{C}\circ\mathfrak{F}_{BL}[\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\psi],

for all
|{\bf k}|\leq k
.
We may partition the term
G=G_{1}+G_{2}
where
G_{1}
arises from the right hand side
F
and

G_{2}
arises from the cutoff.

Summing over
{\bf k}
,
Proposition
3.3.4
then follows immediately from Proposition
A.3.1
, the Plancherel formulae of Section
3.2.6
,
the definitions of
{}^{\rm elp}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
and
{}^{\natural}\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k$}}}{\mathcal{X}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

and the fact that

\iota_{V_{0}-\omega^{2}\geq b_{\rm elliptic}\omega^{2}/2}\lesssim\chi_{\natural},

(A.81)

provided that
b_{\rm trap}>0
in (
3.28
) is taken sufficiently small,
depending on
b_{\rm elliptic}

(where we recall the definition of
\chi_{\natural}
from Section
3.3.1
).

Let us now fix
\gamma_{\rm elliptic}>0
such that

[-\gamma_{\rm elliptic},\gamma_{\rm elliptic}]\subset I_{1}\setminus\cup_{n\neq 1}I_{n}

(A.82)

where

I_{1}
is the interval of Proposition
A.1.8
.
(This thus determines
r_{\rm elliptic}(\omega,m)
of (
3.30
).)

Remark A.3.2
.

In view of the above proof, we may now also quickly explain how to
immediately obtain Proposition
3.3.2
from the result stated in
[
DRSR16
]
.
It suffices to obtain a fixed frequency estimate for all

(\omega,m,\Lambda)\in\mathcal{G}_{\natural}
where the degeneration function is
replaced by
\chi_{\natural}
and with error term controlled by the quantity actually
estimated in
[
DRSR16
]
, which
at fixed frequency gives an estimate that degenerates at the maximum
r_{\rm max}(\omega,m,\Lambda)

of the full potential
V(\omega,m,\Lambda)
.
One may assume without loss of generality that
\omega^{2}_{\rm large}\gg 1

in the definition of
\mathcal{G}_{\natural}
.
Let us note that for
\omega^{2}_{\rm large}\gg 1
, then
there is an
\epsilon>0
independent of frequency in the
range
\mathcal{G}_{\natural}
such that

r^{0}_{\rm max}-\max\{r^{0}_{\rm min},r_{+}\}>2\epsilon
and
|r_{\rm max}-r^{0}_{\rm max}|<\epsilon/2
,
while
|\hat{V}_{\rm max}-V_{\rm max}|<\epsilon\omega^{2}
. (Hhere
\hat{V}_{\rm max}
denotes the
maximum value of the full potential
V
. Recall that in the notation of the present paper,

V_{\rm max}
denotes the maximum value of
V_{0}
.)
We note then that if
V_{\rm max}\geq(1+b_{\rm trap})\omega^{2}
, we remove the
degeneration at
r_{\rm max}
by
applying the estimate (
A.54
)
arising from
h=\xi\Delta r^{-2}\chi_{r^{0}_{\rm max}}
(where we replace
b_{\rm elliptic}
with
b_{\rm trap}

in the definition of
\xi
) and where
\chi_{r^{0}_{\rm max}}
equals one in an
\epsilon
neighbourhood
of
r^{0}_{\rm max}
and
0
outside a
2\epsilon
neighbourhood.
On the other hand,
if
V_{\rm max}\leq(1-b_{\rm trap})\omega^{2}
, we remove the
degeneration at
r_{\rm max}

by applying the estimate (
A.55
) to

y=(r-r^{0}_{\rm max})\chi_{r^{0}_{\rm max}}
.
Note that where
\chi_{r^{0}_{\rm max}}=1
all terms in the bulk are good modulo lower
order terms because
we also have there
-V_{0}^{\prime}(r-r^{0}_{\rm max})\geq 0
, in view of the properties
of
V_{0}
and the fact that by our choice
of
\epsilon
we have there
r\geq r^{0}_{\rm min}
.
All error terms are absorbed by the
fact that we have already nondegenerate control from the precise result stated
in
[
DRSR16
]
for all quantities in the
(\epsilon,2\epsilon)

r
-annulus around
r^{0}_{\rm max}
.

Let us also note the following helpful inequality for later:

\iota_{r\leq r_{1}}\iota_{\rm elliptic}(V_{0}+\omega^{2})|u|^{2}\lesssim\Delta(\Lambda+\omega^{2}+m^{2})|u|^{2}

(A.83)

independent of frequency. The prefactor
\Delta
on the right hand side arises in front of all frequencies, not just
\Lambda
, because
of (
A.4
).

A.4
The global bulk coercivity: Proof of Theorem
3.3

In this section, we prove
Theorem
3.3
.
Recall from the statement of the theorem
that we are given
E>0
arbitrary and
e_{\rm red}>0
sufficiently small.
(The smallness constraints on
e_{\rm red}>0
will appear in the course of the proof.)

In the non-superradiant regime,
our currents will take the form

\displaystyle J^{{\rm main},n}[\psi]

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\tilde{J}^{y_{n}}[\psi]+\tilde{J}^{f_{n}}[\psi]+\tilde{J}^{f_{\rm fixed}}[\psi]+\tilde{J}^{y_{\rm fixed}}[\psi]+\tilde{J}^{\hat{z}}[\psi]+e_{\rm red}\tilde{J}^{z}_{\rm red}[\psi]

(A.84)

\displaystyle\qquad+E\left(\tilde{J}^{T}[\psi]+\chi_{{\rm Killing},n}\frac{(2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}{(1-2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}\tilde{J}^{Z}[\psi])\right)

while in the superradiant regime
our currents will take the form

\displaystyle J^{{\rm main},n}[\psi]

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\tilde{J}^{y_{n}}[\psi]+\tilde{J}^{f_{n}}[\psi]+\tilde{J}^{f_{\rm fixed}}[\psi]+\tilde{J}^{y_{\rm fixed}}[\psi]+\tilde{J}^{\hat{z}}[\psi]+e_{\rm red}\tilde{J}^{z}_{\rm red}[\psi]

(A.85)

\displaystyle\qquad+E(\tilde{J}^{T}[\psi]+\chi_{{\rm Killing},n}\alpha_{n}\tilde{J}^{\Omega_{1}}[\psi]).

Here,

\alpha_{n}
is the parameter (
3.43
),
and the functions
f_{n}
,
y_{n}
,
\hat{z}
,
z
,

\chi_{n,{\rm Killing}}
are to be described below.

The functions will have the property that for
r\geq R_{\rm freq}
, where
R_{\rm freq}

is a paremeter that will depend on
E
,
we will have
\chi_{n,{\rm Killing}}=0
,
f_{n}=0
and

y_{n}(r^{*})=B_{\rm indep}

(A.86)

for some constant
B_{\rm indep}>0
(to be chosen later) independent of
n
indexing
the frequency range. (In fact, (
A.86
) will hold for all
R\geq 1.2R_{\rm pot}
.)
We will have
z(r^{*})=0
for
r\geq r_{2}
.
Thus, in particular,
for
r\geq R_{\rm freq}
,
we have that

J^{{\rm main},n}[\psi]=J^{\rm main}[\psi]

is
independent of
n
.

We will explain the choice of our
n
-independent functions in
Section
A.4.1
and prove coercivity in certain regions.
We will then treat the non-superradiant ranges first in
Section
A.4.2

followed by the generalised
superradiant ranges in Section
A.4.3
.
We will fix all remaining
parameters in our multiplier constructions in Section
A.4.4
and
finally, we will complete the proof in Section
A.4.5
.

A.4.1
The choice of
f_{\rm fixed}
,
y_{\rm fixed}
,
\hat{z}
and
z

The functions
f_{\rm fixed}
,
y_{\rm fixed}
,
\hat{z}
and
z
will be independent of
n
. They
are chosen as follows.

We define
f_{\rm fixed}(r)
so that

f_{\rm fixed}=0\text{\ for\ }r\leq R_{\rm pot},\quad f^{\prime}_{\rm fixed}\geq 0\text{\ for\ }r\geq R_{\rm pot},\quad f\geq 1,f^{\prime}_{\rm fixed}=b_{\rm tail}r^{-2},\,f^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\rm fixed}=6b_{\rm tail}r^{-4}\text{\ for\ }r\geq 1.1R_{\rm pot},

(A.87)

and we define
y_{\rm fixed}
by

y_{\rm fixed}=0\text{\ for\ }r\leq R_{\rm pot},\quad y_{\rm fixed}^{\prime}\geq 0\text{\ for\ }r\geq R_{\rm pot},\quad y_{\rm fixed}^{\prime}=b_{\rm tail}r^{-3}\text{\ for\ }r\geq 1.1R_{\rm pot}

(A.88)

where
b_{\rm tail}>0
is a small parameter which will be fixed immediately
below in Proposition
A.4.1
.

We define
\hat{z}
, depending on
R_{\rm freq}
, so that

\displaystyle\hat{z}=0\text{\ for\ }r\leq R_{\rm pot},\,\,\hat{z}^{\prime}\geq 0\text{\ for\ }r\geq R_{\rm pot},\,\,\hat{z}^{\prime}=0\text{\ for\ }r\geq R_{\rm freq},

(A.89)

\displaystyle\hat{z}=r\text{\ for\ }R_{\rm pot}\leq r\leq.9R_{\rm freq},

(A.90)

\displaystyle 0\leq\hat{z}^{\prime}\leq r^{\prime}\text{\ for\ }9R_{\rm pot}\leq r\leq R_{\rm freq}.

(A.91)

Finally, we define

z
so that

{z}=0\text{\ for\ }r\geq r_{2},\qquad{z}=-\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})}{r-r_{+}}\text{\ for\ }r\leq r_{1}.

(A.92)

Note that by our comments in Section
A.2
, the above indeed
define currents which smoothly extend to the horizon
r=r_{+}
.

We have

Proposition A.4.1

(Coercivity properties of the
n
-independent elements of the current)
.

Given
R_{\rm freq}\geq 2R_{\rm pot}
, let
f_{\rm fixed}
,
y_{\rm fixed}
,
\hat{z}
and
z
be defined as above
with a fixed
b_{\rm tail}>0
sufficiently small.
Then, in the region
1.1R_{\rm pot}\leq r\leq.9R_{\rm freq}
we have

\mathfrak{P}^{f_{\rm fixed}}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{y_{\rm fixed}}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{\hat{z}}[u]\gtrsim r^{-2}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\omega^{2}|u|^{2}+\Lambda|u|^{2})+|u^{\prime}-i\omega u|^{2}+r^{-3}|u|^{2}

(A.93)

while for
r\geq.9R_{\rm freq}
we have

\mathfrak{P}^{f_{\rm fixed}}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{y_{\rm fixed}}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{\hat{z}}[u]\gtrsim r^{-2}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+r^{-1}\omega^{2}|u|^{2}+r^{-1}\Lambda|u|^{2}+r^{-2}|u|^{2}).

(A.94)

The inequalities remain true when further restricted to the region
r\geq 1.2R_{\rm pot}
upon the addition of
\mathfrak{P}^{y_{n}}[u]
to the right hand
side for all values of
B_{\rm indep}\geq 0
in (
A.86
) (recall this too is independent of
n
).

Proof.

This follows immediately from the formulae of Section
A.2
, where
we have used (
A.11
). (Note that one controls lower order error terms because (
A.11
) refers to
V
and not
V_{0}
.)
∎

Let us note already that Proposition
A.4.1
translates into bulk coercivity statements
for
{\tilde{K}}^{y_{\rm fixed}}+{\tilde{K}}^{f_{\rm fixed}}+{\tilde{K}}^{\hat{z}}+{\tilde{K}}^{y_{n}}

and thus in particular gives the proof of Proposition
3.4.15
.

Let us also note:

Proposition A.4.2

(Coercivity properties of the redshift current)
.

In
r_{+}\leq r\leq r_{1}
we have,

\mathfrak{P}^{z}_{\rm red}[u]\gtrsim\Delta^{-1}|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m)u|^{2}+\Delta(\Lambda|u|^{2}+|u|^{2}).

(A.95)

Proof.

This follows immediately from (
A.57
) and the definition (
A.92
)
in view of (
A.10
) and the constraint
r_{1}<r_{\rm pot}
.
∎

We define
\chi_{\rm red}
a cutoff such that
\chi_{\rm red}=1
for
r\leq r_{2}

and
\chi_{\rm red}=0
for
r\geq r_{1}
.

A.4.2
The non-superradiant ranges

Given a nonsuperradiant frequency range indexed by
n
,
let us recall the degeneration function
\chi_{n}
defined in Section
3.4.3
,
which vanishes in
[r_{n,1},r_{n,2}]
, and satisfies

\{\chi_{n}\neq 1\}\subset(r^{\prime}_{n,1},r^{\prime}_{n,2})
,
where
[r_{n,1},r_{n,2}]\subset(r^{\prime}_{n,1},r^{\prime}_{n,2})\subset(r_{\rm pot},R_{\rm pot})
.
Recall also
the auxiliary
\tilde{\chi}_{n}
, which also
vanishes in
[r_{n,1},r_{n,2}]
but which equals
1
in the support of
\chi_{n}
.
Recall finally the
n
-independent functions

f_{\rm fixed}
,
y_{\rm fixed}
,
\hat{z}
,
z
defined in Section
A.4.1
.

The crux of the proof for non-superradiant frequency ranges
\mathcal{F}_{n}

will be the following fixed frequency statement.

Proposition A.4.3

(Non-superradiant degenerate bulk coercivity)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{n}
be a nonsuperradiant range.
Let
B_{\rm indep}>0
be sufficiently
large. Let the parameter

b_{\rm elliptic}>0
from (
3.31
) be sufficiently small (with smallness requirement depending on
B_{\rm indep}
).
Then there exist functions
y_{n}(r)
,
f_{n}(r)

with
y_{n}(r)=B_{\rm indep}
for
r\geq 1.1R_{\rm pot}
,
and
f_{n}(r)=0
for
r\geq R_{\rm pot}
, and given arbitrary

E>0
, a function
\chi_{n,{\rm Killing}}(r)
satisfying

\chi_{n,{\rm Killing}}(r)=0
for
r\geq R_{\rm freq}

such that the following holds for all sufficiently small
e_{\rm red}\geq 0
:
For all admissible frequencies
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
such that

(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
, we have in the region

r_{+}\leq r\leq R_{\rm freq}
the coercivity bound

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}^{y_{n}}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{f_{n}}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{f_{\rm fixed}}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{y_{\rm fixed}}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{\hat{z}}[u]+e_{\rm red}\mathfrak{P}^{z}_{\rm red}[u]

\displaystyle-E\,\mathfrak{P}^{T}[u]-E\,\frac{(2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}{(1-2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}\mathfrak{P}^{\chi_{n,{\rm Killing}}Z}[u]

\displaystyle\geq c\Delta r^{-4}\chi_{n}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+r^{-1}\omega^{2}|u|^{2}+r^{-1}\Lambda|u|^{2})+ce_{\rm red}\chi_{\rm red}\Delta^{-1}|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m)u|^{2}

\displaystyle-C\Delta r^{-6}\tilde{\chi}_{n}|u|^{2}-C\Delta\iota_{\rm elliptic}(V_{0}+\omega^{2})|u|^{2}.

(A.96)

Moreover, for
\epsilon_{\rm red}>0
sufficiently small, there exists
an
r_{\rm close}(e_{\rm red})>r_{+}
such that
in the region
r_{+}\leq r\leq r_{\rm close}
, inequality (
A.96
)
in fact holds without the final term on the right hand side.

Remark A.4.4
.

It is specifically Proposition
A.4.3

which constrains
R_{\rm freq}
to be large depending on
E
.
We emphasise that, according to our conventions, the constants
c
,
C
on the right hand side
of (
A.96
) will potentially depend on
our yet to be chosen parameters
b_{\rm elliptic}
,
B_{\rm indep}
and

E
(though they will in fact not depend on
e_{\rm red}
).

Proof.

We will first prove (
A.96
)
in the case
e_{\rm red}=0
. The statement for small
enough positive
e_{\rm red}>0
then follows immediately from
Proposition
A.4.2
, given that we may absorb all negative terms
in
\mathfrak{P}^{z}_{\rm red}
away from the horizon into the terms already controlled.
Note that indeed, we may remove the last term from inequality (
A.96
)
in some region

r_{+}\leq r\leq r_{\rm close}(e_{\rm red})
, since in this region
we have
\Delta\iota_{\rm elliptic}(V_{0}+\omega^{2})\lesssim\Delta^{2}(\Lambda^{2}+\omega^{2}+m^{2})
by (
A.83
).

In what follows thus, we consider only the case
e_{\rm red}=0
.

Recall the degeneration function
\chi_{n}
defined in Section
3.4.3
.
For all
B_{\rm indep}
sufficiently large, we define
y_{n}(r^{*})
to be a function such
that
y_{n}(r)
smoothly extends to
r=r_{+}
and such that the following are satisfied:

\displaystyle y_{n}=0\quad\text{in\ }\{\chi_{n}=0\},

(A.97)

\displaystyle y^{\prime}_{n}\geq\chi_{n}\Delta/r^{2}+B_{\rm large}|y_{n}|\Delta/r^{2}\quad\text{for all\ }r\in[r_{+},1.1R_{\rm pot}],

(A.98)

\displaystyle y_{n}^{\prime}\geq 0\quad\text{for all\ }r\geq 1.1R_{\rm pot},\qquad y_{n}^{\prime}=0\quad\text{for all\ }r\geq 1.2R_{\rm pot},

(A.99)

\displaystyle y_{n}=B_{\rm indep}\text{\ for all\ }r\geq 1.2R_{\rm pot},\qquad B_{\rm indep}>0\text{\ constant independent of\ }n,

(A.100)

\displaystyle 0\leq y_{n}^{\prime}\leq C(B_{\rm large},B_{\rm indep})\chi_{n}\quad\text{for all\ }r\in[r_{+},1.1R_{\rm pot}],

(A.101)

where
B_{\rm large}\gg 1
is to be determined below (depending only on
B_{\rm indep}
and
b_{\rm elliptic}
).
We define
f_{n}(r^{*})
to be

f_{n}=0{\rm\ in\ }\{\chi_{n}=0\},\qquad f_{n}^{\prime}=\chi_{n}^{\frac{1}{2}}\text{\ in\ }\{\chi_{n}\leq b_{\rm small}\},\qquad f_{n}=0\text{\ in\ }\{\chi_{n}\geq 2b_{\rm small}\},

(A.102)

where
b_{\rm small}>0
is a sufficiently small parameter which we immediately fix so that the support of
f

is completely contained in the enlarged region
(r^{\prime}_{n,1},r^{\prime}_{n,2})
.
Note that if
\chi_{n}
vanishes to sufficiently high order, then
|f_{n}|\gtrsim\chi_{n}

in
\{\chi_{n}\leq b_{\rm small}\}
.

The function
y_{n}
will moreover be independent of the choice of
B_{\rm indep}
in the region
r_{+}\leq r\leq 1.1R_{\rm pot}
. It is the necessity of taking
B_{\rm large}
in (
A.98
)
which will constrain
B_{\rm indep}

to be sufficiently large, in view of the condition that
y^{\prime}_{n}\geq 0
. We note that our assumptions
imply in particular that
y_{n}\leq B_{\rm indep}
.

Thus, let us remark already that by the above definition, the

|u^{\prime}|^{2}
term on the right hand side of (
A.96
)
is manifestly controlled in the region
r_{+}\leq r\leq 1.1R_{\rm pot}
by

\mathfrak{P}^{y_{n}}[u]
and in the region
r\geq 1.1R_{\rm pot}
by

\mathfrak{P}^{f_{\rm fixed}}[u]
.
(In the former region, the only competing term comes from
\mathfrak{P}^{f_{n}}[u]

in the region
\{b_{\rm small}\leq\chi_{n}\leq 2b_{\rm small}\}
. If
B_{\rm large}

is sufficiently large, this can be absorbed in the term coming from
\mathfrak{P}^{y_{n}}[u]
.)

Note that requiring
\chi^{\prime}_{{\rm Killing},n}\leq 0
, we have

\displaystyle-\mathfrak{P}^{T}[u]-\frac{(2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}{(1-2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}\mathfrak{P}^{\chi_{n,{\rm Killing}}Z}[u]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\chi^{\prime}_{{\rm Killing},n}(2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}{(1-2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m){\rm Im}(u^{\prime}\bar{u})

(A.104)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\chi^{\prime}_{{\rm Killing},n}(2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}{(1-2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m){\rm Im}((u^{\prime}-i\omega u)\bar{u})

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\chi^{\prime}_{{\rm Killing},n}(2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}{(1-2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m){\rm Im}(i\omega u\bar{u})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\chi^{\prime}_{{\rm Killing},n}(2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}{(1-2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m){\rm Im}((u^{\prime}-i\omega u)\bar{u})

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\chi^{\prime}_{{\rm Killing},n}(2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}{(1-2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m)\omega|u|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\frac{\chi^{\prime}_{{\rm Killing},n}(2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}{(1-2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)}(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m){\rm Im}((u^{\prime}-i\omega u)\bar{u})

where for the last inequality we have used the fact that

\alpha_{n}(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m)\omega/a=\alpha_{n}am^{2}\frac{\omega}{am}(\frac{\omega}{am}-\frac{1}{2Mr_{+}})\geq 0
in the non-superradiant
regime to drop the second term.

Let us now define
\chi_{{\rm Killing},n}
such that

\chi_{{\rm Killing},n}=1\text{\ for\ }r\leq 1.1R_{\rm pot},\qquad 0\leq-\chi^{\prime}_{{\rm Killing},n}\leq\epsilon r^{-1}\text{\ for\ }r\geq 1.1R_{\rm pot},\qquad\chi_{{\rm Killing},n}=0\text{\ for\ }r\geq.9R_{\rm freq}.

(A.105)

Note that given arbitrary
\epsilon>0
, we can ensure (
A.105
)
provided we take
R_{\rm freq}
sufficiently large.

It follows from the above and inequality (
A.93
)
of Proposition
A.4.1
and
(
A.100
)
(and the fact that
by (
A.11
), we have
V^{\prime}<0

for
r\geq R_{\rm pot}
),
that given
E>0
, we have
in the region
1.2R_{\rm pot}\leq r\leq.9R_{\rm freq}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}^{y_{n}}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{f_{\rm fixed}}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{y_{\rm fixed}}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{\hat{z}}[u]+E\,\mathfrak{P}^{T+\chi_{{\rm Killing},n}\Omega_{1}}[u]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle cr^{-2}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\omega^{2}|u|^{2}+\Lambda|u|^{2})+c|u^{\prime}-i\omega u|^{2}

(A.106)

\displaystyle\qquad-E\chi^{\prime}_{{\rm Killing},n}\alpha_{n}m{\rm Im}((u^{\prime}-i\omega u)\bar{u})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle cr^{-2}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\omega^{2}|u|^{2}+\Lambda|u|^{2})+c|u^{\prime}-i\omega u|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-r^{-2}E\epsilon m^{2}|u|^{2}-E\epsilon|u^{\prime}-i\omega u|^{2}.

Recalling that
m^{2}\leq\Lambda
, we see that given arbitrary
E>0
, we may chose

\epsilon
small enough (thus requiring
R_{\rm freq}
large enough!) so that we may
absorb the terms in line (
A.106
) into the previous terms.
It follows then from the support properties of the elements of the current
and from inequality (
A.94
) of
Proposition
A.4.1
that (
A.96
)
holds
for
r\geq 1.2R_{\rm pot}
.

Thus it suffices to focus on the region
r_{+}\leq r\leq 1.2R_{\rm pot}
.

With all our current functions defined, let us note that
the zeroth order terms arising in the bulk, including those additional terms
arising from replacing
V
,
V^{\prime}
with
V_{0}
,
V_{0}^{\prime}
,
may be estimated by the expression

C\Delta r^{-6}\tilde{\chi}_{n}|u|^{2}

(A.107)

appearing on the right hand side of (
A.96
)
where
\tilde{\chi}_{n}
is the auxiliary degeneration function defined
in Section
3.4.3
(we may in fact multiply the above by
\iota_{r\leq 1.2R_{\rm pot}}
).
Thus, in what follows we may indeed ignore zeroth order terms in the bulk identities
and, moreover, replace
V
by
V_{0}
, etc.

We will first show that
(
A.96
) holds in this region
(with
e_{\rm red}=0
).

We consider separately the subranges of
r_{+}\leq r\leq 1.2R_{\rm pot}

for which

(i)

V_{0}-\omega^{2}\geq b_{\rm elliptic}\,\omega^{2}
,

(ii)

\omega^{2}-V_{0}\geq b_{\rm pot}\,\omega^{2}
,

(iii)

-b_{\rm pot}\,\omega^{2}\leq V_{0}-\omega^{2}\leq b_{\rm elliptic}\,\omega^{2}
,
|V_{0}^{\prime}|\leq\Delta b^{\prime}_{\rm pot}\,\omega^{2}
,

(iv)

-b_{\rm pot}\,\omega^{2}\leq V_{0}-\omega^{2}\leq b_{\rm elliptic}\,\omega^{2}
,
V_{0}^{\prime}>\Delta b^{\prime}_{\rm pot}\,\omega^{2}
,

(v)

-b_{\rm pot}\,\omega^{2}\leq V_{0}-\omega^{2}\leq b_{\rm elliptic}\,\omega^{2}
,
V_{0}^{\prime}<-\Delta b^{\prime}_{\rm pot}\,\omega^{2}
,

where
b_{\rm pot}>0
,
b^{\prime}_{\rm pot}>0
are the parameters from Proposition
A.1.8
.

Let us note that for
0<b_{\rm elliptic}\leq b_{\rm pot}
,
it follows from (
A.49
)
of Proposition
A.1.8

that the range (iii) is completely
contained in the vanishing set of
\chi_{n}
. Thus inequality (
A.96
)
trivially holds in this range. We henceforth require
b_{\rm elliptic}
to be sufficiently small so that (iii) is indeed contained
in the vanishing set of
\chi_{n}
.
We will further restrict the smallness of
b_{\rm elliptic}
further down.

Concerning the range (i), let us recall that restricted to
r\in[r_{+},1.2R_{\rm pot}]
,
the support of
\iota_{\rm elliptic}

is precisely the set
V_{0}-\omega^{2}\geq b_{\rm elliptic}\omega^{2}
, since by our
choice (
A.82
) of
\gamma_{\rm elliptic}
and the fact that
n\neq 1
, we have
(recall the definition (
3.30
)) that
r_{\rm elliptic}(\omega,m)=r_{+}
.
Moreover, we have (
A.42
) for non-superradiant
n
.
Note that for
r\leq r_{\rm pot}
, we have
-y_{n}V_{0}^{\prime}\gtrsim\Delta\Lambda
in the range (i).
Since moreover we have

V_{0}-\omega^{2}\gtrsim V_{0}+\omega^{2}
in this range,
it follows that

\mathfrak{P}^{y_{n}}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{f_{n}}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{f_{\rm fixed}}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{y_{\rm fixed}}[u]\geq c\Delta\chi_{n}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\omega^{2}|u|^{2}+\Lambda|u|^{2})-C\Delta\iota_{\rm elliptic}(V_{0}+\omega^{2})|u|^{2}-C\Delta\tilde{\chi}_{n}|u|^{2}

whence
inequality (
A.96
) indeed holds
(with
e_{\rm red}=0
).

In the range (ii), we note that
by our requirement (
A.98
), we have if

1.1R_{\rm pot}\leq r\leq 1.2R_{\rm pot}
then

-f_{\rm fixed}V_{0}^{\prime}|u|^{2}+y_{\rm fixed}(\omega^{2}-V_{0})|u|^{2})-y_{\rm fixed}V_{0}^{\prime}|u|^{2}+(y_{n}^{\prime}(\omega^{2}-V_{0})-y_{n}V_{0}^{\prime})|u|^{2}\gtrsim(\omega^{2}+\Lambda)|u|^{2}

while if

r\leq 1.1R_{\rm pot}
then

(y_{n}^{\prime}(\omega^{2}-V_{0})-y_{n}V_{0}^{\prime})|u|^{2}\geq(\Delta r^{-2}b_{\rm pot}\omega^{2}(\chi_{n}+B_{\rm large}|y_{n}|)-y_{n}V_{0}^{\prime})|u|^{2}.

Now let us note that from (
A.9
), we have the one sided
bound

-V_{0}^{\prime}\lesssim\Delta\omega^{2}

in
r_{+}\leq r\leq r_{\rm pot}
.
Now let us note that we have
\omega^{2}\gtrsim\Lambda\Delta
in the range (ii) for

r_{+}\leq r\leq 1.2R_{\rm pot}
. (This follows
because we have
\omega^{2}\gtrsim a^{2}m^{2}
in view of the restriction to nonsuperradiant frequencies,
and thus
\omega^{2}\gtrsim|V_{\gamma}|
and hence
\omega^{2}\gtrsim V_{0}-V_{\gamma}
.) On the other hand,
in
r\leq 1.2R_{\rm pot}
we have

|V^{\prime}_{0}|\lesssim\Delta\Lambda+\Delta\omega^{2}.

It follows from the above and the sign of
y_{n}
near
r_{+}
that if
r\leq 1.1R_{\rm pot}
,
we may fix
B_{\rm large}
in (
A.98
) (depending on the choice of
b_{\rm pot}
already
fixed!) so that

(y_{n}^{\prime}(\omega^{2}-V_{0})-y_{n}V_{0}^{\prime})\gtrsim(\chi_{n}+|y_{n}|)r^{-2}(\Delta\omega^{2}+\Delta\Lambda).

This again yields (
A.96
) for this range
(with
e_{\rm red}=0
). Note that by our comments after the definition of
f_{n}
, fixing

B_{\rm large}
determines the sufficiently largeness restriction on
B_{\rm indep}
.

On the other hand, it follows from (
A.47
)
and (
A.48
) of Proposition
A.1.8
and
the definition of
y_{n}

that range (iv) is contained in the set
\{f_{n}\leq 0,y_{n}\leq 0\}
whereas the range
(v) is contained in the set
\{f_{n}\geq 0,y_{n}\geq 0\}
. Note then that, the
u^{\prime}
term aside,
the signs of terms in these two ranges are only competing
if
0<V_{0}-\omega^{2}\leq b_{\rm elliptic}
, in which case, by restricting
b_{\rm elliptic}
to be
even smaller (depending on
B_{\rm large}
which has already been fixed and depending on
B_{\rm indep}
), we may indeed
ensure that

-y_{n}V_{0}^{\prime}-y_{n}^{\prime}(V_{0}-\omega^{2})-f_{n}V^{\prime}_{0}\gtrsim\chi_{n}\Delta r^{-2}(\omega^{2}+V_{0}).

Here we are using that
0\leq y_{n}^{\prime}\lesssim\chi_{n}
with an implicit constant which depends
on
B_{\rm indep}
and the already chosen
B_{\rm large}
, which
follows from (
A.101
),
while
y_{n}+f_{n}\gtrsim\chi_{n}
, which follows by combining (
A.98
)
and (
A.102
).
This again implies that (
A.96
) holds in this range
(with
e_{\rm red}=0
).

We have thus shown (
A.96
) in the entire region

r_{+}\leq r\leq 1.1R_{\rm pot}
with
e_{\rm red}=0
.

By our comments at the beginning of the proof, the full statement (
A.96
) for sufficiently small
e_{\rm red}>0

as well as the additional statement regarding
r_{\rm close}(e_{\rm red})
follow.
∎

A.4.3
The generalised superradiant ranges

We recall again the
n
-independent functions

f
,
y_{\rm fixed}
,
\hat{z}
,
z
defined already in Section
A.4.1
.

The crux of the proof is again a fixed frequency statement. We distinguish the
cases
n\neq 0
and
n=0
.

We first give a proposition for the case
n\neq 0
.

Proposition A.4.5

(Generalised superradiant bulk coercivity for
n\neq 0
)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{n}
be a generalised superradiant range for
n\neq 0
.
Let
B_{\rm indep}>0
be sufficiently
large. Let the paramater
b_{\rm elliptic}>0
from (
3.31
) be sufficiently small (with smallness
depending on
B_{\rm indep}
).
Then there exist functions

y_{n}(r)
,
f_{n}(r)
,
\chi_{n,{\rm Killing}}(r)

with
y_{n}(r)=B_{\rm indep}
for
r\geq 1.1R_{\rm pot}
and
\chi_{n,{\rm Killing}}(r)=0
,
f_{n}(r)=0
for
r\geq R_{\rm pot}
such that, given

E>0
arbitrary, the following holds for all sufficiently small
e_{\rm red}\geq 0
:
For all admissible frequencies
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
such that
(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
, we have in the region

r_{+}\leq r\leq R_{\rm freq}
the coercivity bound

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}^{y_{n}}[u]+

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}^{f}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{y_{\rm fixed}}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{\hat{z}}[u]+e_{\rm red}\mathfrak{P}^{z}_{\rm red}[u]-E\,\mathfrak{P}^{T+\chi_{n,{\rm Killing}}\alpha_{n}\Omega_{1}}[u]

\displaystyle\geq c\Delta r^{-4}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+r^{-1}\omega^{2}|u|^{2}+r^{-1}\Lambda|u|^{2})+ce_{\rm red}\chi_{\rm red}\Delta^{-1}|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m)u|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-C\Delta r^{-6}|u|^{2}-C\Delta\iota_{\rm elliptic}(V_{0}+\omega^{2})|u|^{2}.

(A.108)

Moreover, for
\epsilon_{\rm red}>0
sufficiently small, there exists
an
r_{\rm close}(e_{\rm red})>r_{+}
such that
in the region
r_{+}\leq r\leq r_{\rm close}
, inequality (
A.108
)
in fact holds without the final term on the right hand side.

Remark A.4.6
.

As with Proposition
A.4.3
,
we again reiterate that, according to our conventions, the constants
c
,
C
on the right hand side
of (
A.108
) will depend on the yet to be chosen
parameters
B_{\rm indep}
,
E
and
b_{\rm elliptic}
(though not
e_{\rm red}
).

Proof.

As in the proof of Proposition
A.4.3
,
it suffices to show (
A.108
) for
e_{\rm red}=0
.
The more general statement and the claim about
r_{\rm close}
will then follow immediately
as before.

Recall the intervals
[r_{n,1},r_{n,2}]
from Proposition
A.1.8
,
where
r_{n,2}\leq R_{\rm pot}
.
Requiring
0<b_{\rm elliptic}\leq b_{\rm pot}
, we have that
for all admissible
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
with
(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
,

[r_{n,1},r_{n,2}]\subset{\bf R}_{\rm elliptic}(\omega,m,\Lambda),

(A.109)

i.e.
\iota_{\rm elliptic}(r)=1
on
[r_{n,1},r_{n,2}]
.
Here we use the fact that by our choice of
\gamma_{\rm elliptic}
,
if
n\neq 1
then
r_{\rm elliptic}(\omega,m)=r_{+}
, while if
n=1
,

r_{+}(\omega,m)<4M<r_{n,1}<r_{n,2}<6M<1.2R_{\rm pot}
.

We define
\chi_{{\rm Killing},n}
so that

\chi_{{\rm Killing},n}=1\text{\ for all }r\leq r_{n,1},\qquad\chi_{{\rm Killing},n}=0\text{\ for all }r\geq r_{n,2},

(A.110)

and define

y_{n}(r)
to be smooth in
r\in[r_{+},\infty)
and to satisfy

y_{n}\left(\frac{r_{n,1}+r_{n,2}}{2}\right)=0,\quad 2\Delta r^{-2}B_{\rm large}(1+|y_{n}|)\geq y_{n}^{\prime}\geq\Delta r^{-2}B_{\rm large}(1+|y_{n}|)\text{\ for all\ }r\in[r_{+},1.1R_{\rm pot}],

(A.111)

and also (
A.99
), (
A.100
), (
A.101
).
Again,
B_{\rm large}\gg 1
will be a large parameter to be determined in what
follows, and this will also determine the largeness requirement on
B_{\rm indep}
. We may
again take
y_{n}

independent of
B_{\rm indep}
in the region
[r_{+},1.1R_{\rm pot}]
.

In the case
n\neq 1
, we define
f_{n}(r)=0
identically.
In the case
n=1
,
we define
f_{n}
such that

f_{n}^{\prime}\geq 0
for all
r>r_{+}
,
f_{n}=0
for
r\geq 2.1M
, and

f_{n}^{\prime}\geq\Delta,\qquad f_{n}\leq-\tilde{B}_{\rm large}\text{\ for\ all\ }r\in[r_{+},2.06M]

(A.112)

where
\tilde{B}_{\rm large}\gg 1
is again a large parameter (depending on
B_{\rm indep}
) which must be determined.

In view of the support of
\chi_{{\rm Killing},n}
and Proposition
A.4.1
,
it follows as in the proof of Proposition
A.4.3
that (
A.108
) indeed holds for
r\geq 1.2R_{\rm pot}
.

We will now show that (
A.108
)
holds for
r_{+}\leq r\leq 1.2R_{\rm pot}
(with
e_{\rm red}=0
).
Recall as before that the
y^{\prime}|u^{\prime}|^{2}
term in the bulk already yields control
of the
|u^{\prime}|^{2}
term, and in view of the presence of (
A.107
) on
the right hand side of (
A.108
), without
now the
\tilde{\chi}_{n}
factor, we may replace
V
,
V^{\prime}
by
V_{0}
,
V_{0}^{\prime}
in all calculations modulo
error terms controlled by this.

Let us consider first the case
n\neq 1
.
As in the proof of Proposition
A.4.3
,
we
consider separately the subranges of
r_{+}\leq r\leq 1.2R_{\rm pot}

for which

(i)

V_{0}-\omega^{2}\geq b_{\rm elliptic}\,\omega^{2}
,

(ii)

\omega^{2}-V_{0}\geq b_{\rm pot}\,\omega^{2}
,

(iii)

-b_{\rm pot}\,\omega^{2}\leq V_{0}-\omega^{2}\leq b_{\rm elliptic}\,\omega^{2}
,
|V_{0}^{\prime}|\leq\Delta b^{\prime}_{\rm pot}\,\omega^{2}
,

(iv)

-b_{\rm pot}\,\omega^{2}\leq V_{0}-\omega^{2}\leq b_{\rm elliptic}\,\omega^{2}
,
V_{0}^{\prime}>\Delta b^{\prime}_{\rm pot}\,\omega^{2}
,

(v)

-b_{\rm pot}\,\omega^{2}\leq V_{0}-\omega^{2}\leq b_{\rm elliptic}\,\omega^{2}
,
V_{0}^{\prime}<-\Delta b^{\prime}_{\rm pot}\,\omega^{2}
,

where
b_{\rm pot}>0
,
b^{\prime}_{\rm pot}>0
are the parameters from Proposition
A.1.8
.

If require
0<b_{\rm elliptic}\leq b_{\rm pot}
, then
the range (iii) is empty by (
A.40
)
of
Proposition
A.1.8
(superradiant
frequencies are not trapped). We henceforth restrict to
b_{\rm elliptic}>0
sufficiently small so that
the range (iii) is indeed empty.

Note that the support of
\chi^{\prime}_{{\rm Killing},n}
is contained entirely in the range (i)
in view of (
A.109
). We may bound the part of the bulk term arising
from the multiplier
T+\chi_{n,{\rm Killing}}\alpha_{n}\Omega_{1}
as follows:

|E\chi^{\prime}_{{\rm Killing},n}\alpha_{n}m{\rm Im}((u^{\prime}-i\omega u)\bar{u})|\lesssim\epsilon|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\epsilon^{-1}m^{2}+\epsilon\omega^{2})|u|^{2}\lesssim\epsilon|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\epsilon^{-1}(V_{0}-\omega^{2})|u|^{2}

(A.113)

where we use the fact that
n\neq 1
to bound
m^{2}\lesssim\omega^{2}
(note the constant
in this
\lesssim
is worse the finer our partition, but this is of no relevance here).
For small enough
\epsilon
we may absorb the first term on the right hand side of (
A.113
) in the control provided
by
y^{\prime}|u^{\prime}|^{2}
, while the second term on the right hand side of (
A.113
) is controlled by the
\Delta\iota_{\rm elliptic}(V_{0}-\omega^{2})|u|^{2}
term on the right hand side of (
A.108
).
Thus, the bulk term of the
T+\chi_{n,{\rm Killing}}\alpha_{n}\Omega_{1}
multiplier is now completely accounted for.
Moreover, we have (
A.42
) since
n\neq 1
, and
again, for
r\leq r_{\rm pot}
, we have
-y_{n}V_{0}^{\prime}\gtrsim\Delta\Lambda
in the range (i).
Thus in the range (i), inequality (
A.108
) indeed holds
(with
e_{\rm red}=0
).

In the range (ii), we note that for
B_{\rm large}
sufficently large (depending on the choice of
b_{\rm pot}
which we recall has been fixed), inequality (
A.108
) again holds
provided
r\geq r_{\rm pot}
(with
e_{\rm red}=0
). On the other hand,
by (
A.41
),
if
r\leq r_{\rm pot}
, then

V_{0}^{\prime}\gtrsim\Delta(\Lambda+\omega^{2})
while
y_{n}\leq-b
. Thus (
A.108
) again holds
(with
e_{\rm red}=0
).

Similarly, for
b_{\rm elliptic}>0
suitably small,
it follows now from (
A.37
)
and (
A.38
)
of Proposition
A.1.8
that
the range (iv) is contained in the set
y_{n}\leq-b
whereas the range
(v) is contained in the set
y_{n}\geq b
. Note then that the signs of terms in these two ranges is only competing
if
0<V_{0}-\omega^{2}\leq b_{\rm elliptic}
, in which case, by restricting
b_{\rm elliptic}
to be
even smaller (depending on
B_{\rm large}
which is already fixed and
B_{\rm indep}
), we may indeed
ensure that
-y_{n}V_{0}^{\prime}-y_{n}^{\prime}(V_{0}-\omega^{2})\gtrsim\Delta r^{-2}(\Lambda+\omega^{2})
,
where we have used (
A.111
) and (
A.101
).
Thus again, inequality (
A.108
) holds
in these ranges (with
e_{\rm red}=0
).

In view of our comments at the beginning about the case
e_{\rm red}>0
and
r_{\rm close}
,
this completes the proof in the case
n\neq 1
.

For the case
n=1
, the above arguments establish (
A.108
) in the region
r\geq 1.2R_{\rm pot}

since the definition of
y_{n}
is the same as above, and in this region we have
f_{n}=0
.
Recall that for
n=1
,
4M<r_{n,1}<r_{n,2}<6M
and thus
the bulk term of the
T+\chi_{n,{\rm Killing}}\alpha_{n}\Omega_{1}
multiplier
in particular vanishes in
r\leq 2.06M
. Let us fix
B_{\rm large}
in (
A.111
) to be
1
.
From
statement (
A.43
) of Proposition
A.1.8

and (
A.112
),
it follows that
-f_{n}V_{0}^{\prime}\geq b\tilde{B}_{\rm large}\Delta r^{-2}(\Lambda+\omega^{2})
in
r\leq 2.06M
,
where
\tilde{B}_{\rm large}
is the parameter in (
A.112
).
Thus, for
\tilde{B}_{\rm large}
sufficiently large (depending on
B_{\rm indep}
), it follows,
again using (
A.111
) and (
A.101
),
that

-f_{n}V_{0}^{\prime}-y_{n}V_{0}^{\prime}-y_{n}^{\prime}(V_{0}-\omega^{2})\geq\frac{1}{2}b\tilde{B}_{\rm large}\Delta r^{-2}(\Lambda+\omega^{2}),

whence (
A.108
) holds in
r\leq 2.06M

(with
e_{\rm red}=0

and without the
\iota_{\rm elliptic}
terms).

Considering now the region
2.06M\leq r\leq 1.2R_{\rm pot}
, we note that by (
A.45
) this
lies entirely in the region (i), and thus we may argue exactly as with the
n\neq 1
case for region (i),
where
we now use (
A.44
) to control
m^{2}|u|^{2}
and
\Lambda|u|^{2}
terms. In
particular, note that
to argue (
A.113
), we use
that for
n=1
, by (
A.44
), we have that
\omega^{2}+m^{2}\lesssim V_{0}
on the set
2.06M\leq r\leq 1.2R_{\rm pot}
.
We obtain (
A.108
) (with

e_{\rm red}=0
, but now with the
\iota_{\rm elliptic}
term).

(Note that we can indeed set
B_{\rm large}=1
in (
A.111
) because the
largeness was not necessary in region (i).)

It follows that (
A.108
) holds
in
r_{+}\leq r\leq 1.2R_{\rm pot}

with
e_{\rm red}=0
.

The full statement in the
e_{\rm red}>0
case again follows in view of the remarks at the beginning
of the proof.
∎

For the remaining low frequency range
n=0
, we have the following:

Proposition A.4.7

(Bulk coercivity for low frequency range
n=0
)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{0}
denote the low frequency range.
Let
B_{\rm indep}>0
be sufficiently
large.
There exist

y_{0}(r)
,
f_{0}(r)
,
\chi_{0,{\rm Killing}}(r)

with
y_{0}(r)=B_{\rm indep}
for
r\geq 1.1R_{\rm pot}
such that
the following hold, for all

E>0
arbitrary and for
e_{\rm red}\geq 0
sufficiently small.
For all admissible frequencies
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
such that
(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{F}_{0}
,
we have in the region

r_{+}\leq r\leq R_{\rm freq}
the coercivity bound

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}^{y_{0}}[u]+

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}^{f_{0}}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{f}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{y_{\rm fixed}}[u]+\mathfrak{P}^{\hat{z}}[u]+e_{\rm red}\mathfrak{P}^{z}_{\rm red}[u]-E\,\mathfrak{P}^{T+\chi_{0,{\rm Killing}}\alpha_{0}\Omega_{1}}[u]

\displaystyle\geq c\Delta(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\omega^{2}|u|^{2}+\Lambda|u|^{2})+ce_{\rm red}\chi_{\rm red}\Delta^{-1}|u^{\prime}+i(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m)u|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-C\Delta r^{-5}|u|^{2}-C\Delta\iota_{\rm elliptic}(V_{0}+\omega^{2})|u|^{2}.

(A.114)

Moreover, for
\epsilon_{\rm red}>0
sufficiently small, there exists
a
r_{\rm close}(e_{\rm red})>r_{+}
such that
in the region
r_{+}\leq r\leq r_{\rm close}
, inequality (
A.114
)
in fact holds without the final term on the right hand side.

Proof.

Recall that for
n=0
we have
[r_{0,1},r_{0,2}]=[4M,5M]
.

We define
\chi_{{\rm Killing},0}
as in (
A.110
)
with
n=0
.
Note the bound

E\,\mathfrak{P}^{T+\chi_{0,{\rm Killing}}\Omega_{1}}[u]=E\alpha_{0}\chi^{\prime}_{\rm Killing}\,{\text{Q}}^{\Omega_{1}}[u]\geq-E\epsilon^{-1}C|u|^{2}-E\epsilon C|u^{\prime}|^{2},

(A.115)

since
m^{2}\leq B_{\rm low}
.
Now consider
y_{0}
defined again to satisfy (
A.111
) with
n=0

and also (
A.99
), (
A.100
) and (
A.101
)
and define
f_{0}
by (
A.112
).
In the region
r\geq 1.2R_{\rm pot}
, (
A.114
)
holds as before, whereas in the region
r_{+}\leq r\leq 1.2R_{\rm pot}
, we note that
we may choose
\epsilon
small enough so that we may absorb
the second term on the right hand side of (
A.115
)
in the term
y^{\prime}|u^{\prime}|^{2}
.
The proof then proceeds as in the
n=1
case,
noting that in the region
r_{+}\leq r\leq 2.06
, we have

-V_{0}^{\prime}\geq c\Delta\Lambda-C\Delta
, while
in the region
2.06\leq r\leq 1.2R_{\rm pot}
,
we have
\Delta\Lambda\lesssim V_{0}+1
.
∎

A.4.4
Fixing all remaining parameters

Let us fix
B_{\rm indep}
so as to satisfy all the largeness assumptions of the propositions of this section.
We may also now fix
the parameter
b_{\rm elliptic}
of (
3.31
) which was
constrained (independently of
E
,
e_{\rm red}
)
by the proof of Propositions
A.4.3
and
A.4.5
).
Now we may
fix
b_{\rm trap}
to satisfy the constraints of Proposition
A.1.8

(related to (
A.46
) as well as (
A.51
))
and (
A.81
). (We recall that

\gamma_{\rm elliptic}
was
fixed by (
A.82
).)

Given arbitrary
E>0
, let us
fix
R_{\rm freq}(E)

as a function of
E

from Proposition
A.4.3
(cf. Remark
A.4.4
),

With the above choices, we have that
all parameters appearing in the definition of the functions
of (
A.84
)
and (
A.85
)
are completely determined from the stipulation of an arbitrary
E>0
and a sufficiently
small
e_{\rm red}>0
, with the dependence as indicated above.

A.4.5
Finishing the proof of Theorem
3.3

In view of the identifications (
A.71
)–(
A.76
) from Section
A.2
and the Plancherel relation (
A.77
)
applied with

u:=\mathfrak{C}\circ\mathfrak{F}[P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}\psi],

the proof of Theorem
3.3
follows
from Propositions
A.4.3
,
A.4.5
and
A.4.7
, according to whether
n\geq N_{s}+1
,

1\leq n\leq N_{s}
, or
n=0
, respectively, upon summing and
integrating over Carter frequencies
(\omega,m,\ell)
,
as long as we may bound the contribution of the two negative-signed terms
on the right hand side of (
A.96
), (
A.108
) and (
A.114
), respectively.

Concerning the contribution of the zeroth order
negative signed terms,
In view of the support of
\tilde{\chi}_{n}
,
note that from (
A.46
), (
A.51
) we have

\tilde{\chi}_{n}|u|^{2}\lesssim\chi_{\natural}|u|^{2}

for all
n=0,\ldots,N
.
By definition of
\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf k}
, then
there either exists a
{\bf\tilde{k}}
with
|{\bf\tilde{k}}|=k-1

such that

u=i\omega\,\mathfrak{C}\circ\mathfrak{F}[P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf\tilde{k}}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}\psi]

or there exists such a
{\bf\tilde{k}}
such that

u=im\,\mathfrak{C}\circ\mathfrak{F}[P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf\tilde{k}}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}\psi].

Thus, denoting
\tilde{u}=\mathfrak{C}\circ\mathfrak{F}[P_{n}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\bf\tilde{k}}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}\psi]
,
we have

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{R^{*}_{\rm freq}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{m,\ell\geq|m|}\tilde{\chi}_{n}\Delta r^{-5}|u|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{R^{*}_{\rm freq}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{m,\ell\geq|m|}\chi_{\natural}\Delta r^{-5}|u|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{R^{*}_{\rm freq}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{m,\ell\geq|m|}\chi_{\natural}\Delta r^{-5}(m^{2}+\omega^{2})|\tilde{u}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle{}^{\natural}\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi].

Concerning the contribution of the first-order negative signed terms, we notice that
if
(\omega,m)
are such that
m=0
or
\gamma=\omega/ma\in I_{n}
for
n\geq 2
, then
for all admissible
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
,

\iota_{\rm elliptic}(r,\omega,m,\Lambda)(m^{2}+\omega^{2})(V_{0}+\omega^{2})\lesssim\iota_{\rm elliptic}(r,\omega,m,\Lambda)(V_{0}-\omega^{2})^{2}

(A.116)

(with the
\sim
depending on a bound
|\omega|\gtrsim m
provided by
\gamma
in the
m\neq 0
case).
In the case where
\omega/ma\in I_{n}
for
n=1
, we similarly have (
A.116
),
where we use that

\iota_{\rm elliptic}(r,\omega,m,\Lambda)
is supported in
r\geq 2.01M
since

2.01M<r_{+}(\omega,m)
,
and thus
and we use (
A.44
) to bound
m^{2}\lesssim V_{0}
in this region.
Finally, if
n=0
, then (
A.116
) clearly holds for all admissible
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
with
(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{F}_{0}
.

If
\tilde{u}
,
|{\bf\tilde{k}}|=k-1
are as above,
then we have

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{R^{*}_{\rm freq}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{m,\ell\geq|m|}\iota_{\rm elliptic}\Delta(V_{0}-\omega^{2})|u|^{2}dr^{*}d\omega

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{R^{*}_{\rm freq}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{m,\ell\geq|m|}\iota_{\rm elliptic}\Delta(V_{0}-\omega^{2})(m^{2}+\omega^{2})|\tilde{u}|^{2}dr^{*}d\omega

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{R^{*}_{\rm freq}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{m,\ell\geq|m|}\iota_{\rm elliptic}\Delta(V_{0}-\omega^{2})^{2}|\tilde{u}|^{2}dr^{*}d\omega

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle{}^{\rm elp}_{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny{\boxed{+1}}}}}\,\underaccent{\scalebox{.8}{\mbox{\tiny$k\mkern-6.0mu-\mkern-6.0mu1$}}}{\mathcal{X}}(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})[\psi].

This completes the proof.

A.5
The global boundary coercivity at
\mathcal{S}
: Proof of Proposition
3.4.8

We first consider the boundary term of
J^{{\rm main},n}
at
\mathcal{H}^{+}
.
One can of course analyse this directly using (
3.50
)
and (
3.51
), but given our formalism, it is convenient
to infer it from a horizon computation at the fixed Carter frequency level:

Proposition A.5.1

(Non-superradiant horizon coercivity)
.

Let
e_{\rm red}>0,E>0
be arbitrary. Let

y_{n},\alpha_{n}
correspond to a non-superradiant range
\mathcal{F}_{n}
. Then
for all admissible frequencies
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
, not necessarily satisfying

(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
,
we have

\displaystyle\left({\text{Q}}^{y_{n}}[u]+e_{\rm red}{\text{Q}}^{z}_{\rm red}[u]-E_{n}\,{\text{Q}}^{T}[u]-E_{n}\alpha_{n}\,{\text{Q}}^{\Omega_{1}}[u]\right)(r_{+})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle EB_{1}(\omega-\alpha_{n}m)(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m)|u|^{2}

(A.117)

\displaystyle\quad+B_{2}\omega m|u|^{2}+B_{3}\omega^{2}|u|^{2}+B_{4}m^{2}|u|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+e_{\rm red}(B_{5}\Lambda+B_{6})|u|^{2}

where
E_{n}:=E(1-2Mr_{+}\alpha_{n}/a)^{-1}
and
where
B_{1},B_{5},B_{6}>0
. In particular,
for admissible
(\omega,m,\Lambda)
with
(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
,
then

\left({\text{Q}}^{y_{n}}[u]+e_{\rm red}{\text{Q}}^{z}_{\rm red}[u]-E_{n}{\text{Q}}^{T}[u]-E_{n}\alpha_{n}\,{\text{Q}}^{\Omega_{1}}[u]\right)(r_{+})\gtrsim E\left(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m\right)^{2}|u|^{2}+e_{\rm red}\Lambda|u|^{2}

(A.118)

if
E
is chosen sufficiently large depending on
e_{\rm red}
.

Proof.

The identity (
A.117
) follows from the definition of the currents
together with the boundary behaviour of
u
as
r^{*}\to-\infty
.
On the other hand, recall (
3.43
).
Note that for all nonsuperradiant ranges we have that either
m=0
or

\omega/am
is
bounded away from
0
and
\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}
.
Thus we
have a uniform lower bound

(\omega-\alpha_{n}m)(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m)\gtrsim(\omega-\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}m)^{2}+\omega^{2}+m^{2}

on the distance of the non-superradiant intervals to the threshold.
We may now absorb the terms multiplying
B_{2}
,
B_{3}
and
B_{4}
for large enough
E
.
This yields (
A.118
).
∎

Now note that we may re-express the boundary contribution of currents
on
r=\hat{r}

with respect to the volume form
r^{2}dt^{*}d\phi\sin\theta d\theta

and a rescaled normal
\hat{\rm n}_{\rm r=\hat{r}}
which corresponds to our chosen
normal at the horizon. The coefficients of the quadratic expression

J^{{\rm main},n}[\psi]\cdot\hat{\rm n}_{\rm r=\hat{r}}

are then smooth as spacetime functions.
Let us note that when expressed in the coordinate
basis
\partial_{r}
,
\partial_{t^{*}}
,
\sin^{-1}\theta\partial_{\phi^{*}}
, and
\partial_{\theta}

of Kerr star coordinates,
it follows that

\displaystyle J^{{\rm main},n}[\psi]\cdot\hat{\rm n}_{r=r_{0}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle E_{n}{\rm Re}\left((\partial_{t^{*}}\psi+\alpha_{n}\partial_{\phi^{*}}\psi)\overline{(\partial_{t^{*}}\psi+\frac{a}{2Mr_{+}}\partial_{\phi^{*}}\psi)}\right)+B_{2}{\rm Re}\left(\partial_{t^{*}}\psi\overline{\partial_{\phi^{*}}\psi}\right)+B_{3}|\partial_{t^{*}}\psi|^{2}+B_{4}|\partial_{t^{*}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-C(r_{+}-r_{0})\left(|\partial_{t^{*}}\psi|^{2}+\sin^{-2}\theta|\partial_{\phi*}\psi|^{2}+|\partial_{\theta}\psi|^{2}\right)-C(r_{+}-r_{0})^{2}|\partial_{r}\psi|^{2}-C|\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+ce_{\rm red}\left(|\partial_{t^{*}}\psi|^{2}+\sin^{-2}\theta|\partial_{\phi*}\psi|^{2}+|\partial_{\theta}\psi|^{2}\right)+ce_{\rm red}(r_{+}-r_{0})|\partial_{r}\psi|^{2}.

Now from Plancherel and the nonsuperradiant condition, we obtain that for
E
sufficiently
large

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{S}}J^{\rm main}[\psi_{n,{\bf k}}]\cdot\hat{\rm n}_{r=r_{0}}r^{2}dt^{*}d\phi\sin\theta d\theta

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{S}}\Big(cE\left(|\partial_{t^{*}}\psi_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}+|\partial_{\phi^{*}}\psi_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+ce_{\rm red}\left(|\partial_{t^{*}}\psi_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}+\sin^{-2}\theta|\partial_{\phi*}\psi_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}+|\partial_{\theta}\psi_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+ce_{\rm red}(r_{+}-r_{0})|\partial_{r}\psi_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}-C|\psi_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}\Big)\,r^{2}dt^{*}d\phi\sin\theta\,d\theta.

This yields (
3.54
) for
r_{0}<r_{+}
sufficiently close to
r_{0}
,
upon rewriting it so as to
replace
\hat{\rm n}_{r=r_{0}}
with
{\rm n}_{r=r_{0}}

and the volume form with the induced
volume form. The second term on the right hand side of (
3.54
) arises from bounding the integral
of
|\psi_{n,{\bf k}}|^{2}
by the flux term on
\mathcal{S}
.
